# CHANGELOG — design-revision history (pre-implementation, culminating in v1.9)

*No version below was ever implemented — these are design-review passes. Labels v4/v5/v6 are historical pass names; the current consolidated design is **revision v1.9**. Decision numbers (D#) are the stable identifiers used across the build.*

Every finding from the external design review, traced to the decision that resolves it and the doc sections where it lives. Decisions D35–D48 are new in v6; D1–D34 carry forward.

| # | Review finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|----------------|-----------|-------|
| 1 | "Identify a winning strategy" is statistically out of reach on human timescales (MDE math ⇒ ~20y for 2%/yr alpha at good pairing); success criteria overpromised | **D38** — success reframed as falsification-first; lab-level KPIs defined; `TooEarly` expected common verdict; ensemble tilts as the honest low-power action | MASTER §0.1, §1.1–1.2, §17; DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §6 |
| 2 | Single random twin = one noise path; S3's two-sample test inherits the twin's luck | **D36** — populations of M=200 seeded controls per cadence family + M=50 cost-free; S3 = percentile rank; bands on every chart | MASTER §0.3; CATALOG §5.2; MONITOR S3 |
| 3 | Monitor thresholds were guesses forward operation would take years to falsify; machinery itself never validated | **D37** — Arena Replay: full-pipeline historical mode, quarantined, for machinery validation (randoms ≤ chance over 15y; falsification-speed KPI) and threshold calibration; report archived; calibrated values frozen | MASTER §0.4; DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §5; MONITOR §0.3; BUILD Phase 4 |
| 4 | Index membership had no source (Alpaca can't provide it) | **D35** — EODHD constituents (current + historical since 2000) primary; daily iShares IVV holdings CSV cross-check; Wikipedia fallback; fail-closed on divergence | MASTER §13.1, §13.4 |
| 5 | Sector classification had no source (LowVol cap + Risk screen depended on it) | **D35** — EODHD sector/industry fields; change-logged; static-snapshot fallback with staleness alarm | MASTER §13.1; CATALOG §6.3 |
| 6 | Factor-attribution regression had no factor-return data source | **D41** — Ken French Data Library daily factors; monthly validated refresh; diagnostic-only framing makes the publication lag acceptable and stated | MASTER §10; DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §1.4 |
| 7 | Risk-free rate unsourced (Jensen's alpha undefined without it) | **D41** — French RF series; FRED fallback | DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §1.1 |
| 8 | Covariance for the heat guardrail hand-waved ("supplied matrix"); sample covariance singular/noisy | **D42** — Ledoit–Wolf shrinkage, 252d, EWMA single-index fallback; estimator logged per run | MASTER §12; DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §3.1 |
| 9 | Slippage model shape stated but parameters unspecified — the most consequential number in the system unfalsifiable | **D43** — commission + half-spread buckets + k·σ·√(Q/ADV), k=0.1, participation cap 2% ADV with rejection logging; cost-model version stamped per trade | MASTER §12; DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §3.3 |
| 10 | Ticker changes/reuse, spin-offs, and mixed mergers would corrupt a symbol-keyed ledger within a year | **D39** — security master (`security_id` + ticker_history); full corporate-action semantics incl. spin-off basis allocation; unmapped events fail closed (freeze + alert) | MASTER §13.6; CATALOG §11 |
| 11 | Bar revisions vs "append-only" unresolved; determinism claim undermined | **D40** — versioned append-only bars; per-run watermark; determinism redefined as f(inputs, watermark, seeds); CI greps extended to UPDATE | MASTER §13.5, §14.1 |
| 12 | Single-day idempotency doesn't compose into correct multi-day recovery | **D47** — ordered catch-up protocol, one ACID transaction/day, as-of membership, resumable, logged | MASTER §13.7; BUILD Phase 2 |
| 13 | MDE assumed i.i.d. daily differences — the honesty metric itself overclaimed | **D48** — Newey–West long-run variance in the MDE; AR(1) regression test in CI; same logic in the trade track's blocks | MONITOR Appendix C; DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §1.2 |
| 14 | Daily-return lens wastes the sample rate of high-trade-count families | **D44** — trade-level expectancy track (block bootstrap + own MDE); falsification accelerator; feeds S8; never a standalone promotion basis | DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §1.3; CATALOG §6.2, §9.1 |
| 15 | Regime-conditional conclusions from n≈1 episodes presented as findings | **D45** — episode counter on every regime metric; anecdote badge below 3 episodes | MASTER §15; DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §1.5 |
| 16 | LLM cost fine but unoptimized; true token sink (news text) unspecified; sentiment score's value presumed | **D46** — Batches API (½ price), prompt caching, per-task tiering; news budget enforced pre-token in INewsProvider; score demoted to experimental, priced by the paired A/B; research-assistant primary | MASTER §7; DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §4 |
| 17 | Fundamentals gate (D33) correct but no candidate source named; EDGAR-scale effort understated | D33 retained + **operationalized**: concrete 5-step PIT validation protocol; **EODHD Fundamentals named first candidate** (same subscription); EDGAR/paid feed as alternatives; pass ⇒ logged D49 | CATALOG §7.0; BUILD Phase 8 |
| 18 | EODHD was the originally intended provider; v5 had drifted to Alpaca-only, leaving the gaps above | **D35** — EODHD primary across bars/actions/membership/sectors/news; Alpaca retained as free cross-check/fallback behind the same interfaces; one validated ~$20–30/mo dependency, consciously accepted | MASTER §4, §13.1 |
| 19 | Project risk is scope, not statistics (Phases 0–3.5 = months of solo build) | **§17.1 MVP discipline** — phase gates as protection; PROGRESS.md as month-one deliverable; no Phase-6 work while Phase-3 fairness tests are red | MASTER §17.1; BUILD §2 |

**Mockups:** `paper_trading_ui_mockups.html` and `decision_and_strategies_mockups.html` carry forward from v5 unchanged — the layouts remain valid. Three additions to reflect when the UI is built (already specified in MASTER §15): population band shading behind equity/alpha charts (D36), regime episode counters + anecdote badges (D45), and a replay badge/quarantine styling (D37).

## v1.8 (UI-swappability pass — make the front end replaceable)

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 45 | Blazor Server called C# services in-process over SignalR — no boundary another UI framework (Angular/React/mobile) could consume; "the API" was the Blazor circuit | **D57** — new `AlphaLab.Api` ASP.NET Core minimal-API: JSON read endpoint per screen + command endpoints + SSE + OpenAPI; Blazor re-scoped as a client of it; CI grep forbids `AlphaLab.Evaluation`/`AlphaLab.Data` usings inside `AlphaLab.Web` | MASTER §21; tech stack; arch diagram; FR-32; CLAUDE rule 17 |
| 46 | Honesty-carrying presentation logic (MDE dimming, verdict tiers, percentile chips, allocation clamps, replay quarantine) lived in Blazor view-models — every new UI would re-implement it and could silently violate it | **D58** — serializable honesty read-models computed once in `AlphaLab.Core`/`AlphaLab.Evaluation`; UX rules resolved into DTO fields; UI renders verbatim; UX tests become framework-agnostic read-model tests; a forward read-model can't contain a replay row | MASTER §22; §15 reframed; FR-33; UX_GUIDELINES header; CLAUDE rule 18 |

## Revision v1.8 — API coherence pass (D59–D60)

*Reviewing the D57/D58 UI-boundary specs against the rest of the system surfaced gaps that a builder would otherwise improvise. Closed here.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 47 | After D57 emptied `AlphaLab.Web` of domain logic, the daily job (previously "in-process inside AlphaLab.Web") had **no home**; and the API was told both "no side effects" and "launch a replay," a contradiction | **D59** — dedicated `AlphaLab.Worker` Generic Host owns Quartz + the D53 pipeline + D47 catch-up and is the **sole DB writer**; the API enqueues bounded/long-running intents rather than running them | MASTER decisions + §21 + tech stack + arch diagram; CLAUDE rule 19 + layout; FR-34; RUNBOOK; PROGRESS |
| 48 | "Return the read-model" left failure, long-running, versioning, consistency, and numeric-exactness paths unspecified — exactly where a second client (Angular/mobile) diverges | **D60** — fixed contract: `/api/v1`, uniform error envelope + status codes, 202+job_id for replay/LLM, read-models stamped run_id+watermark, money as strings/minor-units (never floats), UTC ISO-8601 | MASTER §21–22 + tech stack; CLAUDE rule 20; FR-32 extended; TEST_PLAN |
| 49 | Long-running commands (replay, LLM briefs) would have blocked a request thread for minutes–hours if run synchronously in the API | **D60(c)** — async job pattern: 202+`job_id`, progress via `GET /stream/jobs/{job_id}`, poll fallback; executed by `AlphaLab.Worker` | MASTER §21; FR-32; `jobs`/`worker_state` tables |
| 50 | SQLite single-writer guarantee was implicitly at risk once the API could also write (candidate/journal/admin commands) concurrently with a daily run | **D59** — Worker publishes `worker_state.run_in_progress`; API commands during a run are queued or 409, never raced; bounded API writes take the lock briefly between runs | SCHEMA `worker_state`; FR-34; CLAUDE rule 19 |

## Revision v1.8 — on-demand run mode (D61)

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 51 | The Worker assumed an always-on machine with a resident Quartz scheduler; the target machine is often off, making a timer the wrong primary trigger | **D61** — `Worker.Mode=OnDemand` is the default: launch → catch-up through the last completed session → exit. `Scheduled` (Quartz) is an optional config flip for an always-on host. Both do identical work via catch-up (D47); only the trigger differs. Guard: never half-run a day whose close hasn't happened | MASTER decisions + tech stack + §21 + diagram; CONFIG `Worker`; CLAUDE rule 19; FR-34; RUNBOOK; SETUP; START_HERE; new TEST_PLAN fixtures |

## Revision v1.9 — verdict-economics + calibration-realism pass (D63–D65)

*An internal review of v1.8 tested the design's claims against its own mechanics. Three findings, all merged into the doc bodies (nothing lives only in this log).*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 53 | §1.1's "falsification is fast — no-edge strategies retired in months" contradicted the control construction: D36's populations are **turnover-matched and cost-inclusive**, so a merely edgeless strategy pays the same cost drag as its controls and sits at the **median** of its band — it never breaches `P_noise(t)` beyond the false-alarm rate, never goes Suspect, never auto-retires. The steady state would have been silent, indefinite `TooEarly` — and a KPI ("falsification speed") the lab cannot deliver. `FX-S3Trajectory` even asserted the impossible behavior (no-edge ⇒ Suspect) | **D63** — verdict economics corrected: `IndistinguishableFromRandom` is a first-class rendered outcome (`separation_state` in the D58 read-models; chip with day count once track ≥ `Verdicts.SeparationMinTrackDays`); the KPI splits into **anti-predictive detection speed** and **indistinguishability honesty**; fast-kill language reserved for the trade-level track (tests vs zero, D44) and anti-predictive S3/S6 breaches; Golden Rule 31; UX-12; FR-35; fixtures corrected | MASTER §1.1–1.2, §8, §15, §16, §17.1, §20.8, §22, D63; MONITOR §0.8, S3, S6, Appendix A; DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §5–6; CATALOG preamble; UX-12; BUILD FR-35 + Phase 3; TEST_PLAN `FX-S3Trajectory`/`FX-SeparationChip`; CONFIG `Verdicts.*`; CLAUDE rule 21; PROGRESS |
| 54 | Every D56 calibration curve — and therefore S3's statuses, S6's auto-retire, and the replay KPIs — derived from "planted" strategies **no document specified**. A naive constant-drift plant separates smoothly and early; a realistic lumpy factor edge spends years in-band — calibrating `P_edge(t)` on the naive plant would flag every honest strategy Warning for years, recreating the exact failure D56 fixed, invisibly, inside the calibration itself. Single-seed plants repeat the one-noise-path fallacy D36 removed from the controls; survivorship-caveated replay data silently shapes the curves | **D64** — plants fully specified: three kinds (edge / no-edge / **anti-predictive**), regime-conditional autocorrelated overlays on population members (active-session persistence ≈ holding horizon; drift scaled to the annualized target; regime multipliers renormalized; constant drift prohibited except as the naive comparator); ≥50 seeds per plant, curves = medians with archived 25–75% bands; **mandatory plant-sensitivity check** (naive vs realistic) as a permanent calibration-report section; data-vintage stamp on the curves; FR-36 | MASTER §20.9, D64, D56 row; MONITOR §0.8, S3, Appendix A `calibration.plant`; DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS §5; BUILD FR-36 + Phase 4 prompt/DoD; TEST_PLAN `FX-PlantRealism` + updated `FX-Replay15y`/`FX-S3Trajectory`; CONFIG `Calibration.Plant.*`; PROGRESS Phase 4 |
| 55 | The dominant project risk is abandonment during the months-long Phases 0–4 stretch (§17.1 named it but prescribed no route); building screens before replay validates the machinery raises that risk and may be invalidated by what calibration reveals | **D65** — the thin vertical slice is the sanctioned build order: S&P 100, **API-only (Scalar) operation until Phase 4 sign-off**, straight to replay; D58 read-models + API endpoints + tests built on schedule in Phase 3 (honesty never deferred); Blazor screens become a deferrable parallel workstream, hard deadline Phase 7 exit; Phase 0's empty client still ships | MASTER §17.1, D65; BUILD §2 (vertical-slice note, Phase 3 row, FR-24); UX_GUIDELINES phase wiring; START_HERE step 4; PROGRESS Phase 3 |

## Revision v1.9 — build-readiness pass (D66–D67)

*Resolving Phase 0 open questions before the first line of code. Recorded here; the binding text lives in the docs.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 56 | D60 requires every read-model "always stamped" with run_id+watermark, but pre-first-run there is nothing to stamp. A nullable flat stamp would push the guarantee into a doc note consumers can ignore — the convention-over-type failure D58 exists to prevent | **D66** — `ReadModelStamp` is a discriminated union: always-present object with `status: "no_run_yet"\|"stamped"`; run_id/watermark/as_of non-null iff `"stamped"`. Discriminant is run-context presence only (a zero-trade strategy is still `"stamped"`); replay quarantine stays orthogonal (`quarantined:true`, not a third status). Phase-3 invariant test: `"no_run_yet"` before first run, `"stamped"` and stable after | MASTER §22 + D66; Phase 0 read-model DTOs; TEST_PLAN read-model assertions |
| 57 | Phase 0 needs two config keys the design implied but never wrote down (DB path, API bind), and the OpenAPI stack default predated .NET 10 dropping Swashbuckle from templates | **Config additions** — `ConnectionStrings:AlphaLab` (absolute-anchored so Worker/Api/design-time factory share one file), `Api.Bind` (localhost), `Api.OpenApi` (`native` = Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi + Scalar, `/swagger` route preserved; Swashbuckle fallback) | CONFIG_REFERENCE (Api + ConnectionStrings blocks); Phase 0 build prompt |
| 58 | The design carried secrets in env vars / a .NET User Secrets store with the other as fallback — operational ceremony for multi-user/CI/deploy scenarios this local-only single-machine lab does not have | **D67** — secrets live in ONE gitignored `appsettings.Secrets.json` layered over `appsettings.json`; config builder adds those two files only (no `AddEnvironmentVariables`, no `AddUserSecrets`); keys never in the committed repo; CI grep on committed files stays the leak guard. Supersedes finding 33 | CONFIG_REFERENCE Secrets §; SETUP §5; CLAUDE rule 11; MASTER D49 row + tech-stack + providers line + golden rule 11 + NFR-4; BUILD NFR-4 + Phase 0 prompt; INTEGRATIONS auth; RUNBOOK rotation; START_HERE |

## Revision v1.9.1 — final consistency pass (D68–D69 + errata)

*A full cross-document audit run immediately before Phase 0. Every finding below is fixed in place across the doc set; file names retain the v1.9 label. Nothing in this pass changes the design's semantics except finding 65 (a pre-calibration anchor that contradicted D63) and D68/D69 (two decisions the docs had left open or inconsistent).*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 59 | `ConnectionStrings:AlphaLab` used `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, which Microsoft.Data.Sqlite does not expand — Phase 0 would create a literal `%LOCALAPPDATA%` folder or fail; and expanding it would itself read an environment variable, which D67's spirit forbids | Token changed to `{LocalAppData}`; shared `DbPathResolver` in AlphaLab.Data resolves it via `Environment.GetFolderPath(SpecialFolder.LocalApplicationData)` (known-folders API — zero env-var reads) + directory create; used by Worker, Api, and the EF design-time factory; wired in the Phase 0 prompt | CONFIG ConnectionStrings note; BUILD Phase 0; PROGRESS Phase 0 |
| 60 | `jobs` and `worker_state` — which Phase 0's InitialCreate must create — existed only as prose in SCHEMA's enforcement notes (no types, no PK), in a file that forbids inventing columns | Full `CREATE TABLE` DDL added (typed columns, CHECKs, single-row `worker_state` with `id=1` guard) | SCHEMA infra section + enforcement note |
| 61 | Phase 0's Worker was told to "compute missed sessions from the calendar," but `trading_calendar` is a Phase 1 table absent from the infra-only InitialCreate | Phase 0 behavior pinned: no calendar table + zero committed runs ⇒ nothing-to-do ⇒ log + exit 0 | BUILD Phase 0 prompt |
| 62 | The WASM client is cross-origin to the API even on localhost, but no CORS policy was specified anywhere — the Phase 0 DoD ("client renders via the API") would fail in the browser | New `Api:CorsAllowedOrigins` key (dev-only); Phase 0 prompt configures the policy | CONFIG Api block; BUILD Phase 0; PROGRESS Phase 0 |
| 63 | Blazor hosting model ambiguous: D5 said Server, the tech stack said "WebAssembly or Server," while the Phase 0 prompt, D67's no-secrets-in-Web rule, and the CORS note all assume standalone WASM | Pinned: standalone Blazor WebAssembly everywhere; D5 marked hosting-superseded | MASTER D5 + tech stack; CLAUDE.md layout |
| 64 | **D38 was missing from the MASTER §2 decision table** despite being cited by §0, §1.2, Golden Rule 3, and this changelog | D38 row added (success criteria reframed; lab-level KPIs; D63 re-split referenced) | MASTER §2 |
| 65 | The pre-calibration S3 flat anchor set Suspect below the **80th** percentile — but per D63 an edgeless strategy sits at its band's ~50th percentile, so every honest dummy in Phase 3 would go Suspect immediately, recreating inside the anchors the exact failure D56/D63 fixed | Suspect anchor corrected to **< 25th** (the anti-predictive tail; v5's original 0.25 restored); Healthy ≥ 95th unchanged; all "95/80" references updated | MONITOR §0.7, S3, Appendix A; MASTER D56 row + §20.7; BUILD Phase 3 prompt; CONFIG key rules |
| 66 | SCHEMA declared "all money REAL" while D60 promised float-free ledger exactness across the JSON boundary — exactness that starts as an IEEE double has nothing to preserve | **D69** — ledger money is C# `decimal` persisted as TEXT (EF Core's SQLite default); REAL reserved for market-data prices and statistics; every ledger money column re-typed | MASTER D69; SCHEMA header + columns + note; CONFIG key rule; CLAUDE.md rule 20; BUILD Phase 2 prompt |
| 67 | `securities UNIQUE(current_symbol, exchange)` breaks under ticker reuse — the exact scenario D39 exists to handle (a delisted row holding symbol X blocks the new listing that takes X) | Partial unique index: symbol uniqueness enforced only `WHERE delisted_on IS NULL` | SCHEMA securities |
| 68 | The equal-weight benchmark convention was both "pinned" (MASTER v1.8 banner) and "decide at Phase 2 with a D-number" (CATALOG §5.1) | **D68** — pinned now: self-built EW of the eligible universe, monthly rebalance; no ETF proxy | MASTER D68; CATALOG §5.1; BUILD Phase 2 prompt |
| 69 | RUNBOOK backed up `lab.db` (CONFIG says `alphalab.db`), and the 02:00 nightly backup assumed a resident process that OnDemand mode (the default) does not have | Filenames unified to `alphalab.db`; backup runs as the final step of each OnDemand launch, 02:00 job in Scheduled mode only | RUNBOOK §1/§3/§4; CONFIG Ops |

## Revision v1.9.3 — multi-arena capability (D71)

*Additive capability pass: AlphaLab can host multiple isolated single-universe labs ("arenas") — an
S&P 500 arena now, a Russell 2000 or other arena in the future — without ever combining their
competitions. No schema change; the S&P 500 build is unaffected. Full spec in the new companion
`ARENA_ARCHITECTURE_v1.9.3.md`.*

| # | Change | Where |
|---|--------|-------|
| 87 | **New decision D71 — multi-arena isolation via the multi-instance model.** An arena = one single-universe lab isolated at the storage layer (own SQLite file, Worker+Api, snapshot/backup dirs, all derived from `Arena.Id`). Calibration is arena-scoped and never shared. The frontend selects one active arena and never merges leaderboards across arenas. A cross-arena meta-allocator is out of scope. **No SCHEMA change.** | MASTER §2 (D71 row); new `ARENA_ARCHITECTURE_v1.9.3.md` |
| 88 | **New companion document** `ARENA_ARCHITECTURE_v1.9.3.md` — arena vs. universe vs. security mental model; backend (Arena config, arena-namespaced `DbPathResolver`, calibration-scoping table); frontend (arena registry, arena switcher, provenance line, the no-merge rule); operations; and a step-by-step "add an arena" checklist | new file |
| 89 | **`Arena` config block added** (`Arena.Id`, `Arena.DisplayName`); the `ConnectionStrings:AlphaLab` default is now arena-namespaced (`…\AlphaLab\{Arena.Id}\alphalab.db`). `DbPathResolver` injects `Arena.Id` into the path — a file-location change, not a schema change | CONFIG_REFERENCE (Arena block + connection string) |
| 90 | **New UX rule UX-13 — arenas never merge (D71c):** one active arena per view with a calibration-provenance line; cross-arena views are side-by-side panels only, never a single mixed ranking. Same category-error logic that forbids ranking a daily strategy against a monthly one | UX_GUIDELINES (UX-13) |
| 91 | **Operations + onboarding wired for arenas:** RUNBOOK gains a "running more than one arena" section (`-Arena` param on snapshot/migrate; calibration never copied between arenas); SETUP day-zero sets `Arena.Id = "sp500"`; README and START_HERE point to the new companion doc | RUNBOOK; SETUP §7; README §1; START_HERE |

## Revision v1.9.5 — post-Phase-0 consistency errata (deployment relocation + drift repair)

*Recorded after Phase 0 shipped (the first pass performed against a live repo rather than a pre-code
package). Trigger: the deployed database was relocated from the `{LocalAppData}\AlphaLab` token base
to the literal absolute base `E:\AlphaLabDatabase` per the new `docs/DB_RELOCATION.md` runbook —
CONFIG_REFERENCE and the code moved, but several docs still asserted the old base as current fact.
A full cross-document audit then swept for other drift. Everything below is fixed in place; file
names retain their existing labels. Historical entries above (incl. findings 17, 92, 94) are
records of their passes and are deliberately NOT rewritten.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 100 | **The DB relocation was only half-propagated.** `ConnectionStrings:AlphaLab` moved to the literal base `E:\AlphaLabDatabase\{Arena.Id}\alphalab.db` (documented in CONFIG_REFERENCE and the new `DB_RELOCATION.md`), but ARENA §3.1/§3.2 still stated `{LocalAppData}\AlphaLab\{Arena.Id}\…` as *the* database path, RUNBOOK §3/§7 still showed `…\AlphaLab\{Arena.Id}\…` backup paths, BUILD's FR-37 + Phase 0 prompt still said "the resolved path is `…\AlphaLab\sp500\alphalab.db`", and TEST_PLAN's `FR37_ArenaNamespacedDbPath` description asserted the `…\AlphaLab\` base — the exact stale-fact class findings 59/92 fixed twice before | All normative path statements rewritten **base-agnostically** as `<DbBase>\{Arena.Id}\…` where `<DbBase>` comes from `ConnectionStrings:AlphaLab` (this deployment: `E:\AlphaLabDatabase`; portable alternative: the `{LocalAppData}\AlphaLab` token form), each pointing at `DB_RELOCATION.md`; ARENA §3.2's evolution block gains the as-deployed line; the FR37 test description now states the assertion owns the arena segment + token resolution, never the base — so a future relocation cannot redden it or restale these docs | ARENA §3.1 + §3.2; RUNBOOK §3 + §7; BUILD FR-37 + Phase 0 prompt; TEST_PLAN §8 |
| 101 | **Two shipped ops docs were invisible to every documentation map** (the finding-95 drift class): `DB_RELOCATION.md` and `FUTURE_DB_MIGRATION.md` exist in `docs/` but appeared in neither README §1, nor MANIFEST, nor the CLAUDE.md map that every build session loads — a session needing to move or migrate the DB would not find its runbook | Both docs added to README §1 (Tier 2), MANIFEST ("Build & test"), and the CLAUDE.md documentation map, each with a one-line role and the relocation-vs-engine-migration distinction | README §1; MANIFEST; CLAUDE.md map |
| 102 | **D49 decision-number collision.** Finding 17 (v6 pass) reserved "D49" as the number a Phase-8 fundamentals-source pass would log; finding 32 later assigned D49 to the **budget-tier launch configuration** (MASTER §2 — the live meaning used by FR-4, SETUP, INTEGRATIONS, CONFIG). BUILD's Phase-8 table + prompt still said "if pass ⇒ log D49" and CATALOG §7.0 said "a D49 at that time" — executing that instruction would duplicate or overwrite an existing decision | BUILD Phase-8 table + prompt and CATALOG §7.0 now instruct: append a **new** decision under the **next free D-number** at that time (D49 is permanently the budget-tier configuration and is never reused). Finding 17's text stands as history | BUILD §2 Phase-8 row + Phase-8 prompt; CATALOG §7.0 |
| 103 | **`Api.Bind` was a dead key.** Finding 94 moved the bind (host + port) to the committed `Urls` key; the as-built Phase 0 code binds via `Urls` and never reads `Api.Bind` — yet CONFIG still listed it as a live key and MASTER §21 still described it as the future LAN-exposure flag, a dead key inside a "never invent a key" system | `Api.Bind` retired: removed from CONFIG's appsettings block (with a retirement note); MASTER §21's posture reworded — localhost-only is kept by the `Urls` host, and changing that host is the (future) LAN-exposure switch | CONFIG Api block; MASTER §21 |
| 104 | **RUNBOOK described a migration guard that does not exist**: "CI reminds; the migration script refuses without a same-day snapshot" — no spec defines a refusal-and-freshness check, and the built `tools/migrate.ps1` implements the (stronger, simpler) contract of unconditionally taking the snapshot itself before applying | RUNBOOK §3 reworded to the actual contract: `tools/migrate.ps1` is the only sanctioned schema-application path and performs the snapshot as its first step — the guard holds by construction, and `dotnet ef database update` is never run directly against a non-empty DB | RUNBOOK §3 |
| 105 | **ARENA §5's per-arena config option contradicted D67.** "`appsettings.sp500.json`, `appsettings.russell2000.json`" implies a third layered config file, but the D67 builder loads exactly `appsettings.json` + `appsettings.Secrets.json` and nothing else | Per-arena config **directories** pinned as the sanctioned form (each instance runs from its own directory with its own two files; the layered-file option retired) | ARENA §5 |
| 106 | Cosmetics & drift: README's version banner stopped at v1.9.3 though v1.9.4 was merged; MANIFEST's changelog bullet said "v1.9.1 through v1.9.3"; SETUP §6's `.gitignore` item still listed an in-repo `backups/` directory though backups live under the DB base outside the repo (RUNBOOK §3) | Banners in README / MANIFEST / START_HERE / MASTER now carry through v1.9.5; SETUP §6 corrected (backups are outside the repo; `*.db` catches stray copies only) | README banner; MANIFEST; START_HERE; MASTER version note; SETUP §6 |

## Revision v1.9.6 — Phase-0 fix-up back-ported into the build prompt (rebuild-safety pass)

*A code review of the as-built Phase 0 skeleton found six issues; they were fixed against the live
code via a one-off fix-up prompt. This pass **folds those same six fixes into the BUILD Phase 0
prompt, the Phase-0 DoD, the PROGRESS Phase-0 gate, and the TEST_PLAN fixture inventory** so that a
future from-scratch build lands on the fixed skeleton directly — the fix-up is no longer a separate
step that a rebuild would lose. No schema change (the hand-edited InitialCreate INTEGER-PRIMARY-KEY
migration is preserved, rule 14); no new decision; all D-number invariants (D57/D59/D60/D61/D66/D67/D71)
unchanged. Fixed in place; file names retain the v1.9 label.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 107 | **A from-scratch rebuild would reproduce all six Phase-0 review issues**, because the fixes lived only in an after-the-fact fix-up prompt, not in the BUILD Phase 0 prompt the rebuild follows | All six back-ported into the Phase 0 prompt + DoD + PROGRESS gate + TEST_PLAN (rows below). "Entirely-Claude-Code from scratch" now yields the fixed skeleton on the first pass | BUILD §2 Phase-0 row + Phase-0 prompt; PROGRESS Phase 0; TEST_PLAN §8 |
| 107a | **Tests touched the real `E:` drive** — `DbPathResolver.Resolve` creates the target directory, and the FR37 test resolved the compiled default connection string, so the suite created real `E:\` dirs and threw on any machine without an `E:` drive | Prompt now mandates splitting `DbPathResolver.ResolvePath` (pure token replacement, no filesystem) from `Resolve` (resolve + `EnsureDirectoryExists`); the FR37 test and a new `DbPathResolver_ResolvePath_IsPure_NoDirCreate` test resolve temp-rooted strings only — the suite passes with no `E:` drive | BUILD Phase-0 prompt (resolver); TEST_PLAN §8 |
| 107b | **The Api created the DB directory at boot** (including under `WebApplicationFactory` in the Api tests), reintroducing the `E:`-touch — a reader must never create the store (D59, Worker is sole writer) | `AddAlphaLabData(…, bool ensureDirectory = true)` added; the **Api passes `ensureDirectory:false`** (resolves the path, creates nothing); Worker + design-time factory keep the default `true`. Prompt wires the Api DbContext this way | BUILD Phase-0 prompt (Api reader wiring) |
| 107c | **Only OnDemand migrated** — a first-ever `--serve` (Scheduled) launch idled against a nonexistent schema | A shared `SchemaStartup : IHostedService` applies the migration in **both** modes, registered before Quartz and before the OnDemand runner (StartAsync runs in registration order); OnDemandRunner no longer migrates inline and stays a `BackgroundService`. New `SchemaStartup_AppliesInitialCreate` test | BUILD Phase-0 prompt (Worker); TEST_PLAN §8; PROGRESS Phase 0 |
| 107d | **launchSettings port drift** — the Api profile advertised `5114/7010` while the app binds `5230` via `Urls`, so `dotnet run`/F5 opened a dead port; the Web `https` origin was absent from CORS | Prompt pins a single Api `http` profile at `http://127.0.0.1:5230` + `launchUrl=scalar/v1` (no `https` profile); the Web `http` profile stays at `5210` (matching CORS, retaining `inspectUri`), `https` dropped | BUILD Phase-0 prompt (launchSettings) |
| 107e | **Dead config** — `appsettings.Development.json` never loads under the D67 two-file builder, and `Api:Bind` is never read (binding is carried by `Urls`, finding 94/103) | Prompt now says: create no `appsettings.Development.json`, add no `Api:Bind` key | BUILD Phase-0 prompt (config hygiene) |
| 107f | **CI grep gaps** — the D57 Web-isolation grep scanned only `.cs`/`.razor` usings (a `<ProjectReference>` evaded it) and the bars pattern lacked a word boundary (`UPDATE barstool` false-positived) | Prompt's `tools/ci.ps1` spec adds a `src/AlphaLab.Web/**/*.csproj` grep failing on a `ProjectReference` to `AlphaLab.(Evaluation|Data).csproj` (Core stays allowed) and changes the bars pattern to `\bbars\b` | BUILD Phase-0 prompt (CI greps) |

## Revision v1.9.7 — deep-dive review errata (lab-validity + rebuild-safety pass)

*A deep-dive review of the full design set and the as-built Phase-0 code (its findings R-1…R-18 are
folded in place below; the review prose itself is not retained in the repo) audited three
questions: internal consistency, UI-polish readiness, and whether the lab as designed can actually
do its job (identify honest edges without killing them, and kill dishonest ones fast). Everything
accepted is folded in place below — including back-porting the Phase-0 fixes into the BUILD Phase-0
prompt so a from-scratch rebuild lands on the fixed skeleton directly (the finding-107 discipline).
Two new decisions (**D72**, **D73**), one new FR (**FR-38**), one schema change (`config` composite
PK, snapshot-gated). File names retain the v1.9 label.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 108 | **(R-2) The `config` table's documented contract was unimplementable.** `key TEXT PRIMARY KEY` can physically hold one row per key, yet MONITOR §5 and the D56/D63 calibration pipeline require "versioned config rows, never silent edits" — the first calibration write would have forced an improvised schema change mid-Phase-4 | `config` PK → **composite `(key, version)`**; read rule = MAX(version) per key; changes INSERT `(key, version+1)`, never UPDATE/DELETE (append-only, like bars). Rides a snapshot-gated `ConfigVersionedRows` migration; Phase-0 prompt models `HasKey(x => new { x.Key, x.Version })` with a writer-supplied `Version`; `SchemaFidelityTests` asserts the on-disk composite PK + two-versions-insertable | SCHEMA (`config` DDL + enforcement notes); BUILD Phase-0 prompt + DoD; TEST_PLAN §8; PROGRESS Phase 0 |
| 109 | **(R-6) Nothing enforced one good forward run per day.** `runs` has no uniqueness on `as_of`; a blanket unique would be wrong (failed retries and replay legitimately repeat dates), so the real invariant was implicit | Invariant stated in SCHEMA with its enforcement: partial index `ux_runs_ok_forward ON runs(as_of) WHERE status='ok' AND run_kind IN ('live','catchup')`, created in Phase 2 (when Stage-2 first writes runs) — what makes catch-up idempotency and `catchup_log(as_of PK)` mutually consistent | SCHEMA (`runs` comment + notes); BUILD Phase-2 prompt + row |
| 110 | **(R-5, D73, FR-38) The regime proxy was the last silent data dependency** — `Regime.ProxySecurityId: null // set at Phase 1` named no source, validation, fallback, or warm-up, yet the label sits on the calibration critical path (the D64 edge plant modulates drift by regime) and needs ≈3.8y of history before its first computation | **D73**: EODHD `GSPC.INDX` EOD primary (⚠VERIFY on launch tier), `SPY.US` daily-returns cross-check, self-built cap-weight fallback (stable `security_id`); ≈3.8y warm-up before the first label, full replay window before Phase 4; the S&P 500 proxy pinned across the D70 slice. Wired as **FR-38** into Phase 1; label computation fails closed pre-warm-up (`FX-RegimeProxyBackfill`) | MASTER D73 row + §20.1; INTEGRATIONS §9 (new); BUILD FR-38 + Phase-1 prompt + row; CONFIG `Regime.ProxySource`; TEST_PLAN §2 |
| 111 | **(R-3, D72a) Queued jobs had no executor on the default deployment.** The API 202-queues replay/analysis jobs into `jobs`, but OnDemand (the default, D61) exits after catch-up and Scheduled only wakes at close+offset — a UI-launched replay sat `queued` forever, exactly where Phase 4 iterates replay launches | **D72(a)**: OnDemand launch order = **schema → catch-up → drain queued jobs → backup → exit** (`Worker.DrainQueuedJobsOnLaunch`, default true); jobs never run inside a daily write transaction; the 202 body states the execution model and `GET /jobs/{id}` shows queue position. Test `FX-JobDrain` | MASTER D72 row + §21; BUILD FR-34 + Phase-2 prompt + row; CONFIG `Worker.*`; RUNBOOK; TEST_PLAN §3 |
| 112 | **(R-4, D72b) A mid-run crash bricked the command path.** `run_in_progress=1` is necessarily written outside the daily transaction, so a crash left it set forever — every subsequent command 409'd with no documented recovery | **D72(b)**: the running Worker writes `worker_state.heartbeat_at` every `Worker.HeartbeatSeconds` (30); on launch, a flag with a heartbeat older than `Worker.StaleRunThresholdSeconds` (300) is cleared, the orphaned `runs` row marked `failed`, the recovery logged; the Api ignores stale flags for its 409 decision. Test `FX-CrashedRun`; RUNBOOK incident row | MASTER D72 row; SCHEMA `worker_state` comment; BUILD FR-34 + Phase-2 prompt; CONFIG; RUNBOOK incident table; TEST_PLAN §3 |
| 113 | **(R-8) No KPI protected the lab's honest winners.** Replay measured false-promotion and kill speed but never asked whether a *real* lumpy 2%/yr edge **survives** — S6's patience could be calibrated into auto-retiring the exact strategies the lab exists to find, and no gate would notice | **Edge-plant survival KPI**: fraction of D64 edge plants still promotable at 5y/10y; Phase-4 DoD floor `Replay.EdgePlantSurvivalFloor5y` (0.90); every edge-plant auto-retire logged with its triggering signal; a floor failure recalibrates **S6's patience, never the plant** | MASTER §1.2 KPI table + §20.9; MONITOR §0 item 8½ + auto-retire note; BUILD FR-19 + Phase-4 prompt + row; CONFIG; TEST_PLAN §5; PROGRESS Phase 4 |
| 114 | **(R-9) Per-signal calibration ignored the joint test.** Eight signals each calibrated to its own false-alarm rate still fire *some* alarm far more often jointly — "any Suspect" was the operative veto with no stated joint bound | The calibration report records the **joint any-signal false-alarm fraction** for no-edge plants over the replay window, with per-signal contribution, bounded by `Replay.JointFalseAlarmMaxFrac` (0.10) | MONITOR §0 item 8½; MASTER §20.9; BUILD FR-19 + Phase-4 prompt + row; CONFIG; TEST_PLAN §5; PROGRESS Phase 4 |
| 115 | **(R-7) The cost-match was asserted, never verified.** Controls match exit *shape*, not signal autocorrelation — a strategy churning less than its population pays less cost drag than its own null, biasing the S3 percentile **anti-conservatively** (the one direction the lab must never err) with no detector | **Turnover-match verification** (FR-12 amendment): realized annualized turnover persisted per strategy + population each evaluation; outside `Populations.TurnoverMatchTolerancePct` (±30%) of the population median ⇒ **cost-match caveat** in the `StrategyRow` read-model + S3 panel, logged; `matched_params_json` records a realized-turnover target for re-matching. Test `FX-TurnoverMatch` (Phase 3) | BUILD FR-12 + Phase-3 prompt + row; CONFIG `Populations.TurnoverMatchTolerancePct`; TEST_PLAN §4; PROGRESS Phase 3 |
| 116 | **(R-10) The allocator floor was infeasible past 20 strategies** — `WeightFloorPct=5%` floors sum past 100% and the clamp order never said what wins | Floors apply **pre-renormalization and scale down proportionally** when Σfloors > 100% (equivalently the promotable roster caps at ⌊100/`WeightFloorPct`⌋); renormalization never pushes a floored weight below its (scaled) floor | MASTER §20.2; CONFIG Allocator note |
| 117 | **(R-16) The operator's biggest verdict-speed lever was invisible.** Verdict time scales with 1/σ_LR² — pairing tightness dominates everything else the operator controls — yet no surface showed it | UX-2 evidence meter gains the **pairing-tightness chip** (current σ_LR + days-to-verdict at the current gap); pure surfacing — `power_reports` already stores every input; teaches *propose siblings, not strangers* | UX_GUIDELINES UX-2 addendum |
| 118 | **(R-1) WAL was claimed everywhere and enabled nowhere.** RUNBOOK, D22's rationale, and code comments all state WAL, but no `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` exists in code, migrations, or scripts — the deployed DB runs rollback-journal mode, where the Api's reads and the Worker's Stage-2 transaction block each other | `SchemaStartup` executes `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` after a successful migrate, verifies the result, and **fails startup if not `wal`** (same non-zero-exit contract as a failed migration); persistent per file, so the existing DB converts on its next Worker launch; the pragma never runs in the Api (a reader must not convert the store). Test `R1_SchemaStartup_EnablesWal` | BUILD Phase-0 prompt + DoD + row; TEST_PLAN §8; PROGRESS Phase 0 |
| 119 | **(R-11) `migrate.ps1` could snapshot one file and migrate another.** The snapshot step resolves the DB from the Worker's appsettings, but bare `dotnet ef database update` uses the design-time factory's **compiled** default connection string — an appsettings edit (e.g. the DB_RELOCATION move) desynchronizes them and the rule-14 guarantee silently protects the wrong file | `tools/migrate.ps1` resolves `ConnectionStrings:AlphaLab` from the Worker's appsettings (exactly as `snapshot-db.ps1` does) and passes it explicitly via `dotnet dotnet-ef database update --connection "$resolvedCs"` — snapshot and migration provably target the same file | BUILD Phase-0 prompt + DoD; PROGRESS Phase 0 |
| 120 | **(R-17) Curves calibrated on S&P 500 replay judge an S&P 100 forward slice** until the Phase-4 widen — a real, bounded vintage mismatch that was nowhere stated to the operator | The S3 panel + curve read-models carry the caveat string *"curves calibrated on S&P 500 as-of membership; forward universe is the S&P 100 slice until the Phase-4 widen"* — the same vintage honesty the D66 stamp applies to data | MASTER §20.7; UX_GUIDELINES UX-4 addendum |
| 121 | **Hygiene umbrella (R-12/R-13/R-14/R-18).** Code comments called the Api an unqualified "READER" (misleading once D59 grants bounded Phase-3 command writes); template cruft survived (index.html title `AlphaLab.Web`, a learn-more link in the layout, a stale `.gitignore` `%LOCALAPPDATA%` comment); UX-8c's teaching empty state was a bare "no data yet"; and the enum-CHECK extension cost was undocumented | Phase-0 prompt: comments say "reader **plus bounded Phase-3 command writes (D59)**"; title `AlphaLab`, header renders the arena `DisplayName`, no tutorial links; every `ScreenCatalog` entry carries an `EmptyHint` (what appears + which run produces it) and the home page states what runs day one — the empty state teaches. SCHEMA notes: enum CHECK columns extend only via migration + same-PR SCHEMA edit | BUILD Phase-0 prompt; SCHEMA notes; PROGRESS Phase 0 |

*v1.9.7 prompt-completeness follow-up (2026-07-12): two implementation refinements discovered while building the fix-up were back-ported into the BUILD Phase-0 prompt so a from-scratch rebuild inherits them — (a) the `config` **write contract** (version = `MAX(version)+1` at write time in one transaction, never caller-supplied; `Version` modeled `ValueGeneratedNever()`), added to the finding-108 guardrail + `N2_DuplicateExplicitVersion_Rejected` to the test note; and (b) the `migrate.ps1` **exit-code gate** (judge `dotnet-ef` by `$LASTEXITCODE`, not stderr, so the NU1903 warning doesn't trip PowerShell 5.1's `ErrorActionPreference='Stop'`), added to the finding-119 clause. No code, decision, or schema change — the running code already does both; this only closes the rebuild-path gap.*

## Revision v1.9.8 — Phase-0 skeleton review errata (2026-07-13)

*A second deep-dive review, this one of the shipped Phase-0 skeleton against the full v1.9.7 doc set (findings **P0-1…P0-6**; its prose is not retained — the findings are folded in place below and into the docs). The skeleton was found faithful on every load-bearing invariant (the D66 stamp, the five-table infra schema, the three-spots rule, the WAL-or-fail startup, the reader/writer split, the D60 envelope, the reconciled 39-test count); six items were fixed. One (P0-1) was a **DoD claim not actually met**. No architecture, schema, or decision change; D1–D73 and FR-1…FR-38 unchanged.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 122 | **(P0-1, High) The client did not render "every screen."** BUILD 0.6's DoD and the checked PROGRESS box claim the empty-DB client renders every §21 screen (NFR-3), but `ScreenCatalog` listed **8 of the 13** non-parameterized list screens — Trades, Go-live log, Regimes, Risk, and Admin interventions had endpoints + read-models server-side but no catalog entry, so five §15 screens were unreachable and untested against NFR-3 | The five descriptors added to `ScreenCatalog` with UX-8c `EmptyHint`s (the NavMenu picks them up automatically → Home + 13 screens); a class comment records that the two **parameterized** §21 screens (`/strategies/{id}`, `/why-trade/{strategyId}/{date}`) are deliberately excluded from the flat catalog (reached by drilling into a real row). PROGRESS box reworded to "every **non-parameterized** §21 screen" with the exclusion noted | `src/AlphaLab.Web/ScreenCatalog.cs`; BUILD 0.2 + 0.6; PROGRESS Phase 0 |
| 123 | **(P0-2, Medium) A stale, actively misleading factory comment.** `AlphaLabDbContextFactory`'s doc said the bare-`dotnet ef` fallback is the portable `%LOCALAPPDATA%` path; the constant is (correctly) the `E:` literal the three-spots rule mandates. The comment invited "correcting" the constant to match — reddening `ConfigConsistencyTests` or desyncing the three spots | Comment rewritten to the `E:`-literal reality (intentional, byte-identical to both appsettings, guarded by `ConfigConsistencyTests`; relocate via DB_RELOCATION.md, never by editing this constant alone) and noting `--connection` values reach the factory pre-resolved | `src/AlphaLab.Data/AlphaLabDbContextFactory.cs` |
| 124 | **(P0-3, Medium) CI enforced less than checkpoint 0.1 specified, plus a script-robustness gap.** BUILD 0.1 says the **whole** reference graph is CI-enforced at the `<ProjectReference>` level; `ci.ps1` guarded only Web→Core. Separately, `Get-CommittableFiles` ran `git ls-files … 2>$null` under `$ErrorActionPreference='Stop'`, so a git-absent machine threw before the working-tree fallback (and native stderr risked a terminating `NativeCommandError`) | `ci.ps1` gains `Assert-ReferenceGraph` — an allowlist per `src/*.csproj` checked at the `<ProjectReference>` level (an illegal edge fails CI); the Web source-level `using` grep is kept as belt-and-suspenders. The git call is wrapped in the EAP-relax + presence-check pattern (gate on exit code, not stderr). BUILD 0.6's `ci.ps1` spec re-aligned to 0.1's full-graph intent | `tools/ci.ps1`; BUILD 0.6 |
| 125 | **(P0-4, Low) Two resolver tests were weaker than their TEST_PLAN §8 entries.** `FR37_ArenaNamespacedDbPath` and the purity test resolved a single arena — neither asserted the §8 "two arenas → distinct, non-colliding paths" requirement | Both tests fold in a second arena and `Assert.NotEqual` on the resolved data-source paths (canonical **39** count unchanged — strengthened, not added). TEST_PLAN §8 erratum records that the "bare EF factory defaults to sp500" case is intentionally substituted by the pure `FR37_DefaultConnectionString_HasArenaTokenAndDataSource` (the bare factory resolves as a writer and would create `E:\AlphaLabDatabase\sp500\`, violating §8's "passes with no `E:` drive") | `tests/AlphaLab.Data.Tests/DbPathResolverTests.cs`; TEST_PLAN §8 |
| 126 | **(P0-5, Low) Dangling references to a non-existent review file.** Seven-plus docs referenced `docs/reviews/DEEP_DIVE_REVIEW_2026-07-12.md`, absent from the repo — a doc set whose manifest lists a missing file fails its own "self-contained" claim | Policy made explicit: **review prose is not retained as files** — findings are folded into the authoritative docs and traced in this CHANGELOG. Every reference (START_HERE ×2, MASTER §version-note, BUILD, README ×2, MANIFEST ×2, and this file's self-reference) redirected to `CHANGELOG_v1.9.md`; the `reviews/` file-table row and manifest bullet removed | START_HERE (root + docs); MASTER §version note; BUILD §revision note; README (revision note + file table); MANIFEST (Build&test list + revision state); this file |
| 127 | **(P0-6, Low) A silent registry fallback contradicted fail-closed.** If `wwwroot/appsettings.json` loses its `Arenas` section, `ArenaRegistry.FromEntries` silently fell back to the WASM origin, so the client issued API calls **against itself** and rendered an opaque JSON-parse transport error — hard rule 10 ("nothing silently defaulted") applies to the client's one config too | `ArenaRegistry` gains an `IsFallback` flag (true when no registry was configured); `MainLayout` renders a visible config-error banner naming the exact cause and fix instead of leaking transport errors; `FromEntries_Empty` test asserts the flag | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Arenas/ArenaRegistry.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Web/Layout/MainLayout.razor`; `tests/AlphaLab.Core.Tests/ArenaRegistryTests.cs`; BUILD 0.2 + 0.6; PROGRESS Phase 0 |

*Doc-only records from the same review (no code change; recorded so a from-scratch rebuild reaches this exact state): (a) SCHEMA's finding-108 note clarifies the composite `config` PK ships **inside `InitialCreate`** for a from-scratch build, while an already-deployed store would receive it via the snapshot-gated `ConfigVersionedRows` migration — both true in their contexts (A.3-1). (b) The root `README.md` Status section gains a verdict-time expectation paragraph — the lab's fast outputs are anti-predictive kills and `IndistinguishableFromRandom` findings; promotions are slow by construction (C-3). (c) The BUILD Phase-1 checklist gains a line to snapshot the iShares IVV CSV **header shape** as a fixture and fail the provider on format drift, treating count-sanity as a hard gate on **both** sources independently before comparison (C-4). (d) **§B UI recommendations captured (not implemented — D65 defers real UI):** `UX_GUIDELINES_v1.9` gains a "Visual system — design tokens (v5 direction)" section consolidating the v5 palette table (`--bg`/`--panel`/`--ink`/`--line`/`--gold`/`--cyan`+`--band`/`--violet`/`--up`+`--down`/`--amber` + Archivo/Inter/IBM Plex Mono) and the **reserve-from-day-one** rule for the three semantic tokens (`--gold`=live, `--violet`=replay quarantine, `--band`=population); the shell-theming pass is recorded as the deferred BUILD checkpoint **0.7g** (chrome only — dark surfaces, mono numerics, self-hosted fonts, restyled empty-state cards, `NotFound.razor` in-voice, Bootstrap-dist trim), explicitly **not** the D65 data-screen workstream, and logged as a UI-workstream line in PROGRESS. (e) **C-2** (residual risk, no design change): the Phase-4 calibration report **archives the S3 percentile-threshold sampling band** (~±1.5 members at M=200) — MONITOR item 8½ + BUILD Phase-4 DoD + PROGRESS Phase-4 gate. (f) **C-5**: the EODHD `GSPC.INDX` EOD ⚠VERIFY item already rides the Phase-1 checklist (FR-38) — noted, no new action.*

*Decision proposals opened by the review (awaiting resolution, logged in PROGRESS — not yet actioned): (1) **FR-23's "hypotheses" research action has no home** — §21's command list has only `/analysis/brief` and `/analysis/skeptic`, and `jobs.kind` allows only `('replay','analysis_brief','analysis_skeptic')`; before Phase 5, either add a migration extending the CHECK + a §21 entry, or strike the word from FR-23 (A.3-2 / C-6). (2) **Detection-power sweep** — sweep the Phase-4 edge plant across ~three alpha levels (e.g. 2/4/8% ann) and archive empirical P(promoted by t | α) curves + median days-to-promotion, validating the analytic MDE end-to-end against the machinery; awaiting a D-number (C-1).*

## Revision v1.9.9 — Phase-1 completion doc-reconciliation (2026-07-15)

*Phase 1 shipped (checkpoints 1.0–1.10): the S&P 100 slice is backfilled through a live run and the data foundation is code-complete. This pass folds the phase's two **decided-but-unnumbered** decisions and the first-live-backfill findings back into the design docs so a from-scratch rebuild reproduces the corrected state. Two new decisions (**D74–D75**); findings **128–137**. The referenced Phase-1 code already shipped (this is a docs pass, no new code); FR-1…FR-38 unchanged. Two review proposals stay open in PROGRESS by design and take no D-number here — FR-23's "hypotheses" home (C-6) and the Phase-4 detection-power sweep (C-1) — because they are undecided, not merely unnumbered.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 128 | **Index-membership drop was conflated with delisting.** An early phase-plan draft implied a name dropping out of the index should derive `corporate_actions(type='delist')` — forcing a Stage-4 close outside `ExitPolicy` (hard rule 7 / D29) for what is only a universe exit (most dropped names keep trading), and the canonical TEST_PLAN FR-4 fixtures never assert a delist CA on a drop. Implemented correctly in 1.4/1.5 (a drop stamps `removed_on` only) but never recorded as a decision | **D74** — a membership drop stamps `index_membership.removed_on` only; `type='delist'` is reserved for a **true delisting** derived from the exchange symbol-list drop-out (`exchange-symbol-list/US?delisted=1`) / acquisition feed (dormant at launch per D49), landing with Phase 2's corporate-action semantics | MASTER §2 (D74), §13.6; BUILD Phase 2; PROGRESS (proposal retired) |
| 129 | **Canonical ticker identity was implemented but undocumented — the real gap.** 1.4/1.5 canonicalize every source dialect to the EODHD dash form via `SymbolNormalizer`, but no design doc recorded the choice, so a rebuild could pick a different canonical form and silently drop class shares (BRK, BF) from every roster or mismatch bar joins | **D75** — canonical form = EODHD dash (`BRK-B`); mechanical `.`→`-` for the historical/Wikipedia dot form + a curated alias map for the IVV/OEF no-separator forms (`BRKB`→`BRK-B`, `BFB`→`BF-B`) that cannot be split mechanically; anchored to the market-data provider so bar joins need no hot-path translation; the alias map is a stop-and-report seam | MASTER §2 (D75), §13.6 (FR-3) |
| 130 | **Wikipedia membership cross-check returned HTTP 403 at the first live backfill (2026-07-14).** .NET's `HttpClient` sends no default `User-Agent`, and Wikimedia rejects header-less requests — reproduced deterministically (no-UA → 403 / descriptive-UA → 200). The fail-closed reconciler then blocked the whole membership step | The shared `ResilientHttpClient` sets a descriptive `User-Agent` on every request (`ResilientHttpOptions.UserAgent`, default `AlphaLab/1.9 (paper-trading research lab)`, overridable) — now a documented provider rule | INTEGRATIONS §7, §9.1; `ResilientHttpClient` (Phase-1 shared HTTP client) |
| 131 | **A failed backfill lost its API-usage accounting.** `FlushApiUsage` ran only at the successful end of `RunAsync`, so the aborted Stage-1 run made 2 calls but logged 0 rows — the ≥50% headroom check would be blind to spend on any run that fails partway | `BackfillRunner.RunAsync` flushes `api_usage_log` in a `finally`, and each call is counted the instant it succeeds — an aborted run records exactly the calls it spent | `BackfillRunner` (Phase-1 backfill CLI); INTEGRATIONS §1 |
| 132 | **EODHD call cost is per-endpoint, not flat, and a second (per-minute) limit was never modeled.** `api_usage_log` counts calls flatly, but `/news` costs 5 and `/eod-bulk-last-day` costs 100 per request, and EODHD enforces 1,000 requests/minute (via `X-RateLimit-*` headers) independently of the 100k/day cap. The 20-year sp100 backfill's measured spend is **304 calls / 99.7% headroom** (universe-driven, not year-driven) using only cost-1 endpoints, so today's flat count is accurate — but will silently undercount once News/Bulk go live | INTEGRATIONS §1 gains the per-endpoint cost table (`/eod`·`/div`·`/splits`=1, `/news`=5, `/eod-bulk-last-day`=100), the 1,000-req/min limit, and the GMT-midnight counter-reset quirk; §9.5 records the dual limits. **Phase-2 requirement:** `api_usage_log` must weight calls by endpoint cost before Bulk/News come online. §1 marked **VERIFIED 2026-07-15** | INTEGRATIONS §1, §9.5 |
| 133 | **INTEGRATIONS §2 cited the wrong count-sanity config key.** The S&P 500 membership band ([495,510]) was attributed to `Universe.Bootstrap.CountSanity`, which is actually the [99,103] S&P 100 slice band; the [495,510] band's key is the top-level `Universe.MembershipCountSanity` | §2 corrected to `Universe.MembershipCountSanity` ([495,510]); §2b keeps `Universe.Bootstrap.CountSanity` ([99,103]) — matching CONFIG_REFERENCE, where the two keys were already distinct | INTEGRATIONS §2/§2b; CONFIG_REFERENCE |
| 134 | **Reading-convenience PDFs drifted from the authoritative `.md` again** — the finding-99 drift class, recurring. The three PDF snapshots (`MASTER_DESIGN_v1.9.pdf`, `AlphaLab_Explained_Plain_Language.pdf`, `AlphaLab_Master_Design_Section_by_Section.pdf`) still date to 2026-07-13 while MASTER is at v1.9.9; a derivative that silently contradicts its source undermines "rebuild from the docs alone." Same class: CLAUDE.md's map + the root `README.md` file table still said decisions D1–D73 (D74–D75 exist), and CLAUDE.md's map — loaded every build session — omitted `DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS_EXPLAINED.md`, `CHANGELOG_v1.9.md`, and the navigation docs (finding 95's class) | The three PDFs **deleted** and their MANIFEST/START_HERE references removed (the `.md` set is authoritative and self-contained; the plain-language row repoints to `DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS_EXPLAINED.md`); the stale D1–D73 ranges rolled to D1–D75; the CLAUDE.md doc map completed to list every current doc (the previously-orphan `DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS_EXPLAINED.md` now mapped in CLAUDE.md + MANIFEST). No version bump | `docs/*.pdf` (deleted); MANIFEST; START_HERE; CLAUDE.md map; README.md |
| 135 | **Duplicate entry docs are a drift generator with nothing enforcing consistency.** The repo carried two `START_HERE.md` (repo root + `docs/`) kept in lockstep by hand; the root copy silently drifted **three revisions** (it was missing the v1.9.6 + v1.9.9 errata notes and still carried a dead PDF ref) before finding 134 caught it — the same two-copies-diverge failure class as the PDFs, with nothing structural to catch it | Deduplicated: **root `START_HERE.md` is canonical**, `docs/START_HERE.md` deleted. No reference needed repointing — MANIFEST, CLAUDE.md's map, and the document's own links already targeted the root copy | `docs/START_HERE.md` (deleted) |
| 136 | **The setup narrative pointed at source paths absent from the deliverable.** START_HERE step 2 and MANIFEST told a fresh cloner to *copy* `docs/CLAUDE.md` and `docs/PROGRESS.md` into the repo root — but the repo IS the deliverable and already keeps `CLAUDE.md` + `PROGRESS.md` at root; there is no `docs/CLAUDE.md` to copy from, so the step could not be followed | START_HERE step 2 reworded to "clone the repo — the layout is already in place, nothing to copy"; MANIFEST lists `CLAUDE.md` + `PROGRESS.md` as repo-root files. *(`docs/README_v1.9.md` §3 step 2 and its Tier-2 rows carried the same 'copy to repo root' wording — now aligned to the same repo-root framing.)* | START_HERE (step 2); MANIFEST |
| 137 | **The repo's landing README advertised a stale phase.** Root `README.md`'s Status said "Phase 0 complete — the skeleton … 39 tests," but Phase 1 (the data foundation) is code-complete and the live S&P 100 backfill has run (200 tests green) — the first thing a visitor reads was a phase out of date | Status rewritten to "Phase 1 complete — the data foundation" (the market-data layer + the live backfill), the Phases 2–8 remainder, and the 200-test count; the `no_run_yet` note narrowed to the strategy/evaluation screens (no forward pipeline run exists yet) | root `README.md` §Status |

*Verified-and-skipped: the retired D74 proposal asked to correct a BUILD "ticker dropping out ⇒ delist" line — a grep of every committed doc found **no such line** (each `delist` mention is the legitimate true-delisting corporate-action type, or MASTER §6's "a wish-list drop is never an implicit sell"), so no BUILD edit was made.*

## Revision v1.9.10 — Phase-1 review remediation (2026-07-15)

*A second fresh-eyes review of the sealed Phase-1 repo, one pass after v1.9.9. Where v1.9.9 reconciled the docs, this pass fixes **fail-open code defects** the review surfaced (rule-10 violations in a fail-closed codebase) plus audit-trail, scalability, config-consistency, and CI-hygiene gaps — all with **no schema/migration/config-key change** (rule 14 + snapshot-first). Findings **138+**. Three schema-change decision proposals are parked in PROGRESS awaiting **D76** — not numbered here, because they need the schema changes this pass forbids. Checkpoint labels P1R-1…P1R-8 map to the deliverable; findings are numbered in commit order.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 138 | **A fourth, unguarded copy of the DB connection string could silently split-brain the lab (P1R-8).** `ConfigConsistencyTests` forced only the Worker + Api `appsettings.json` to equal `DbPathResolver.DefaultConnectionString` ("three edit spots"), but `tools/Backfill/appsettings.json` — added in checkpoint 1.10, after the guard — holds a fourth copy it never checked. A fresh clone following README §2 repoints the three guarded spots to `{LocalAppData}` and leaves the CLI on `E:\`: the backfill writes a full DB the Worker/Api never see, with no error anywhere | `ConfigConsistencyTests` now guards all three appsettings copies (Api + Worker + **Backfill CLI**, whose path is under `tools/` not `src/`) against the resolver const — the fourth spot. "three → four" corrected in the test doc-comment, `DbPathResolver`, README §2, and DB_RELOCATION §2/§3/§5. No schema change | `ConfigConsistencyTests.cs`; `DbPathResolver.cs`; `README.md` §2; `DB_RELOCATION.md` §2/§3/§5 |
| 139 | **Dividend cash silently fell back to the split-adjusted value (P1R-1).** `CorporateActionIngestion` wrote `CashPerShare = d.UnadjustedValue ?? d.Value`; on a null `unadjustedValue` the fallback writes the split-adjusted `value` — wrong by the cumulative split factor (112× on the oldest AAPL fixture row: 0.10976 actual cash vs 0.00098 adjusted) — into the field the Phase-2 ledger will price cash on. A fail-**open** default in a fail-closed codebase (rule 10) | Fails closed at the **parse boundary** (mirroring `ParseSplitRatio`): `ParseDividends(symbol, json)` throws `FormatException` naming symbol + ex-date on a null `unadjustedValue`; ingestion's `?? d.Value` becomes a defensive `?? throw` (naming `security_id` + ex-date) for the non-provider path. A read-only `tools/audit-dividend-unadjusted.ps1` re-parsed the 101 archived `/div` payloads — **6,204 events, 0 null** — so the throw is safe (no live-cache dividend triggers it); the 6,204 matches the live DB's dividend-row count. No schema change | `EodhdMarketDataProvider.ParseDividends`; `CorporateActionIngestion.cs`; `tools/audit-dividend-unadjusted.ps1`; CLAUDE.md map |
| 140 | **`api_usage_log` overwrote spend, and the headroom check read only run-local spend (P1R-2).** `ApiUsageLogWriter.Record` did `row.Calls = calls` on an existing `(as_of, source)` row, so a second same-day run (the Worker plus a manual backfill) *erased* the first's spend; and `FlushApiUsage` computed the ≥50% headroom total from the run-local `_apiCalls`, so two runs of 2 against a limit of 4 left the DB correctly at 4 while **neither run breached** — the row told the truth and nothing read it | `Record` now **accumulates** (`+= calls`); `FlushApiUsage` persists, **drains** `_apiCalls` (a second flush adds zero), then checks headroom against the accumulated EODHD-family **day total read from the DB**. The ≥50% arithmetic and the `eodhd`+`eodhd_gspc` family grouping are unchanged — only the sum's *source* moved from the run-local dict to the persisted day total. No schema change | `ApiUsageLog.cs`; `BackfillRunner.FlushApiUsage`; +2 negative tests (cross-run breach, double-flush idempotency) + 1 existing flipped to accumulate |
| 141 | **`GetSeries` materialized a security's entire history to return a slice (P1R-3).** `BarReadService.GetSeries` filtered on `SecurityId` only, called `.AsEnumerable()`, then range-filtered `Date` in memory — pulling a name's whole ~4,800-row 20-year history (of 488,217 live bars) to hand back a window. Phase-2's daily pipeline and Phase-4 replay (~5,000 sessions × ~100 names) are the callers | The `[from,to]` range is pushed into SQL via `string.Compare(x.Date, …) >= 0 / <= 0` (EF's translatable form — **not** `CompareOrdinal`, which `SecurityMaster.ResolveAsOf` warns may not translate), so PK `(security_id, date, version)` serves it; the watermark compare stays in memory (`GetBar` precedent). SQLite's default BINARY collation is ordinal, so ISO-8601 lexical order == chronological (semantically safe). One test asserts the generated SQL's WHERE contains the `date` predicate (no client eval); a Theory asserts byte-identical results vs the old in-memory path across the FX-BarCorrection fixture + both watermark boundaries. No behavior change, no schema change | `BarService.GetSeries`; +3 tests (translation proof + byte-identical) |
| 142 | **Raw payloads were archived under the query bound, not the observation day (P1R-4).** `EodhdMarketDataProvider` filed `/eod` under `to` but `/div` and `/splits` under `from`, so the 20-year live run filed every dividend and split payload under `2006-07-15/` — twenty years from the day observed, and colliding runs into one directory; the GSPC regime proxy filed under `to` too. The cache's whole purpose (INTEGRATIONS §9) is provenance-by-observation-day, and correctness rested on the caller passing `to == asOf` | An explicit `asOf` (observation day) is threaded through the **contracts** — `IMarketDataProvider.GetEodAsync/GetDividendsAsync/GetSplitsAsync` and `IRegimeProxyProvider.GetProxyBarsAsync` — and all four archive under it (the `from`/`to` query bounds stay query bounds). `BackfillRunner` passes `o.AsOf`. A test archives with `to ≠ asOf` and asserts all four payloads land under `{source}/{asOf}/`, nothing under the bound partitions. No schema change | `MarketData.cs`; `EodhdMarketDataProvider`; `RegimeProxyProvider`; `BackfillRunner`; +1 test |
| 143 | **Membership roster payloads overwrite each run — no dated archive exists (P1R-4b, stop-and-report).** The two remaining `rawCache.Save` sites (`WikipediaMembershipCrossCheck`, `ISharesHoldingsMembershipProvider`) archive under literal `"latest"`, so every run overwrites the prior roster payload. `IIndexMembershipProvider.GetMembersAsync` carries no as-of and `index_membership` has no `observed_at`, so the raw archive is the *only* artifact that could answer "what did OEF/Wikipedia say on date X" — yet none is retained | **Reported, not changed** (a contract + archival-semantics decision, not a mechanical fix like 142): recommend threading an observation date into `IIndexMembershipProvider` and archiving under it. Recorded in PROGRESS's decision proposals, alongside the D40/`corporate_actions` provenance gap it rhymes with. No code change this pass | `WikipediaMembershipCrossCheck`; `ISharesHoldingsMembershipProvider`; PROGRESS proposals |
| 144 | **The documented 30-day raw-cache retention did not exist (P1R-5).** `RawCache`'s doc comment and INTEGRATIONS §9 both promise "30-day retention", but nothing pruned anything — `tools/raw-cache/` grows forever | `FileRawCache.Prune(utcNow)` deletes files older than `RetentionDays = 30` (a fixed constant, **not** a config key — rule 14) by **mtime** (robust to the un-dated `latest` partitions + any mis-partitioning), then sweeps now-empty partition dirs deepest-first (never the root); deletion is confined to the root by construction; a null/empty root fails closed. The backfill CLI invokes it after a successful run. **Caveat (flagged, not decided):** the retention will eventually delete the **bootstrap run's payloads** — this review's only artifact behind the P1R-1 audit + an honest ingest rebuild. Their mtime is recent (2026-07-14/15) so they survive the next 30 days; the exposure is a later backfill. **Whether the bootstrap set should be exempt is open** (Part C: copy `tools/raw-cache/` off-machine before prune goes live). No schema change | `FileRawCache.Prune`; `tools/Backfill/Program.cs`; +3 tests (prune / fail-closed root / missing-root no-op) |
| 145 | **SCHEMA promised foreign keys the database does not have (P1R-6).** `docs/SCHEMA_v1.9.md` carries **13 `REFERENCES` clauses**; the EF model declares none by explicit decision (`DataEntities.cs`: "the SCHEMA `REFERENCES` links are documentation only"), and SQLite would not enforce them anyway without `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` per connection, which nothing sets — so the rule-14 source of truth described constraints the shipped DB does not enforce | Added one bullet to SCHEMA's `## Enforcement notes` recording that the `REFERENCES` clauses are **documentary** (no FK constraints declared, no shadow indexes created, the pragma is off), so the DDL stays a 1:1 description of the shipped migration; referential integrity is upheld in code, not the engine. **Docs only, no code, no migration** | `docs/SCHEMA_v1.9.md` §Enforcement notes |
| 146 | **Nothing enforced CI, and the two tolerated advisories were tolerated by convention only (P1R-7).** `tools/ci.ps1` was green only when an operator ran it, on Windows — there was no `.github/workflows`, so nothing gated a push; and `ci.ps1` had no vulnerability check, so the two known NU1903 transitive advisories were tolerated silently | (a) A minimal GitHub Actions workflow (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) runs `tools/ci.ps1` via `pwsh` on push + PR (`windows-latest`, `actions/setup-dotnet` 10.0.x) — mirroring local CI, not reimplementing it. (b) A **report-only** `dotnet list package --vulnerable --include-transitive` step in `ci.ps1` (after build, so it also runs under `-SkipTests`) surfaces the advisories without gating, commented to become a hard gate once the two clear (`SQLitePCLRaw.lib.e_sqlite3 2.1.11` via EF Core; `Microsoft.OpenApi 2.0.0` via AspNetCore.OpenApi). (c) **LICENSE:** the public repo has none → all-rights-reserved by default; surfaced as an operator action, not chosen unilaterally. CLAUDE.md's map notes the new `.github/workflows/` dir (finding-134 class) | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `tools/ci.ps1`; `CLAUDE.md` map |

*Verified-and-skipped: (1) `BarReadService.GetBar` was checked as a sibling of P1R-3 and already narrows by `SecurityId == id && Date == date` in SQL before its in-memory watermark resolve — no full-history scan, no change. (2) The P1R-1 dividend audit's first run false-flagged all 101 payloads: a Windows PowerShell 5.1 pitfall (`@($raw | ConvertFrom-Json)` captures the whole array as one element), not a data defect — fixed in the script (assign-then-normalize) and re-run clean (6,204 events / 0 null). (3) The 6,204 audited events match the live DB's dividend-row count, confirming the audit covered the ingested population.*

## Revision v1.9.11 — Phase-1 review remediation, cont. (2026-07-15)

*A third fresh-eyes pass, asking one question: can a stranger clone this repo on any future date and build their own arena? It fixes one **live doc error** (already on origin), three code/hygiene gaps, and registers + reconciles the `REBUILD.md` runbook the operator drafted in v1.9.10 — all with **no schema/migration/config-key change** (rule 14). The S&P 500 widening gap (finding 151) is parked in PROGRESS awaiting **D76**, not numbered as a fix, because it needs a MASTER/CONFIG decision this pass forbids. Findings **147+**. The arena-id correction (147) was shipped and pushed alone, ahead of review, because it was wrong on `origin/main`; the rest are committed and held for review.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 147 | **REBUILD §5 gave a cloner two wrong instructions about the arena id (Checkpoint 0).** It claimed the default arena is "named for a universe it doesn't hold" and told a cloner to "Set your own `Arena.Id` before the first run." Per D70 both are wrong: `Arena.Id = "sp500"` **is** correct — this arena is the S&P 500 lab, currently holding the 100-name launch slice and widening **in place** after Phase-4 sign-off. Renaming to `sp100` would strand the store and be undone later; setting the id in one file splits the store | Replaced the paragraph: `sp500` is right, leave it; `Arena.Id` is not a per-clone knob — change it **only** to stand up a *separate* arena (D71), and then the same new value in all three backend appsettings. Links `DB_RELOCATION.md` for the connection-string mechanics rather than restating them. **Docs only.** Shipped + pushed **alone** ahead of the rest of v1.9.11 (a live-error correction on `origin/main`) | `docs/REBUILD.md` §5 |
| 148 | **`Arena:Id` was an unguarded fifth consistency value (P1R-9).** `ConfigConsistencyTests` proved the four connection-string *template* copies are byte-identical — but the template is `Data Source=...\{Arena.Id}\alphalab.db`, a template with a blank in it. Each process (Api/Worker/Backfill) fills `{Arena.Id}` from its own `Arena:Id`, so three byte-identical connection strings can still open three different databases if their `Arena:Id` disagree — the second half of finding 138's split-brain, which the template guard cannot see | A second guard extracts `AssertArenaIdsAgree(paths)` (reads `Arena:Id` from each, throws naming the disagreeing dir=id pairs unless all equal). The positive test **locks** the three real backend appsettings (all `sp500`); the negative drives the *same* method with a mismatched temp pair (sp500/sp100) and asserts it throws — a real failing-on-broken-guard test, not a LINQ tautology. **Honest note:** the positive lock cannot fail on current config (it is correct); the failing-on-current-code negative lives at the method level. Web/wwwroot's plural `Arenas` registry (different shape) is untouched. DB_RELOCATION §1 notes `Arena:Id` resolves the template. No schema change | `ConfigConsistencyTests.cs` (+2); `DB_RELOCATION.md` §1 |
| 149 | **`--universe sp500` was a trap that cost ~300 API calls to discover (P1R-10).** `BackfillArgs.Parse` accepted `sp500` and set band `[495,510]`, but `tools/Backfill/Program.cs:71,75` hardcode the OEF + Wikipedia **S&P 100** feeds. The flag selects only the band, so an sp500 run fetched ~101 names and fail-closed at the count-sanity gate with `count sanity breach: primary=101, crosscheck=101, band=[495,510]` — a data-shaped error for an unwired-code cause, minutes and ~300 calls in. A newcomer debugs their API token | The `sp500` switch arm now **throws at parse** naming the real cause (unwired providers) and pointing at the D76 widening proposal; the run exits in the first second, not the fifth minute. The generic unknown-universe message no longer advertises `sp500`. IVV is **not** wired — that is the D76 decision, not a fix. The `Parse_Sp500_WidensCountBand` test became `Parse_Sp500_Rejected` (Throws + "not wired"), which fails on the pre-fix code. No schema change | `BackfillArgs.Parse`; `BackfillArgsTests` (repurposed) |
| 150 | **Nothing checked that the live sources still look right — green CI only proved the fixtures parse (P1R-11).** Between `--dry-run` (resolves config, zero network) and `SETUP §7` (a manual, once-in-2026 ritual) there was nothing, and every provider test is fixture-backed — so `ci.ps1` stays green while OEF's URL moves, Wikipedia's markup changes, or an EODHD endpoint is revised. The first live backfill already hit a Wikimedia 403 caught only by running against the real thing (finding 130) | New `--preflight` command (`BackfillPreflight`): one **read-only** pass over every live contact point — DB-path writability (a write-free ancestor probe; catches a half-applied relocation / missing drive before any spend), OEF + Wikipedia count-band, `/eod/AAPL.US` adjusted_close, `/div/AAPL.US` unadjustedValue (finding 139's parse throw surfaces as a named Fail *before* the backfill hits it), `/eod/GSPC.INDX` index shape, and a calendar-age **warn**. Creates no DB, writes nothing (NullRawCache), exits non-zero on any Fail, and exercises the *same* providers a live run uses. `NyseCalendar.SpecialClosuresReviewedThroughYear = 2025` is a documentary const mirroring `FileRawCache.RetentionDays`, **not a config key** (rule 14). Probe windows are asymmetric on purpose (eod/GSPC short shape-check; div wide = `o.From`, where a null `unadjustedValue` lives). No schema change | `BackfillPreflight.cs`; `NyseCalendar.cs`; `BackfillArgs`; `tools/Backfill/Program.cs`; +9 tests |
| 151 | **D70's post-Phase-4 widening is not achievable as built (stop-and-report → D76).** D70(a) promises the universe widens sp100→sp500 "by a config flip + backfill delta, no code change." Both legs are unmet: `CONFIG_REFERENCE:39-40` documents `Universe.Bootstrap.Universe` as "consumed by the backfill CLI," but the CLI reads `--universe` from argv; `UniverseOptions` (S&P-500 defaults) is **bound by nothing** and its defaults *contradict* the CLI's behaviour; `Program.cs:71,75` hardcode `Oef()` + `wikipedia_sp100`. So the widening will require code, and a future reader could wrongly assume `UniverseOptions` is live | **Reported, not built** (a MASTER §2 / CONFIG_REFERENCE decision, D-number territory): recommend either (a) make provider selection config-driven so the flip is genuinely a config flip, or (b) correct D70 + `CONFIG_REFERENCE`'s false "consumed by the backfill CLI" claim. **Cost note:** the repo already carries `tests/Fixtures/IVV_holdings.csv`, `tests/Fixtures/sp500_constituents.html`, and `ISharesHoldingsOptions.Ivv()` — everything except the wiring and the decision — so option (a) is "bind + map to existing presets," testable against on-disk fixtures, not "build S&P 500 support." Recorded in PROGRESS awaiting D76 | `CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md`; `UniverseOptions.cs`; PROGRESS proposals |
| 152 | **`REBUILD.md` existed but was registered nowhere (P1R registration; finding-134 class).** The operator drafted `docs/REBUILD.md` in v1.9.10 (committed at HEAD), but it appeared in neither `MANIFEST.md`'s file list nor `CLAUDE.md`'s documentation map — the exact omission finding 134 fixed for other files | Registered `docs/REBUILD.md` in `MANIFEST.md` (file list, near DB_RELOCATION; manifest title version deliberately not rolled — no CI doc-version guard) and `CLAUDE.md`'s doc map. Reconciled REBUILD §2/§5 with what shipped: `--preflight` added as §2's first live check; the sp500 bullet rewritten (rejected at parse, widening = D76); the "no command performs it" clause and the calendar bullet pointed at `--preflight`. Also corrected CLAUDE's DB_RELOCATION line "three edit spots" → "four connection-string edit spots + Arena:Id agreement" (a v1.9.10 finding-138 straggler). **Docs only** | `docs/MANIFEST.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `docs/REBUILD.md` §2/§5 |

*Verified-and-skipped: (1) `src/AlphaLab.Web/wwwroot/appsettings.json` carries the plural `Arenas` registry (an `ArenaEntry` list with `baseUrl`) — a different shape that never opens the DB, deliberately excluded from the `Arena:Id` guard. (2) `UniverseOptions` is confirmed defined-but-unbound (no `Configure`/`GetSection`/`.Bind`/`.Get<>` anywhere, no appsettings `Universe` section) — recorded as the D76 proposal, not a code fix. (3) The `--preflight` splits probe was intentionally omitted (~3 EODHD calls: eod, div, GSPC): the operator's enumerated check list covers the shapes a backfill depends on, and splits share the fail-closed `ParseSplitRatio` path already exercised by the div check's sibling logic.*
