## Revision v1.9.13 — pre-Phase-2 schema decisions (2026-07-15)

*The **first schema change since Phase 1** (SCHEMA had its last edit in v1.9.7). Three of the five parked "D76-territory" proposals are **decided and their schema/read seams landed** — the ones Phase 2's ledger and funnel build directly on, so settling them now avoids re-shaping the foundation mid-build. Each is a snapshot-first EF migration (rule 14) with SCHEMA updated in the same pass, a leakage/point-in-time or round-trip test (rule 4), and a MASTER §2 row. Decisions **D76–D78**; decided range now **D1–D78**. The "D76 territory" cluster label is **retired**: D76/D77/D78 are the three decided here, and the two remaining proposals — the **S&P 500 widening** and **membership provenance** — become un-numbered open proposals (their premature "(D76)" citations, including the `--universe sp500` error string, were de-numbered to "an open proposal, see PROGRESS"). Test count **223 → 236** (Data 214). `ci.ps1` green; migrations verified against a copy of the live 488k-bar arena (the `ux_corporate_actions_identity` unique index applied cleanly, proving no existing duplicates and the `version` default backfilled the 6,273 corporate-action rows). The live arena migration is surfaced as an operator action (not auto-applied).*

| # | Decision / finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|--------------------|-----------|-------|
| 160 | **D76 — `corporate_actions` was unversioned + read by nothing at a watermark, so Phase-4 replay would leak the future and dividend restatements were silently dropped.** `observed_at` was written but never read; ingestion was existence-skip on `(security_id, type, effective_date)` (first-write-wins); no read service existed. Migration `CorporateActionVersioning` (no hand-edit — no new PK). **Known limitation documented:** a regular + special dividend on one ex-date collapse to one versioned line (a stop-and-report seam; any discriminator must be feed-supplied, not amount-based). Callers pass the run's `AsOf` (`BackfillRunner`, `BackfillPreflight`); the dormant `EodhdIndexMembershipProvider` + the `BackfillRunnerTests` stub match the new signature. **Contract + archival semantics only — no `index_membership` column, no migration, no D-number** (the proposal's own framing). Test: a second case in `RawCacheArchivalTests` asserts both rosters land under `{source}/{asOf}/` and nothing under `latest` (+1). Two v1.9.13 stragglers folded in: the MANIFEST title/body + CHANGELOG-coverage line (still v1.9.12) and the root-README test count (still 223) rolled current | `Membership.cs`; `ISharesHoldingsMembershipProvider.cs`; `WikipediaMembershipCrossCheck.cs`; `EodhdIndexMembershipProvider.cs`; `BackfillRunner.cs`; `BackfillPreflight.cs`; `RawCacheArchivalTests.cs`; `BackfillRunnerTests.cs` |

## v1.9.17 — Phase-2 build findings (checkpoints 2.1–2.7)

*The first findings raised **by building** Phase 2 rather than by reviewing it. Finding **A** is a live rule-14 hazard the Phase-2 migration creates and this pass closes; the rest are recorded here as they are resolved by the checkpoint that surfaces them. Schema: the **M1 `Phase2Ledger`** migration creates the eight ledger tables SCHEMA already declared, and **M2 `Phase2RegimeLabels`** the two regime tables — both no-DDL-change (the migration implements what the doc documents). Tests **237 → 460**. NOTE: this table's letters (A/B/L/N…) are assigned in build order and are a SEPARATE scheme from the pre-build plan's findings A–M — where they overlap in text (e.g. the plan's "bankruptcy haircut key") the row says so.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| A | **`SchemaStartup` auto-migrated the live store on every Worker launch, bypassing the mandatory pre-migration snapshot.** BUILD 0.4 specifies this becomes a pending-migration fail-fast "from Phase 1"; it was never shipped, and stayed harmless only because nothing was ever pending — `tools/migrate.ps1` had always migrated the store before the Worker saw it. The moment a Phase-2 migration ships un-applied, an ordinary evening launch would apply it to the operator's live 79 MB store with **no snapshot**, which RUNBOOK §2 forbids ("never run `dotnet ef database update` directly against a non-empty DB") and rule 14 exists to prevent. Phase 2 is also the first phase whose tables hold the lab's **own output** (`trades`, `decisions`, `equity_curve`) — rows no provider can re-fetch — so an auto-migrate here is a silent-data-loss path. | `SchemaStartup` now **verifies and never applies**: `GetPendingMigrationsAsync()` → if any, log the pending list, fail the host non-zero, and name the sanctioned path (`pwsh tools/migrate.ps1 -Arena {id}`). Fails closed (rule 10). The **Backfill CLI** gets the matched treatment with one deliberate asymmetry: it may still **create** a store that does not exist (there is no data to lose, `snapshot-db.ps1` explicitly no-ops on a fresh install, and REBUILD.md §2's documented bootstrap depends on it) but **refuses to migrate an existing one**. Tests: `SchemaStartup_PendingMigrations_FailsClosed_AndAppliesNothing` asserts the store is left with **zero** user tables — it proves the non-action, not just the exception. | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/SchemaStartup.cs`; `tools/Backfill/Program.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/SchemaStartupTests.cs` |
| B | **The eight ledger tables' on-disk fidelity.** SCHEMA declares exactly ONE CHECK across them (`trades.side`) and none on `strategies.status`, `accounts.run_kind`, `cash_events.type`, or `trades.reason`; EF 10 would also have added `AUTOINCREMENT` to four rowid keys. | M1 hand-edited per rule 14 on the four rowid PKs (`accounts.account_id`, `trades.trade_id`, `cash_events.event_id`, `decisions.decision_id`) — `strategies` is a TEXT PK and three tables are composite, so neither needs it. Exactly one CHECK created. All nine money columns explicitly `HasColumnType("TEXT")` (D69) so a future provider/convention change cannot silently demote the ledger to REAL. `SchemaFidelityTests` 15 → **23 tables**, with the still-absent tables and each one's reason named in the assertion. | `docs/SCHEMA_v1.9.md` (AUTOINCREMENT enforcement note); `src/AlphaLab.Data/Migrations/*_Phase2Ledger.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Data.Tests/SchemaFidelityTests.cs` |
| L | **Stage-1's `liquid` gate filters nothing, and nothing in the design says what it would filter on.** §6's funnel diagram describes Stage 1 as "(in-index flag, liquid, priced)", but `liquid` is defined nowhere as an *eligibility* concept: D20 says "Liquid default" (an S&P-500 universe is liquid by construction, so no filter is needed), and D43's "liquidity bucket" prices a *fill* rather than gating a *name*. There is no `Eligibility.*` config block and no threshold to read, so the gate cannot be built without inventing a key — which "never invent a key" forbids and which would smuggle a design decision in as an implementation detail. The only non-inventing substitute — a data-availability check ("a computable 21-session ADV window with non-zero volume") — is passed by every S&P 100 name and duplicates what `DataQualityGate` already catches, so it would be a no-op wearing the costume of a filter. | **Not built** (correctly), but the no-op is now **stated where it can be seen and executed**, not merely omitted: the `Eligibility` XML doc says "LIQUID CURRENTLY FILTERS NOTHING" and explains why, and `FR7_Stage1_LiquidGateFiltersNothing_AVanishinglyIlliquidNameIsStillEligible` pins it as a test (a name with a 1-share/day ADV is fully eligible) so the fact is executable rather than a comment someone skims. Recorded as **PROGRESS proposal P1** — decide (define an `Eligibility.*` threshold with a D-number, or strike `liquid` from §6) **before the D70 sp500 widening**, where the tail genuinely is less liquid and a no-op gate becomes a silent wrong answer rather than a harmless one. | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Funnel/Eligibility.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Core.Tests/FunnelTests.cs`; PROGRESS (P1) |
| N | **"21-day realized vol" has two consumers and no stated convention.** D43's impact term needs a daily σ (`k·σ·√(Q/ADV)`) and D50/§20.1's volatility component needs "21-day realized vol" against its own trailing distribution — but neither states the window convention (does an N-day vol read N closes or N+1?) nor the denominator (n or n−1). Two independent implementations would answer differently the first time either drifted, and the disagreement would surface as a mispriced fill or a wrong regime label — neither of which points at its cause. | Defined **once**, in `PriceStatistics` (Core, pure), consumed by `BarFeatureView` now and by the FR-26 labeler at 2.8 so the two cannot drift. Both conventions are pinned by name in tests: an **N-session vol reads N+1 closes** (N returns), and the stdev is the **sample** form (n−1) — the population form is biased low, which on the impact path means systematically under-charging, so `SampleStdev_UsesTheBesselCorrection_NotThePopulationForm` explicitly asserts the rejected value too. Dispersion below two values is **null, never 0.0** — a zero σ claims a riskless name and prices D43's impact term at exactly nothing. | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Domain/PriceStatistics.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Core.Tests/PriceStatisticsTests.cs` |
| O | **ADV-in-shares is not split-invariant, and there is no adjusted volume to read.** D43's participation cap and its √(Q/ADV) ratio need ADV in *shares* (2.3 settled that seam), but `bars.volume` is raw as the feed delivers it, so a 21-session window spanning a split mixes pre- and post-split share counts and the average is not a share count in any single basis. EODHD supplies no adjusted volume, so there is nothing else to read. | **Documented as a stop-and-report seam, not papered over**, in `BarFeatureView.Adv21Shares`. Bounded: it distorts at most one 21-session window per split, and it biases the participation *cap* rather than a *price*. `Adv21Notional` is provably immune — price÷r × volume×r is invariant — which is why the D43 spread bucket never sees it. Revisit if a split lands on a name being actively traded near the cap. Separately pinned: notional uses the **raw** close, never the adjusted one (`FR10_AdvNotional_UsesTheRawClose_NotTheAdjustedOne`) — the adjusted close is a back-projected price that never existed, and on a 20-year-old bar it would understate notional by every dividend and split since, quietly bucketing a mega-cap as illiquid. | `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/BarFeatureView.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Data.Tests/BarFeatureViewTests.cs` |
| P | **"Re-size the whole book to target every day" is the obvious Stage-4 default and it is wrong — §5.1's own acceptance criterion disproves it.** §6 describes Stage 4 as "open/add/trim/exit" but never says WHICH positions get re-sized on a given day. Daily rebalance-to-target reads as the natural default, and it silently fails Buy&Hold's acceptance test by construction: §5.1 requires B&H to "enter once, never churn, pay ONE entry cost", but under daily rebalancing B&H would trade every session nudging each drifted position back to target. Momentum says the same from the other side — the catalog insists it is "never run without the rank buffer" precisely because boundary churn bleeds cost, and the buffer saves nothing if the sizer re-trades the book behind it each evening. | Resolved in `PortfolioPlanner.ScopeFor`: **price drift is allowed; a position is sized when opened and then left alone until its policy says otherwise.** Only `ScheduledRebalance` re-sizes, and only on its own cadence (`RebalanceScope.WholeBook` on a rebalance day, else `OpensOnly`) — which is exactly what "trim" means in §6 and what D68's monthly EW rebalance is. Composes across all three Phase-2 policies: Never → one sizing, no churn; ScheduledRebalance(21) → monthly re-weight; RankBuffer → size each entry once, exit on rank. **NOT done:** continuously enforcing `Sizing.PositionCapPct` against live drift — a real exposure, but trimming it daily reintroduces the churn; the cap binds at sizing time and live-exposure guardrails are FR-17 (Phase 7). Pinned by `FR9_ARebalanceTrimTradesTheDelta_ANameAlreadyAtTargetTradesNothing`. | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Funnel/PortfolioPlanner.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Core.Tests/FunnelRunnerTests.cs` |
| Q | **`decisions.stage_json` is a two-job artifact, and the second job is easy to under-build.** It is both (1) the "why this trade" provenance SCHEMA:204 names AND (2) the physical carrier that hands orders decided at close T to the T+1 run that fills them — there is no `orders` table (a decision confirmed this checkpoint; see PROGRESS). That second job imposes constraints a pure-provenance blob would not have: it must round-trip byte-exactly, refuse an unknown shape rather than best-effort parse it, and carry a `SecurityId` a human can read. A default `System.Text.Json` serialization satisfies none cleanly — `SecurityId` (a struct) serializes as `{"value":42}`, and an unversioned blob parsed by a future build throws an opaque `JsonException` about some inner field rather than naming the real cause. | `DecisionSnapshot` carries a `snapshot_version` (`ds-1.0`); `FromJson` **peeks the version before binding the body** and fails closed with a clear message on an unknown/missing version (rule 10 — a half-understood order list is worse than a stopped run). `SecurityIdJsonConverter` writes a bare number (and a bare string as a dict key), so stage_json is `"security_id": 42`, readable and round-tripping. Note: stage_json is **internal** (DB TEXT, read by the Worker), NOT an API read-model, so D60/rule-20's money-as-strings contract does not bind it — `decimal` serializes as an exact JSON number and round-trips losslessly. Pinned by `FR9_Stage6Orders_RoundTripThroughStageJson_Exactly`, `FR9_AnUnknownSnapshotVersion_IsRefused`, `FR9_SecurityIdSerializesAsABareNumber`. **Size watch:** the snapshot is deliberately complete (every eligible name + score + reason, ~KB/strategy-day) — worth monitoring at Phase-4 replay scale; recorded in PROGRESS. | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Funnel/DecisionSnapshot.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Core/Domain/SecurityId.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Core.Tests/FunnelRunnerTests.cs` |
| R | **The stoppage freeze fires on a SINGLE missing session, not just a persistent stoppage — deliberately, but it is a seam.** §13.6 says "bar-stoppage with no mapped event freezes the position and alerts", and the honest fail-closed reading (rule 10) is: a held name with no bar today cannot be valued today, so carrying it at a stale price is the silent misprice to avoid. So `StoppageFreezeReason` freezes on one missing session. For the liquid S&P 100 a benign one-day gap is rare, so this is safe now — but a threshold ("N consecutive missing sessions") would be a new `Data.StoppageFreezeSessions` CONFIG key + a D-number, so it is **not** invented here. | **Documented as a stop-and-report seam** in `CorporateActionLedger.StoppageFreezeReason` and recorded in PROGRESS. Bounded by an operator: a freeze alerts and an operator resolves it (a benign gap is unfrozen, or the next day's bar makes the point moot). Revisit **before the sp500 widening**, whose thinner tail may produce benign single-day gaps that would otherwise freeze noisily. A dividend/split does NOT clear the freeze (the name should still be trading); only a terminal action (merger/spin-off/delist) explains a missing bar, and §13.6 part 2 owns that exit. Pinned by `FR9_FxUnmapped_*` and `FR9_ADividendDoesNotExplainAMissingBar_StillFreezes`. | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Ledger/CorporateActionLedger.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Data.Tests/CorporateActionApplierTests.cs`; PROGRESS |
| S | **Same-ex-date dividend-then-split ordering is unstated in §13.6, and it changes the dividend amount.** If a dividend and a split share one date, the dividend must be paid on the pre-split shares of record — pay it after the split and it doubles. §13.6 lists the semantics but not the order. | Resolved by relying on the existing D76 read order: `GetActionsAsOf` returns actions ordered by `(effective_date, type)`, and `'dividend' < 'split'` lexically, so on a shared date the dividend is applied first (pre-split shares) and the split second. Made explicit in `CorporateActionApplier` (a comment on the order it depends on) rather than left to luck; the per-action re-read of the position means the split then sees the post-dividend share count, which is correct (a dividend does not change shares). Zero occurrences on the live 6,273-row feed; prospective. | `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/CorporateActionApplier.cs` |
| T | **Two §13.6 rules the design calls "config" never had a key** (the pre-build plan's findings **B** and **C**): the delist "bankruptcy haircut configurable (D30)" and the spin-off "scheduled liquidation rule, config". Without keys the behaviour is hard-coded and unfalsifiable. | Added the `CorporateActions` config section + `CorporateActionsOptions` (Core): `BankruptcyHaircutPct` (delist exits at `last_print × (1 − pct/100)`) and `SpinoffLiquidationDays` (0 = exit-only). Both govern DORMANT feeds (D49), so neither fires on live data in Phase 2; the keys make the behaviour stated and testable. **The haircut default (0) carries a real tension, stated in the option's doc:** a "terminal, no successor" delist is usually distress, so 0% can flatter a wipeout — but a large default would fabricate a loss the feed cannot substantiate, so the honest resolution is a KNOWN-severity operator override, not a guessed default. `SpinoffLiquidationDays > 0` EXECUTION (selling the receipt after N days) is a Stage-4 behaviour deferred to Phase 7; the key is carried for fidelity and PROGRESS records the line. | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Config/CorporateActionsOptions.cs`; `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md` |
| U | **Merger and spin-off BASIS handling is genuinely underspecified in §13.6, and the feeds are dormant.** A mixed merger must split basis between the cash and stock legs; a spin-off must split basis between the parent and the receipt "by the action's ratio (fallback: first-print relative value)" — but a single share-count `ratio` field cannot define a value-basis split. | **Documented, defensible approximations rather than invented precision** — and the fixtures pin the INVARIANTS (basis conserved, position created), not the soft split. Mixed merger: the FULL target basis carries to the stock leg and the cash leg is a distribution (total value is conserved at the event; only realize-now vs realize-later differs — a tax-grade allocation is a nicety a paper lab does not need). Spin-off (`SpinoffAllocation`): by-ratio path allocates basis share-proportionally (crude, no prices) and the first-print path allocates by relative value (honest, the §13.6 fallback). Both are stop-and-report seams recorded in PROGRESS; prefer feeding the first-print path when the feed turns on. Forced-event fills waive standard costs (§13.6) and stamp `cost_model_version = "corp-action-waived"` so they are never mistaken for a D43-priced fill. | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Ledger/CorporateActionLedger.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Core/Ledger/SpinoffAllocation.cs` |
| V | **`processed_on` is bait: a column that can never be correctly written, with a comment inviting a future implementer to wire against it.** SCHEMA read `-- NULL until ledger applied`, an instruction; but it is a GLOBAL column on a PER-ACCOUNT operation, so stamping it would make a replay skip an action a forward run marked (breaking the quarantine), and D76 forbids UPDATE here anyway. | Three amendments (the operator's three, applied verbatim): (1) the SCHEMA comment is **rewritten** — "ALWAYS NULL, never written; D76 forbids UPDATE; idempotency is one-transaction-per-day + `ux_runs_ok_forward`, not this flag" (the entity XML doc too); (2) the CI guard is **widened** — `ci.ps1` now `Assert-NoMatch`es `DELETE/UPDATE corporate_actions` beside the bars pair, turning "we agreed not to" into "it can't merge" (D76 extended append-only to a second table; the guard finally follows); (3) "drop the column" is **logged as a deferral** (PROGRESS proposal **P5**) with its reason — dropping it is a migration + a D-number and the live store already has it, so not now, but a column that buys nothing and can never be written is a deferral, not a resolution. | `docs/SCHEMA_v1.9.md`; `src/AlphaLab.Data/Entities/DataEntities.cs`; `tools/ci.ps1`; PROGRESS (P5) |
| W | **The D50 regime label is path-dependent (hysteresis), and the naive way to make it fast is a quarantine leak.** §20.1's trend label holds yesterday's value unless a flip is confirmed over 5 sessions, so today's label depends on the whole trajectory since warm-up. The tempting shortcut — read yesterday's persisted `regime_labels` row for the "prior label" — is wrong: that row was computed at a *different* run's watermark, so reading it imports a forward run's state into a replay (breaking rule 1) and makes the label non-reproducible under D40's f(inputs, watermark). Two further conventions §20.1 leaves unstated: whether "today" sits inside its own trailing vol distribution, and which percentile rule sets the `high_vol` threshold. | `RegimeLabeler` (Core, pure) **recomputes the entire trajectory** from the watermarked series it is handed and never reads a prior persisted label — so the label at asOf is a pure function of proxy data ≤ asOf at the watermark (F-LEAK is therefore a service-level test: the label is invariant to future bars + later-observed corrections, and `inputs_hash` carries the watermark so a re-run at a different watermark is a distinct provenance). It reuses `PriceStatistics.RealizedVolDaily` (finding N) so D50's σ is byte-identical to D43's. Conventions pinned in code + tests: the trailing vol window is **inclusive** of today (immaterial at 756 points, fixed for reproducibility) and the threshold is the **linear-interpolation** percentile. Episodes (D45) accrue **forward** from the first live label — the incremental close/open chain records the forward sequence, never backfilling 20 years of warm-up history. | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Regime/RegimeLabeler.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/RegimeLabelService.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Core.Tests/RegimeLabelerTests.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Data.Tests/RegimeLabelServiceTests.cs`; PROGRESS (P6) |
| X | **`Regime.*` config was silently ignored (the plan's finding F).** `AddAlphaLabMembership` registered an unbound `new RegimeOptions()`, so every bound `Regime:*` value was discarded — harmless only because nothing consumed it yet. FR-26 (the regime-label service) is the first consumer, so 2.8 owns the bind (CONFIG key rule 7 — the consuming phase binds). | `AddAlphaLabMembership` now accepts the bound `RegimeOptions` (the composition root binds `config.GetSection("Regime")` and passes it; the Data layer stays free of an `IConfiguration` dependency, keeping the bind at the root where finding F places it) and registers `IRegimeLabelService`. `RegimeBindTests` proves the passed instance is the resolved one and that no bind keeps the CONFIG defaults. The sibling `DataQualityOptions`/`CalendarOptions` binds land with their consumers (2.10 / catch-up); `UniverseOptions` is deliberately left unregistered (wiring it IS the D70-widening proposal, finding 151), and the option's doc names all three so the next reader knows which are wired and which are not. | `src/AlphaLab.Data/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Data.Tests/RegimeBindTests.cs` |
| Y | **The cap-weight benchmark proxy was pinned to "an S&P 500 ETF" while the strategies trade the S&P 100 slice (the pre-build plan's finding I).** §5.1 named "an S&P 500 ETF proxy", but the traded universe through Phase-4 sign-off is the D70 S&P 100 slice — so Jensen's-alpha would be measured against a DIFFERENT universe than the strategies actually trade, a benchmarking bias. Separately, §5.1's own acceptance ("total return = proxy total return − one entry cost") is a claim the funnel + ledger must satisfy end-to-end, and nothing exercised it yet (the D53 pipeline is 2.10). | The cap-weight proxy is now **config-driven** (`CapWeightProxy.SymbolFor`): `oef_csv` ⇒ `OEF.US`, `ivv_csv` ⇒ `IVV.US`, following `Universe.Bootstrap.MembershipPrimary` — so the S&P-500 widening flips the benchmark with no code change; unknown sources fail closed. §5.1 is corrected and the **expense-ratio bias is disclosed** (~0.20%/yr OEF, ~0.03%/yr IVV sits inside the benchmark — a known, bounded, one-directional bias, named rather than swapped for a synthetic index; the self-built EW benchmark avoids it, D68). The acceptance is proven by a **pure-engine harness** (`BuyAndHoldModel` scores all-1.0; funnel → `OrderFill`/`VirtualBroker` → §13.6 dividend → mark-to-market, composed exactly as 2.10 will) over a span with a dividend: total return equals proxy total return − one entry cost **EXACTLY, no tolerance** (fractional shares + no overnight gap on the entry make it satisfiable, per OrderBuilder's note), and the book **enters once and never churns** (one trade). **Benchmark run-settings seam (stated, for 2.10):** the CW account needs `Sizing.PositionCapPct = 1.0` (100% in one name) and the EW account needs `Guardrails.MaxConcurrentPositions ≥ the universe` — per-account settings the pipeline supplies, not model concerns. | `src/AlphaLab.Strategies/BuyAndHoldModel.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Strategies/CapWeightProxy.cs`; `docs/STRATEGY_CATALOG_v1.9.md` §5.1; `tests/AlphaLab.Strategies.Tests/BuyAndHoldModelTests.cs` |
| Z | **`accounts.starting_cash` is NOT NULL with no source anywhere (the pre-build plan's finding K).** The schema requires an opening balance but nothing decided or recorded it. | `Accounts.StartingCash` ($100,000) is added to CONFIG_REFERENCE and written by `DummyRoster` as a **versioned `config` row** (`MAX(version)` — the `Regime.ProxySecurityId` precedent), so the value the accounts opened at is auditable rather than a literal only the seeder knew; an operator-set row is honoured over the default, and the roster is idempotent (a re-run registers no duplicate strategy/account and appends no config version). The dummies (`BuyAndHoldModel` ×2 baselines, `ThresholdModel` an honestly-`unregistered` candidate) are the first rows in `strategies`; the trend dummy scores on **adjusted** close (D30) and its 0.0-below-SMA is dropped by the zero-score invariant, exercising a real sparse selection. | `src/AlphaLab.Strategies/DummyRoster.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Strategies/ThresholdModel.cs`; `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md`; `tests/AlphaLab.Strategies.Tests/DummyRosterTests.cs` |

## v1.9.18 — Phase-2 build findings (checkpoints 2.10–2.11 — the D53 pipeline + D47 catch-up)

*The FIRST committed write to the live store, then the catch-up that drives it across missed days. `AlphaLab.Worker` gains the D53 staged daily pipeline (`DailyPipeline`), its zero-write Stage-1 fetch (`Stage1Fetch`), the config binds finding F named, and the D47 catch-up (`MissedSessionResolver` + `CatchupRunner`). Schema: **M3 `RunsForwardUniqueIndex`** — index-only (the `ux_runs_ok_forward` partial unique index), no DDL beyond it; 2.11 adds no migration. Tests **460 → 489** (Worker 9 → 37, +1 Data fidelity test). Letters continue the build-order scheme.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| AA | **The run-row lifecycle is over-constrained — four rules collide and exactly one ordering satisfies them.** FR-29 says a Stage-1 failure writes NOTHING; `data_quality_flags.run_id` is NOT NULL (so flags need a run row); D72 says `run_in_progress` is written OUTSIDE the daily transaction; and `ux_runs_ok_forward` is partial on `status='ok'` (so non-ok rows must be able to exist). A naive "open the run row first, do everything in one transaction" violates FR-29 (a Stage-1 reject would leave a row); "write the run row last" (D53's words) leaves the flags with no run_id and the crash invisible to the next launch. | The pipeline runs the exactly-one ordering RUNBOOK §1 confirms: **(1)** Stage 1 fetch+gate, zero writes; a reject aborts before any row; **(2)** a small committed txn inserts `runs(status='running')` + sets `run_in_progress=1` (committed, so a crash stays visible for D72 recovery); **(3)** ONE Stage-2 transaction does bars → actions → regime → funnel+fills per account → quality flags (run_id now exists) → `status='ok'` → `catchup_log`; **(4)** a small txn clears `run_in_progress` on success. "Run row last" is honoured as *finalisation* last — the row is 'running' from step 2 and flips to 'ok' at the end of step 3. A crash in step 3 rolls the whole day back, leaving `'running'` + `run_in_progress=1` (FX-CrashedRun's fixture) for the next launch. | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/DailyPipeline.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/Pipeline/DailyPipelineTests.cs` |
| BB | **"Stage 1 writes nothing" is a discipline unless it is made structural.** FR-29's zero-write-on-failure is easy to state and easy to violate — any service that holds a `DbContext` can write, so a runtime-only assertion ("the test checks row counts didn't change") leaves the guarantee one careless edit from breaking. | `Stage1Fetch` **takes no `AlphaLabDbContext` in its constructor**, so it *cannot* write — a type-level fact pinned by `Stage1Fetch_TakesNoDbContext_SoItCannotWrite` (reflection over the ctor). The runtime side is pinned too: `Stage1_ProviderHardFails_NoRunRow_NothingWritten` hard-fails the provider and asserts every table is untouched (no runs row, no bars, `run_in_progress` still 0). The orchestrator resolves Stage 1's read-only inputs (the roster, prior actions, last-stored dates) and hands them in, so the fetch touches only the network + the pure gate. | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/Stage1Fetch.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/Pipeline/DailyPipelineTests.cs` |
| CC | **D77's gate warnings had a home but no writer wired to it.** `data_quality_flags` + `IDataQualityFlagStore` landed pre-Phase-2 (D77), but `DataQualityGate`'s `Warn`s were still discarded — "an alarm nobody can see is not an alarm." | The pipeline persists every staged security's flags (warn AND reject) under the run_id once Stage 2 has opened it — the run-row lifecycle (AA) exists partly so `run_id` is available before the flags are written. A run-day WARN (a robust-z return outlier) is proven to land with the correct `run_id` (`RunDay_PersistsGateWarn_UnderRunId`). Flags on dates already gated on a prior run (≤ `LastStoredDate`) are dropped so a re-fetch never re-emits yesterday's warnings — pinned in isolation by `Stage1FetchTests`. | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/Stage1Fetch.cs`, `DailyPipeline.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/Pipeline/Stage1FetchTests.cs`, `DailyPipelineTests.cs` |
| DD | **The Worker had no `Eodhd` section, and two of finding F's three binds were still unwired (the pre-build plan's findings D + F).** CONFIG scoped `Eodhd` to the Backfill CLI, but the forward pipeline must call EODHD for the daily delta; and `DataQualityOptions`/`CalendarOptions` were still registered as unbound `new()` defaults (2.8 wired only `Regime`). | The Worker composition root binds `Eodhd`, `Costs`, `Sizing` (`Mode=equal`, finding E), `Guardrails`, `Data`, `CorporateActions`, and `Regime` from appsettings and registers them BEFORE `AddAlphaLabMembership` (so its `TryAddSingleton` defaults become no-ops) — closing finding F's remaining two binds at their consuming phase (CONFIG key rule 7). The EODHD token is read **defensively** (a missing token is not a startup failure — a no-op launch spends nothing; the provider only 401s on a real fetch). `UniverseOptions` stays unregistered on purpose (wiring it is the D70-widening job, finding 151). CONFIG_REFERENCE is corrected: `Eodhd` is no longer backfill-only. | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Program.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Worker/appsettings.json`; `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md` |
| FF | **D47 catch-up needs a forward-start anchor the plan states only for the running case, and a "last completed session" the calendar cannot answer alone.** The plan's set is `SessionsBetween(lastOk+1, lastCompletedSession)` minus ok forward runs — but on a FRESH store there is no `lastOk`, and the calendar knows sessions, not whether a session's close has passed *now* (the D61 guard is "process through the last COMPLETED session only"). Two silent-wrong-answer traps: replay 20 years on first launch (no floor), or process today before its close (a half-day). | `MissedSessionResolver` (Worker) is a pure query over runs + bars + calendar + clock. **Floor** = the session after the last 'ok' forward run's as_of; on a fresh store it falls back to the latest **bar date** (the backfill watermark), so forward operation begins where the historical data ends — never 20 years back; an empty store (no runs, no bars) has nothing to do. **Last completed session** = the latest session whose **ET close has passed** (`now_ET > CloseTime`, close time NOT close+offset — the offset is Scheduled-mode-only per CONFIG); a pre-today session is always complete, today's only after its close. The ET compare (via an injected `TimeProvider` + a cross-platform ET `TimeZoneInfo`) handles DST for free. **Self-correcting resume:** a failed Stage-2 rolls back its bars AND leaves no 'ok' row, so neither the anchor nor the exclusion set advances past a failed day — the next launch resumes exactly there. **`CatchupRunner`** replays the set oldest-first, ONE transaction per day, a FRESH DbContext (DI scope) per day so no cross-day change-tracker state leaks; a fail-closed day STOPS the loop (the committed prefix persists) rather than building later days on a bad one (the T+1 chain depends on it). run_kind: a session processed on its own ET date is 'live', a recovered one 'catchup' (which also writes `catchup_log`). No LLM for past days — structural (no `IAnalysisProvider` until Phase 5). Pinned by `MissedSessionResolverTests` (empty/fresh/caught-up/before-close/after-close/resume) + `CatchupRunnerTests` (FX-Outage5d, replay-in-order, same-evening no-op, no-run-before-close, fail-closed stop). | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/MissedSessionResolver.cs`, `CatchupRunner.cs`, `OnDemandRunner.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/MissedSessionResolverTests.cs`, `Pipeline/CatchupRunnerTests.cs` |
| EE | **P4 closed, and finding M (gate the never-gated backfill) is bounded, not silently daily-re-gated.** The `orders`-table revisit window (P4) reached its 2.10 deadline; and finding M ("the pipeline must gate the backfilled bars ingestion never vetted") admits a naive reading — re-fetch + re-gate 20 years every day — that would hammer the API budget AND re-emit every historical warning daily (flag spam). | **P4 is SETTLED on `decisions.stage_json`** (decision unchanged): the real stage_json shape (finding Q) confirms an order has no lifecycle (decided at close T, fully resolved at open T+1 — filled / clipped to `capacity_rejections` / rejected), so a table would be a write-once/read-once second copy of a fact stage_json must record anyway, and `capacity_rejections` already gives the SQL-queryable rejection audit. Finding M: the daily gate runs over the fetched delta with a **bounded context tail** (enough neighbours for the outlier/reconciliation checks), and flags on already-gated dates are dropped (CC) — so new problems are caught without re-emitting old ones. The **one-time sweep of the full backfilled history** is recorded as **PROGRESS proposal P7** (it wants a `bars.gated_through` marker rather than a daily full-history re-gate). The **daily-membership REFRESH** (re-fetching OEF/Wikipedia) is a stated deferral — the funnel reads the current stored roster (`MembersAsOf`); wiring the membership providers into the forward Worker duplicates the Backfill CLI's composition and belongs with the D70-widening work. | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/DailyPipeline.cs`, `Stage1Fetch.cs`; PROGRESS (P4 closed, P7 new) |

## v1.9.19 — Phase-2 build findings (checkpoint 2.12 — the D72 launch order + backup; Phase 2 CLOSED)

*The process model that wraps the daily write: the OnDemand launch order **stale-run recovery → catch-up → job drain → local backup → exit**, the liveness heartbeat, the API's 409 decision (`IWorkerLiveness`, shipped as decision logic — the endpoint lands with FR-32), and the API rate-limit seam. No migration. Tests **489 → 527** (Worker 37 → 55, Data 265 → 285). Letters continue the build-order scheme.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| GG | **Stale-run recovery is only possible because step 2 of the run-row lifecycle commits OUTSIDE the daily transaction — and it must run BEFORE catch-up.** A crash inside Stage 2 rolls the day back but leaves `runs(status='running')` + `run_in_progress=1` committed (AA's step 2). Left alone, that flag would make the API 409 every command forever (rule 24). Recovery after catch-up would be too late — the resolver would already have tried to replay the orphaned session with the flag still set. | `StaleRunRecovery` is step 1 of the D72 order: it finds the orphan (`run_in_progress=1` with no fresh heartbeat), marks its run `'failed'`, and clears the flag — so the crashed day becomes an ordinary missed session the catch-up resolver replays fresh. `OnDemandRunner` drives recovery → catch-up → drain → backup → exit in exactly that sequence. Pinned by `StaleRunRecoveryTests` (orphan recovered = FX-CrashedRun) + `LaunchOrderTests.FullLaunchOrder_AfterCrash_RecoversThenCatchesUp` (the orphan is cleared, then Run1 replays to exactly one `'ok'` row beside the `'failed'` one). | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/StaleRunRecovery.cs`, `OnDemandRunner.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/StaleRunRecoveryTests.cs`, `LaunchOrderTests.cs` |
| HH | **Liveness is TWO facts, not one — conflating them either 409s a dead run forever or lets two writers run.** "Is a write in progress?" has two answers that must not collapse: `IsLive` (`run_in_progress=1` AND a fresh heartbeat — a real writer, the 409 case) versus `IsStale` (`run_in_progress=1` but the heartbeat is stale/absent — an orphaned crash the next launch recovers, and must NEVER 409). A missing or unparseable heartbeat is the ambiguous case. | The pure `WorkerLivenessEvaluator` (AlphaLab.Data) returns both facts; a missing/unparseable heartbeat fails toward **stale** (fail closed — never treat an unknown as a live writer that blocks commands forever). The same verdict drives the Worker's recovery (GG) and — via `IWorkerLiveness` (Data, reached by Api→Data in Phase 3) — the API's 409. The staleness threshold is **caller-supplied** (the Worker passes `Worker.StaleRunThresholdSeconds`, 5× the 30s heartbeat) so Data stays ignorant of Worker options. A launch that finds a **fresh** heartbeat fails closed (`OverlappingWriterException`, D59 sole writer) — the partial **`FR34_NoOverlappingWriters`** guard (the endpoint-level 409 test lands with FR-32). Pinned by `WorkerLivenessTests` (idle/live/stale/null-heartbeat/boundary + reader) + `StaleRunRecoveryTests.FreshHeartbeat_AnotherWriterLive_FailsClosed`. | `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/WorkerLiveness.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Worker/StaleRunRecovery.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Data.Tests/WorkerLivenessTests.cs` |
| II | **An empty job registry must fail closed and VISIBLE, not leave jobs stuck at 'queued'.** Phase 2 registers no `IJobExecutor` (replay/analysis are later phases), so a queued job of any kind has no handler. Silently skipping it would leave it `'queued'` forever — indistinguishable from "not yet started". | `JobDrainer` (step 3, behind `Worker.DrainQueuedJobsOnLaunch`) resolves an executor by `Kind` from a kind-keyed registry; an unregistered kind marks the row `'failed'` with `error_json` naming the missing executor (`no executor registered for kind 'replay' (Phase 2 registers none)`). The "drain outside any write transaction" invariant is enforced **twice**: the drainer asserts `db.Database.CurrentTransaction is null` on entry, and FX-JobDrain's fake executor itself opens its OWN write transaction (which SQLite's single writer would block if one were held). Pinned by `JobDrainTests` (registered→done + own-txn marker; unregistered→failed with named reason; empty queue no-op). | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/JobDrain.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/JobDrainTests.cs` |
| JJ | **The backup path derives PURELY, the copy is per-day idempotent, and pruning is by filename date — not mtime.** RUNBOOK §3's per-launch backup needs a location, an anti-clobber rule, and a retention rule, all deterministic and arena-namespaced (rule 23). | `DbPathResolver.BackupDirectory`/`BackupFilePath` are **pure** (string composition, no filesystem — the `ResolvePath` precedent): `<DbBase>\{arena}\backups\alphalab-{date}.db`, namespaced by construction. `LocalBackup` (step 4) runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` (folds the WAL so a plain file copy is consistent) → copies, **skipping if today's file exists** (a same-day re-launch is a no-op) → prunes copies older than `Ops.BackupRetentionDays` by the **date parsed from the filename** (deterministic, immune to a `touch`). The **off-machine** copy stays a manual operator action (mandatory from the first Phase-2 write; the lab never ships credentials to reach an off-machine target). New `Ops` config section + `OpsOptions` (Core). Pinned by `LocalBackupTests` (dated copy / same-day skip / prune / `TryParseBackupDate`) + `DbPathResolverTests` (backup-path purity). | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/LocalBackup.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Data/DbPathResolver.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Core/Config/OpsOptions.cs`; `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md` |
| KK | **API limits are TWO independent ceilings, and the daily delta is the first workload that can approach either (INTEGRATIONS §1 Phase-2 items).** The 1,000/min limit (surfaced per-response via `X-RateLimit-*`) is distinct from the 100k/day cap (`api_usage_log` headroom); and call cost is **per-endpoint, not flat** — a flat count under-reports against the daily cap the moment a heavy endpoint comes online. | `ResilientHttpClient` honours the **minute** limit reactively: it reads `X-RateLimit-Remaining` and, when at/below a floor, pauses before returning (pure `RateLimitGuard.CooldownFor`, so the header→delay decision is unit-tested without a live endpoint). Endpoint **weighting** ships through the existing `Count(source,n)`/`Record(...)` seam via `EodhdEndpointCost` (`/eod`·`/div`·`/splits`=1, `/news`=5, `/eod-bulk-last-day`=100) — the backfill uses only cost-1 endpoints so its 304 total is numerically **unchanged**, but `api_usage_log` is now weight-correct for when `/news` (Phase 5) and `/eod-bulk-last-day` arrive. **`eod-bulk-last-day` is declared but NOT wired** — at sp100 it costs 100 units vs 101 per-symbol (a wash); it only pays off after the sp500 widening. Pinned by `RateLimitTests` (cooldown decision + header throttle through a stub handler) + `EodhdEndpointCostTests` (weights + unknown→throws). | `src/AlphaLab.Data/Http/ResilientHttpClient.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Data/Providers/EodhdEndpointCost.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/BackfillRunner.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Data.Tests/RateLimitTests.cs`, `EodhdEndpointCostTests.cs` |

## v1.9.20 — PR #1 review findings (pre-merge fixes on the Phase-2 branch)

*Three review findings on PR #1 (the Phase-2 branch), fixed before merge as one commit: an undocumented config key (rule 14's registry), a fail-OPEN backup on a busy WAL checkpoint (rule 10), and a liveness comment that oversold an inert mechanism. One behaviour change — the backup now fails closed. Tests **527 → 528**.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| LL | **`Benchmark.CapWeightProxySecurityId` was an undocumented config key.** `CapWeightProxy.ProxySecurityIdConfigKey` defines it, the D53 pipeline reads it, PROGRESS names writing it as an operator action — but CONFIG_REFERENCE had no `Benchmark` entry, while both stated precedents (`Regime.ProxySecurityId`, `Accounts.StartingCash`) are documented there. Rule 14 covers config keys, and CONFIG_REFERENCE is the registry that makes a key real: an operator following the docs could not learn the key's name, shape, or failure mode. | A `Benchmark` entry added following the `Regime.ProxySecurityId` pattern: the key exists ONLY as a versioned `config` row (MAX(version)) holding the resolved proxy ETF's security_id, written by the OPERATOR after ingesting the proxy as a real security (`OEF.US` under `oef_csv`; `IVV.US` after the D70 widening — `CapWeightProxy.SymbolFor`, never hardcoded), read every run by the D53 pipeline (the Stage-1 fetch set + the CW account's universe). ABSENT ⇒ the CW account holds cash — a logged readiness gap (rule 10), never a guessed symbol. | `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md` |
| MM | **`LocalBackup` failed OPEN on a busy checkpoint.** `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` returning busy≠0 was logged as a warning and the `File.Copy` proceeded anyway. A copy taken without a completed checkpoint can silently omit recently committed transactions, and a copy concurrent with a partially applied checkpoint can capture a torn main file. The Api running as a separate reader process is a SUPPORTED topology, so busy is reachable in normal operation — this was a silent-integrity-loss path, the exact class rule 10 exists for. | Fail closed: the checkpoint retries a small bounded number of times (3 attempts, ≤1s busy-wait each via `CommandTimeout`, 200ms between), and if still busy the backup ABORTS with a loud error and a failed `LocalBackupResult` (`FailureReason` set) — **no file is written**. A backup of unknown integrity is worse than a missing one with a visible error. The clean path (checkpoint → copy → per-day idempotency → filename-date pruning) is unchanged. Negative test: a second reader connection holds an open deferred read transaction mid-read (pinning the WAL, reproducing the separate-reader topology in-process), and the test asserts the result reports failure and the backups directory contains no file. | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/LocalBackup.cs`; `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/LocalBackupTests.cs` |
| NN | **The Stage-2 `StampHeartbeat()` was inert as a liveness signal, and its comment oversold it.** The write sits INSIDE the Stage-2 transaction — invisible to every other connection until commit, and at commit already stale by the transaction's duration. During a long Stage 2 the `HeartbeatService`'s own-connection beats block on the write lock too, so `heartbeat_at` effectively freezes at its pre-transaction value. The comment claimed "a long day stays 'live' rather than tripping a false stale-positive" — a guarantee the mechanism cannot provide. | The stamp is KEPT (a harmless post-commit refresh; removing it would change the Stage-2 commit contents for no gain) and the comments rewritten honest, at the call site, on the method, and in `HeartbeatService`'s class doc (which made the same claim from the other side): it is a post-commit refresh only and cannot prevent a false stale-positive during the transaction. The real protections within one day are named where the old claim stood: the run-open stamp (step 2's committed txn), the 5× `StaleRunThresholdSeconds` headroom over the Phase-3 <60s day target, and the 3× slow-transaction warning. No new liveness mechanism added. | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/DailyPipeline.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Heartbeat.cs` |

## v1.9.21 — the AI-role pass (decisions D79–D82; golden rule 32; findings 170–173)

*Resolves the operator's stated objective: a self-improving system where all decision inputs live in the local store and are passed to an AI that makes the decision, improving over time from the same stored data. AI is given three defined seats and the arena prices each one. Merged into `MASTER_DESIGN_v1.9.md` §2 (D79–D82), §1.2 (two KPIs), §7/§8/§14/§17, golden rule 32, and the full spec in the new §23. The honesty rails stay LLM-free; rule 24 extends to every prompt/model/pack-recipe change. Two new tables, one CHECK extension. UI overhaul consolidated into `alphalab_ux_mockups.html` (the single mockup file; the earlier per-topic and v2 files were removed). No change to the Phase 0–2 as-built ledger/funnel/data code; this is a design pass that scopes Phase 5–6.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 170 | **The self-improvement loop's generative step was entirely manual and unspecified.** Every document specified what happens *after* a candidate exists; nothing specified how verdicts, attribution, and closed outcomes produce the next candidate. For a system whose goal is self-improvement, the generator was a blank. | **D82** — the researcher seat: `POST /api/v1/analysis/hypotheses` (FR-23 resolved KEPT; `jobs.kind` CHECK gains `analysis_hypotheses`) reads locally stored evidence and proposes a **draft** pre-registered hypothesis; the operator remains the registrar (rule 30). Every proposal must cite parent evidence (422 without). `Research.ForkBudgetPerYear` (6) + `Research.MaxConcurrentCandidates` (3) ration the deflated-Sharpe spend. | MASTER §2 (D82), §23.4 |
| 171 | **AI's role had drifted to an accessory (D7 read-only), contradicting the intent that AI make decisions from stored data.** | **D79** — three seats: researcher (candidate generation), contestant (an LLM decision layer as a first-class `IModel` with a mandatory no-LLM twin), advisor (deferred). New **golden rule 32**: no AI output is an input to any component that judges AI outputs. | MASTER §2 (D79), §7, §16 (rule 32), §23.1 |
| 172 | **Passing decision inputs to the AI risked re-buying stored data as tokens, and had no determinism/audit story.** | **D80/D81** — the context-pack contract: packs are built locally through the versioned read services at (asOf, watermark), persisted to `ai_context_packs` with a hash; **raw series never enter a prompt** (derived features only, ~$0.01–0.03/trading day at Batches pricing with L0/L1 caching). The contestant's response persists to `ai_decisions` **before use** and every re-run replays the row (0 API calls) — determinism = f(inputs, watermark, seeds, stored AI outputs). Forward-only (D16): `IArenaReplay` rejects an AI seat by construction. Memory Option A (frozen lesson set) default; Option B (frozen update-rule R) permitted and measured against A. **No vector store** (§14.2 reaffirmed; sqlite-vec in-file only if ever needed). | MASTER §2 (D80–D81), §14, §23.2–23.3, §23.5 |
| 173 | **The design's self-declared "primary improvement mechanism" (the allocator) itself never faced falsification, and the version note / test counts across MASTER, README, MANIFEST, START_HERE had gone stale (falsely claiming no code exists; test count frozen at 237 vs actual 528).** | Two §1.2 KPIs added — **allocator value-add** (D51 blend vs static equal weight, paired NW-MDE, validated in Phase-4 replay against the D64 plants) and **researcher yield**. The four docs' stacked errata notes are collapsed to a single pointer to this CHANGELOG + `PROGRESS.md`; inline status/counts removed in favour of those two sources of truth. | MASTER §1.2; README/MANIFEST/START_HERE headers |

Proposals **P8–P11** opened in `PROGRESS.md`; the S&P-1500 widening-target proposal gains the Grinold-overclaim and compute-scale caveats. Landed as one commit via PR.*

## v1.9.25 — funnel cash constraint + basis math (decision D84; resolves findings 190 & 195)

*A code pass: fixes two Phase-2-review findings recorded in v1.9.23 whose deadline was "before Phase 3". **Behaviour change — Stage 5 now sizes new opens against available cash (D84). No schema, migration, or config-key change; tests 528 → 539; `ci.ps1` green.** Landed on `fix/finding-190-cash-constraint` via PR.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 190 ✔ | **RESOLVED (D84). Stage 5 sized new opens against total equity, not available cash** — an already-invested account could buy more than the cash it held (implicit leverage), and an `OpensOnly` book is never re-sized, so the overspend ratcheted across days (the RankBuffer case §13.6 warns of). Populations and the allocator size against equity too, so the artifact would have contaminated the calibration null bands; §23.6 named it the AI-contestant prerequisite | **D84** appended to MASTER §2. `Sizing.Size` gains a `decimal availableCash` ceiling: the capped equal-weight book is scaled DOWN proportionally so the total never exceeds available cash (near-zero cash ⇒ opens nothing); `SizingResult` gains `SpendableCash` so `UninvestedCash` is honest on opens-only days. `FunnelRunner` passes the account's **cash** for new opens and its **equity** for a whole-book rebalance (`ScheduledRebalance`/D68, which re-weights the whole book and self-funds); `FunnelInputs` gains `Cash`; `DailyPipeline` threads the already-computed cash. No held position is ever sold to fund an open (rule 7); the residual overnight-gap/cost overshoot is bounded + self-correcting (finding 196). Tests: `D84_*` (FunnelTests: spends-exactly-cash / near-zero-opens-nothing / cash-binds-with-the-cap; FunnelRunnerTests: opens-sized-to-cash-holds-untouched / accumulating-book-never-ratchets) + the pipeline no-structural-leverage guard; the B&H "enter once, never churn" acceptance is unchanged | MASTER §2 (D84), §6 (Stage 5), §0 provenance; `STRATEGY_CATALOG_v1.9.md` §9; `src/AlphaLab.Core/Funnel/Sizing.cs`, `FunnelRunner.cs`; `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/DailyPipeline.cs` |
| 195 ✔ | **RESOLVED. Basis math routed ledger money through a `double` ratio** — the sell-leg reduction scaled a decimal `CostBasis` by `(decimal)(newShares / existing.Shares)`, computing the ratio in binary floating point against D69 | The buy-add and proportional sell-reduce move to a pure decimal helper, `BasisMath` (`AddBuy` / `ReduceForSale`) — the ratio is `(decimal)newShares / (decimal)oldShares`, decimal end to end; `PostFill` calls it. Landed with 190's funnel work (the same Stage-5/PostFill surface). Test: `BasisMathTests` (thirds — where the double ratio visibly drifts — resolve exactly in decimal) | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Ledger/BasisMath.cs` (new); `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/DailyPipeline.cs` (PostFill); `tests/AlphaLab.Core.Tests/BasisMathTests.cs` |

Proposal **P12** opened in `PROGRESS.md` (the cash-shortfall allocation policy: scale-to-fit chosen as D84; the rank-order-fill alternative and the rejected sell-to-fund / per-trade-maturity-gate options recorded). The remaining Phase-2-review findings (191–194, 196–197) stay open with their deadlines.

## v1.9.26 — the no-LLM twin's Stage-2 scorer (decision D85; proposal P13)

*Records the scoring rule D81 left open: how the mandatory no-LLM twin produces a per-name Stage-2 score once the LLM call is removed. **Docs only — no code, schema, migration, config-key, or test change; tests stay 539; `ci.ps1` green.** Decision **D85** appended to MASTER §2 (refining D81); the rejected foils (pure equal-scoring; a tuned/selected simple rule) recorded as proposal **P13** in `PROGRESS.md`, mirroring the D84/P12 shape. Landed as one commit via PR.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 207 | **D81 mandates a mechanics-identical no-LLM twin and M.1 makes the contestant-minus-twin difference the headline "does the AI add value?" number — but D81 never defined the twin's Stage-2 scorer**, i.e. how it scores each shortlisted name once the LLM call is removed (`FX-TwinPairing` fixed everything *around* the score — pre-filter, breadth, sizing, exits, costs, seed — but not the score). A Phase-6 implementer would have had to invent it, and that choice silently sets what the whole A/B measures: pure equal-scoring is too weak a bar ("the AI beat scoring everything evenly"), a tuned/selected rule is the strongest foil but riggable after results are seen (p-hacking) | **D85** fixes the scorer as pre-registered contestant policy: a **frozen equal-weight z-score blend of the same pack features the contestant is shown** — each feature cross-sectionally standardized across the day's shortlist at the watermark, then equal-weight averaged, no tuning, no free params, sign conventions fixed in the recipe; deterministic and pure (F-DET, no API call); a `config_json` frozen param (rule 24/32 — a change forks a new candidate); identical *form* across contestants with the feature set following `Ai.PackRecipeVersion` (a richer pack ⇒ a correspondingly richer naive baseline); **ordered degenerate-day handling** — drop zero-variance features, then an equal-score fallback + `degenerate_blend` flag if no nonzero-variance feature remains or the shortlist has <2 names (covering the <2-name day, the all-features-flat multi-name day, and the empty-feature-set edge alike), never a divide-by-zero or NaN; and not replay-admissible on its own (a `status='control'` twin paired to a replay-refused contestant, existing only to price the seat forward). The rejected alternatives (pure equal-scoring; a tuned/selected simple rule) are recorded as **P13**. Isolates the LLM's *weighting skill* while holding information constant | MASTER §2 (D85), §23.3 (rules 2/4), §0 provenance + line-5 banner (D1–D85); `STRATEGY_CATALOG_v1.9.md` §9; `CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md` (Ai NOTE); `TEST_PLAN_v1.9.md` §6 (`D85_*` fixtures + extended `FX-TwinPairing`); `PROGRESS.md` (P13 + status line + session log); `ORIENTATION.md` (Separation-3 twin section already names the blend); `CLAUDE.md` / `MANIFEST.md` D-range roll |

## v1.9.34 - the cohort maturation curve KPI (D88)

*Docs-only pass; **a D-numbered decision (D88), not additive-only**; no table change and no migration applied - one additive covering index recorded in SCHEMA for the Phase-3 build; two additive config keys (`Kpi.CohortBucketMonths`, `Kpi.CohortMinStrategies`); no code change; tests stay 551, plus the newly specified `FX-CohortCurve` fixture (builds in Phase 3 with the other D58 read-models); `ci.ps1` green. Adds the cohort maturation curve as a lab-level §1.2 KPI: admission-vintage cohorts' median D36 population percentile vs track length, age-aligned, no survivorship, descriptive only. A standalone reference panel ships at `docs/mockups/cohort_curve_panel.html` (the packed `alphalab_ux_mockups.html` is untouched; it absorbs the panel when the UI workstream regenerates it, D65). Landed as one commit via PR.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 216 | **The lab graded the researcher loop on yield (§1.2 Researcher yield) but had no way to answer "is the loop learning what to test, or just recombining without gaining?"** - no diagnostic compared later admission cohorts to earlier ones at equal maturity, and any naive version of one would flatter the loop (survivorship from dropped retirees, wall-clock instead of age alignment, sub-MDE gaps read as progress, plant cohorts posing as forward evidence) | Decision **D88**: the cohort maturation curve - promotable strategies grouped by admission vintage (`strategies.created_on` bucketed by `Kpi.CohortBucketMonths`, default 6; optional fork-generation grouping via `parent_strategy_id`), each cohort's MEDIAN D36 population percentile (the S3 source, reused verbatim - never a second percentile) plotted against track length t in trading days, age-aligned. Rails resolved into the `CohortMaturationReadModel` (FR-39, MASTER §22, Phase 3): retired members retained (no survivorship); thin cohorts (`Kpi.CohortMinStrategies`, default 3) and sub-MDE cohort gaps dimmed via the D58 `{value, display, reason}` shape; replay cohorts `quarantined:true`, never co-plotted (D37, rule 26); arena-scoped (D71); **descriptive only - never a gate, monitor, or allocator input** (both load-bearing rails named in the D88 rationale). New §1.2 KPI row; Analysis/Journal panel (UX-15 + `CohortCurvePanel`, PlannedBadge until Phase 3); fixture `FX-CohortCurve` (a-e assertions); `Kpi.*` config group; SCHEMA storage-neutral note + additive covering index `ix_overfitting_checks_path` (the S3 percentile path was not queryable by `(strategy_id, as_of)`; migration lands with Phase 3). **Recorded, NOT built** - the read-model ships in Phase 3, a synthetic replay version is exercised at Phase-4 calibration against the D64 plant cohorts, and the curve is meaningful only once several forward cohorts accrue track length | MASTER §1.2 + §2 (D88 + provenance + line-5 banner) + §15 + §22, `BUILD_AND_PROMPTS_v1.9.md` §1 (FR-39) + §2 Phase-3 row + §4 Phase-3 prompt, `SCHEMA_v1.9.md` (index + enforcement note), `CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md` (`Kpi.*` + rule 7), `UX_GUIDELINES_v1.9.md` (UX-15 + roster + phase wiring), `UX_DESIGN_SYSTEM_v1.9.md` (component 17 + roster), `TEST_PLAN_v1.9.md` §5 (`FX-CohortCurve`) + §8, `docs/mockups/cohort_curve_panel.html` (new), `PROGRESS.md`, `ORIENTATION.md`, `MANIFEST.md`/`README_v1.9.md`/`README.md`/`CLAUDE.md` D-range banners + FR/UX rosters |

Landed as one commit via PR.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
