## v1.9.36 - OS-agnostic DB path resolution (the cloud lift-and-shift seam)

*Code + config + docs; **no D-number** (a resolver fix and a config-value edit, not a decision); no schema change, no migration, no new config key; **no relocation of the live store**; tests 713 -> **720** (seven new resolver cases); `ci.ps1` green. Makes the SQLite store's location drive- AND OS-agnostic so the lab lift-and-shifts to a cloud (Linux) VM as a config-value change with **zero code change at migration time**. Deliberately ships the mechanism and defers the move: the base stays `E:`, and `docs/DB_RELOCATION.md` §5 remains the procedure for moving it. Landed as one commit via PR.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 218 | **The connection-string template was Windows-only in the literal sense, so the documented "relocation is a config edit" promise did not extend across operating systems.** `DbPathResolver.ResolvePath` was pure token substitution and never touched separators, while all four byte-identical spots carried `Data Source=E:\AlphaLabDatabase\{Arena.Id}\alphalab.db`. On Linux that value is not a path at all - it is a SINGLE filename containing backslashes - so a cloud move was "edit the value **and** hope nothing downstream did string surgery on separators", and the failure mode is the silent one hard rule 10 exists to prevent: a fresh EMPTY database created beside the real one, with no error. The `{LocalAppData}` token did not help, because the expanded known-folder contributes the host platform's separator while the rest of the template keeps whatever the author typed - a guaranteed mixed-separator string | **Two-part fix, no new decision.** (1) **Resolver (load-bearing):** after token substitution `ResolvePath` rebuilds the `Data Source` through `SqliteConnectionStringBuilder` with `Path.DirectorySeparatorChar`, so a Windows `\` never survives on Linux and a `/` never survives on Windows - ONE config string is valid on every OS. Still D67-safe (known-folders API only; no `GetEnvironmentVariable`/`ExpandEnvironmentVariables`, no env-var read of any kind), still D71-safe (the `{Arena.Id}` token is untouched and still what isolates arenas), and still **PURE** - string work only, no filesystem access, so `ResolvePath_IsPure_DoesNotTouchTheFilesystem` holds and the snapshot-first discipline (rule 14) is unaffected. Six new tests pin the contract (TEST_PLAN §8), led by `FR37_ResolvePath_NormalizesSeparatorsToTheRunningOs`, which asserts on the **`ResolvePath` output** against a template mixing BOTH separators. **Self-review correction (this pass):** the first version of that test asserted on `GetDataSourcePath`'s output using a forward-slash-only template and was **vacuous** - `Path.GetFullPath` normalizes `/`→`\` on Windows by itself, and `/` is native on Linux, so it passed with the production normalization fully deleted (verified by mutation: removing the builder block left 15/15 green). The load-bearing change had ZERO coverage; the rewrite now fails under that same mutation. Second fix from the same review: **nothing enforced that the resolved store path is ABSOLUTE.** Normalization makes one string *parse* everywhere but does not make `E:` meaningful on POSIX, so deploying the committed value to Linux unedited would have rooted it at each process's CWD - the Worker, the Api, and the Backfill CLI opening three different, freshly-created, EMPTY databases with no error, i.e. precisely the silent failure this pass claims to remove (DB_RELOCATION §1 already forbade a relative path; nothing checked). New `DbPathResolver.RequireAbsoluteStorePath` fails closed (rule 10) at both composition roots - `Resolve` (writers + the design-time factory, refusing BEFORE the directory is created) and `AddAlphaLabData` (readers, so the Api is covered on the same terms) - and `BackfillPreflight` reports it as a Fail reason. A `ubuntu-latest` CI leg now runs the suite, because asserting an OS-agnostic claim only on `windows-latest` proves nothing about the half that matters. (2) **Config:** the four byte-identical spots move together (`DbPathResolver.DefaultConnectionString` + the Worker/Api/Backfill `ConnectionStrings:AlphaLab`) to `Data Source=E:/AlphaLabDatabase/{Arena.Id}/alphalab.db`. **The live store does NOT move:** on Windows that template normalizes to `E:\AlphaLabDatabase\sp500\alphalab.db`, byte-identical to the previously resolved path (verified against the real assembly - resolved path matches and the file exists), so there is no window in which a run could create an empty store at a new path, and no snapshot/move was required. The `{LocalAppData}` token form and a POSIX base (`/var/lib/alphalab/...`) remain the sanctioned options for whenever the base itself moves, per the unchanged `DB_RELOCATION.md` §5 procedure. `tools/Resolve-AlphaLabConnection.ps1` needs no mirror change - PowerShell's `Test-Path`/`Split-Path`/`Join-Path`/`Copy-Item` accept mixed separators on Windows, verified by running `snapshot-db.ps1` against the live arena. The four-spots byte-identity rule, `ConfigConsistencyTests`, and the `Arena:Id` cross-process guard are all unchanged | `src/AlphaLab.Data/DbPathResolver.cs` (`ResolvePath` normalization + `DefaultConnectionString` + class/const xml comments), `src/AlphaLab.Data/AlphaLabDbContextFactory.cs` (xml comment), `src/AlphaLab.Worker/appsettings.json`, `src/AlphaLab.Api/appsettings.json`, `tools/Backfill/appsettings.json`, `tests/AlphaLab.Data.Tests/DbPathResolverTests.cs` (new case), `CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md` (`ConnectionStrings` note + the OS-agnostic-separators rule), `DB_RELOCATION.md` (§1 separator paragraph + the cloud-VM payoff, §6 cheat-sheet), `BUILD_AND_PROMPTS_v1.9.md` §1 (FR-37) + §4 checkpoint 0.3 (so a from-scratch rebuild is born on the normalized form), `ARENA_ARCHITECTURE_v1.9.3.md` §3.1/§3.2, `README_v1.9.md`, `REBUILD.md`, `RUNBOOK_v1.9.md`, `MANIFEST.md`, `README.md`, `PROGRESS.md` |

## v1.9.37 - Phase 3.5: save/continue hardening (D90; FR-25)

*Code + scripts + docs; **one new decision (D90)**; one EF migration (`Phase35PositionSnapshots`, operator step **M4**); no new config key; tests 721 -> **754**; `ci.ps1` green. Completes the checkpoint skipped between Phase 3 and Phase 4: NFR-1 becomes an executable proof rather than a claim, WAL is verified end to end, the off-machine backup leg is scripted, and the RUNBOOK §4 restore drill is automated. `LocalBackup` (2.12), `tools/snapshot-db.ps1` and `tools/Resolve-AlphaLabConnection.ps1` are REUSED, not rebuilt. Branch `feat/phase3.5-save-continue`, one PR.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 219 | **NFR-1 was asserted everywhere and proved nowhere.** MASTER §13.5 line 413 claims "Determinism (NFR1) = f(inputs, watermark, seeds). Any historical run is reproducible forever against exactly the data it saw", and the pieces were individually tested — the watermarked bar read (`FR2_PinnedToMidWatermark_ReproducesV1_ByteIdentically`), the watermarked corporate-action read (`D76_PinnedToMidWatermark_...`), the funnel's own determinism (`FDET_TwoRunsOfTheSameDay_...`). Nothing re-ran a whole committed **day** and compared it to what was committed, so the composed claim — the one the phase gate rests on — had never been exercised | **`reproduce-day --date <yyyy-MM-dd> [--arena]`** (`ReproduceDayRunner`), which re-runs a committed session from its STORED watermark into a rewound throwaway copy and compares decisions, fills, equity points and population draws byte for byte. **Read-only against the arena (D59):** the copy is taken with SQLite's online-backup API from a `Mode=ReadOnly` connection, so no pragma, no checkpoint, and no possibility of a write to the live file. **No network:** `StoredMarketDataProvider`/`StoredRegimeProxyProvider` replay Stage 1 from the store's own versioned bars + corporate actions at that watermark, so the run needs no EODHD token and a live feed's later revision cannot leak into a pinned re-run. Substituting the two PROVIDER interfaces (rather than cutting a seam into `DailyPipeline`) means the orchestrator, Stage 1, the FR-6 gate, the funnel, the ledger and the populations all execute unmodified. The compared set excludes wall-clock columns (`runs.started_at`/`finished_at`, `worker_state.heartbeat_at`), which are SUPPOSED to differ between two runs of the same day, and quality flags, which Stage 1 legitimately re-drops on already-gated dates | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Ops/ReproduceDay.cs`, `src/AlphaLab.Data/Providers/StoredHistoryProviders.cs`, `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/ReproduceDayTests.cs` (`FX-ReproduceDay`), TEST_PLAN §3/§8, MASTER §13.5 |
| 220 | **The pre-day book was unrecoverable, which is why the reproduction claim could not have been honoured for anything the ledger touches.** Every other input already obeys the as-of discipline — bars (D40), corporate actions (D76), context packs (D80) are versioned append-only and read at a watermark. `positions` is the exception: it is current STATE, and `CorporateActionApplier` rewrites it in place through `UpsertPosition` for splits, mergers and spin-offs with **no reversible trade row**, so the book at the start of a past session cannot be folded back out of `trades`. Neither `positions` (current) nor `equity_curve` (a scalar) holds it. The gap was invisible because nothing had yet tried to re-run a past day | **D90 — `position_snapshots`:** the end-of-day book per (account, session, security, run_kind), written in the SAME Stage-2 transaction that already writes `equity_curve`, from the `held` list the pipeline has in hand after corporate actions and T+1 fills (the funnel does not mutate positions, so that list IS the closing book). Column set mirrors `positions` plus the snapshot's keys; `run_kind` is IN the PK so a replay book cannot overwrite the forward one (the `equity_curve` precedent, D37). Idempotent per day: a re-run rewrites that day's rows wholesale rather than upserting, so a name that left the book does not survive as a phantom holding. **`frozen`/`frozen_reason` are load-bearing, not decoration** — `PortfolioPlanner` short-circuits Stage 4 on a frozen position and interpolates the reason VERBATIM into `decisions.stage_json`, so a lossy snapshot would make a frozen-position day un-reproducible; `FR25_ReproduceDay_FrozenPositionDay_IsByteIdentical` pins it (and caught exactly that during the build, when the first version of the fixture froze a position out-of-band). Timing was the argument for doing it now: `runs`/`accounts`/`strategies` are still empty on the live arena, so there is **zero forward history to orphan** | MASTER §2 (D90) + §13.5, `SCHEMA_v1.9.md` (`position_snapshots`), `src/AlphaLab.Data/Entities/LedgerEntities.cs`, `src/AlphaLab.Data/AlphaLabDbContext.cs`, `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/LedgerStore.cs`, `src/AlphaLab.Data/Migrations/*_Phase35PositionSnapshots.cs`, `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/DailyPipeline.cs`, `tests/AlphaLab.Data.Tests/LedgerStoreTests.cs` (`D90_PositionSnapshot_*`) |
| 221 | **A rewind that missed a table would make `reproduce-day` lie.** If any table holding the target day's output survived the rewind, the re-run would upsert onto it and the comparison would report a byte-identical match having proved nothing — a green tick that means the opposite of what it says, and the only failure mode that makes a determinism proof actively harmful | **Fail closed (rule 10) plus a build-time totality guard.** `ScratchStoreGuard.Assert` re-reads every rewound table after the rewind and throws, naming the survivors, if any at-or-after row remains; it is a separate type from the rewind so the check cannot share a bug with the thing it checks. `ScratchStoreClassificationTests` then asserts the classification is **total over the EF model** — every table is declared rewound, untouched-with-a-stated-reason, or specially handled — so a future migration reddens until the new table is classified rather than being silently carried across. **`bars` and `corporate_actions` are deliberately NOT rewound:** the watermark already hides later-observed versions at read time, which is the whole point of D40/D76, and not touching them keeps hard rule 3 and the `ci.ps1` `DELETE FROM bars` guard true by construction rather than by discipline | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Ops/ScratchStore.cs`, `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/ScratchStoreClassificationTests.cs` |
| 222 | **The reproduce path could have drifted from the real pipeline.** A hand-assembled DI graph for the re-run would diverge the first time a config bind was added to `Program.cs` and forgotten — and the reproduction would then quietly compare a DIFFERENT pipeline against the committed one, still reporting pass/fail with total confidence | **`PipelineComposition.AddDailyPipelineCore`** — one shared composition (options binds, data, membership, Stage 1, the orchestrator) used by BOTH the forward Worker and the reproduce runner, so sharing is structural rather than a convention someone maintains. The two axes the hosts must differ on are exactly the two the helper leaves to the caller: the market-data/regime providers and the `TimeProvider` | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/PipelineComposition.cs`, `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Program.cs` |
| 223 | **WAL was set but its checkpoint was never proved.** `SchemaStartup` runs `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` and fails the host if the mode does not read back as `wal` (finding 118) — but nothing proved a checkpoint on the live store actually COMPLETES, which is precisely the assumption `LocalBackup` rests on when it folds the WAL into the main file so a plain file copy is a consistent snapshot | **`verify-wal [--arena]`** + `WalVerification`: reads `PRAGMA journal_mode` (never sets it — a verifier that repaired what it checks could never report the defect it exists to find, and `FR25_VerifyWal_NeverSetsTheMode` pins that), then runs `wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE)` and asserts busy=0, exiting non-zero with a named reason otherwise. PASSIVE rather than TRUNCATE: it is the honest probe, never blocks behind another process's read, and does not compete with the backup step's own checkpoint. **Verified on the live arena** (`journal_mode=wal`, checkpoint completed, exit 0) | `src/AlphaLab.Data/WalVerification.cs`, `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Ops/OpsCommandHost.cs`, `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/WalVerificationTests.cs`, RUNBOOK §3 |
| 224 | **The off-machine backup leg was unscripted, and a day with no manual launch produced no backup at all.** PROGRESS has carried "the off-machine copy is a manual operator action, NOW DUE" since checkpoint 2.10; `LocalBackup` is a same-drive convenience snapshot, so a disk failure took the local copies with the store | **Two operator scripts that touch no database.** `tools/backup-offsite.ps1 -Arena -Destination [-BackupDirectory]` copies the newest backup **by the date in the filename** — the same rule `LocalBackup`'s retention prune uses, deliberately not mtime, which a copy or a restore drill rewrites — then VERIFIES by size + SHA-256 and prints the hash. It fails loudly on a blank/missing destination, a missing backups directory, no backup files, or a hash mismatch (rule 10: an off-site routine that silently does nothing is worse than none, because it also removes the operator's reason to check). `tools/register-nightly-backup.ps1 -Arena [-Time] [-Install]` emits the exact `Register-ScheduledTask` commands for an 02:00 daily task and registers nothing without `-Install`. **Verified against the live arena:** the off-site copy of `alphalab-2026-07-19.db` (95,420,416 bytes) verified SHA-256 clean | `tools/backup-offsite.ps1`, `tools/register-nightly-backup.ps1`, `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/OpsScriptTests.cs`, RUNBOOK §3 |
| 225 | **The nightly task cannot be a `--serve` launch, contrary to the phase brief's own wording.** The obvious reading of "schedule a nightly backup" is a resident Worker in Scheduled mode (D61). That does not work: Scheduled mode registers Quartz with **zero jobs**, so a resident Worker starts, heartbeats, and idles forever — never catching up, never backing up. Emitting that task would have produced a scheduled job that looks correct in Task Scheduler and silently does nothing | **The emitted task is an OnDemand launch** (`dotnet run --project src/AlphaLab.Worker`, no `--serve`), which drives the fully-built D72 order — stale-run recovery → catch-up → job drain → `LocalBackup` → exit — with zero code change. Safe nightly: `LocalBackup` is idempotent per calendar day and catch-up is a no-op once current. `FR25_RegisterNightly_*` asserts the absence of `--serve` in the emitted action as a load-bearing negative. The missing Quartz Scheduled-mode daily job is recorded as a Phase-2/D61 leftover (PROGRESS), explicitly OUT of 3.5 scope and low priority now that 02:00 OnDemand covers the need. Second defect found while verifying: the first version emitted `schtasks.exe /Create /TR "cmd /c cd /d "<repo>" && dotnet run ..."`, whose **nested quotes terminate the outer `/TR` argument** — and this repo's path contains spaces. Switched to `Register-ScheduledTask`, which takes `-WorkingDirectory` as its own parameter so the path never survives a second round of quoting; the script also CONSTRUCTS the action + trigger objects (registering nothing) so "well-formed" is validated rather than asserted about a string | `tools/register-nightly-backup.ps1`, RUNBOOK §3 |
| 226 | **The RUNBOOK §4 restore drill had never been rehearsed or tested**, and §4's own words are "an unrehearsed backup is a hope, not a plan" | **`FX-RestoreThenContinue`**: restores a real per-launch backup over a real store and asserts the post-backup session is discarded, the D47 resolver reports it missed, catch-up replays it, `runs` is gapless, the equity curve matches its pre-restore shape, `position_snapshots` is rebuilt (so the restored store is reproducible again), and a second launch is a no-op. RUNBOOK §4 gains the **`-wal`/`-shm` sidecar deletion** the procedure had omitted — the backup is post-checkpoint and complete, so a surviving newer WAL would replay exactly the transactions the restore is discarding — plus the D90 verification step and a pointer to `verify-wal`/`reproduce-day` afterwards. The operator rehearsal stays: the test proves the PATH, only a real drill proves the operator's backup files are readable on the operator's disk | `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/RestoreThenContinueTests.cs`, RUNBOOK §4, TEST_PLAN §3/§8 |
| 227 | **A mistyped ops verb would have started the sole DB writer against the live arena.** The verbs need config but must not run `SchemaStartup` (which SETS `journal_mode` — a verifier must not repair what it is checking), the heartbeat, or the OnDemand runner | **`WorkerCommandParser` + a dispatch that runs BEFORE any hosted service is registered.** An unknown verb throws rather than falling through to the daily launch, and the parser rejects a malformed or missing `--date`. Both verbs run outside the Generic Host entirely, so "look, do not touch" is structural rather than a matter of registration order | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/WorkerCommand.cs`, `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Ops/OpsCommandHost.cs`, `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Program.cs`, `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/WorkerCommandParserTests.cs` |
| 228 | **Two as-of gaps remain, recorded rather than silently accepted.** (a) `config` rows resolve by `MAX(version)` with no watermark filter, and `strategies.status` is current state, so a change to either after a reproduced session is visible to its re-run. (b) An out-of-band mutation to `positions` between two sessions — which the Phase-7 D55 admin-intervention panel will make possible — is not in the D90 as-of chain, so the following day would diverge | Neither can move the compared set: the funnel reads strategy PLANS not status, and the population engine draws from `(familySeed, memberIndex, gridOrdinal)` alone; they can only change which post-commit EVALUATION path runs, which is why the comparison excludes it. Both are documented in `ScratchStore`'s class comment and opened as **proposal P14** (as-of config resolution + admin actions in the reproducibility chain). Not fixed here: as-of config resolution is a schema-and-read-path change that belongs with the phase that needs it, and the admin panel does not exist yet | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Ops/ScratchStore.cs`, `PROGRESS.md` (P14) |

## v1.9.38 - the Signal Library registered as Phase 4.5 (D91; FR-43..46)

*Docs only; **one new decision (D91)**; no code change; no migration applied (two additive tables, `signals` + `signal_ic`, recorded in SCHEMA for the Phase-4.5 build); four new FRs specified, not built (FR-43..46) with their fixtures named (`FX-SignalScorers`, `FX-SignalIcDeterminism`, `FX-SignalIcPit`, `FX-SignalBackfillIdempotent`, `FX-SignalParity`, `FX-SignalPanel`); one new config block (`SignalLibrary`: key names registered, z-threshold values pinned at build checkpoint 4.5.2); proposal **P15** opened; tests stay 754. The two post-Phase-8 roadmap docs join the tracked corpus in the same pass. Landed as one PR, two commits (pass + PROGRESS maintenance).*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|

## v1.9.39 — the Phase-4 build (Arena Replay): decisions D92–D99, findings 240–247

*The first CODE version stamp in this file: checkpoints 4.1–4.10 shipped on `feat/phase4-arena-replay` (754 → 819 tests; migration M5). Resolves the standing pre-Phase-4 register: findings 192/193/194 fixed (4.1), proposals P5 → **D94**, P6 → **D93**, P10 → **D92**, P14a → **D96**, and P7's replay-side half → **D97** (the persisted-flags sweep of the forward slice stays open under P7). FR-40 actioned as **D99**; the replay watermark contract and curve-row format are **D95**/**D98**.*

| # | Finding (build-discovered) | Resolution | Where |
|---|----------------------------|-----------|-------|
| 240 | **EF's SQLite table REBUILD resurrects the AUTOINCREMENT the rule-14 hand-edit stripped**: M5's `DropColumn(processed_on)` would have regenerated `corporate_actions` with `action_id … AUTOINCREMENT` (the no-autoincrement guard caught it) | The drop is a hand-edited plain `ALTER TABLE … DROP COLUMN` (no rebuild; the column is unindexed/unconstrained), behind the D94 fail-loud precondition | M5 migration; `Schema_IntegerPrimaryKeys_HaveNoAutoincrement` |
| 241 | **`HistoricalMembershipIngestion` was not re-run idempotent**: blind `AddRange` double-inserted the `(security_id, added_on)` PK on any re-run of the same CSV | Upsert by key; a longer CSV closes a previously-open interval; a same-CSV re-run writes nothing | `HistoricalMembershipIngestion.Ingest`; `D97_GateSweep_RerunReproducesIdenticalExclusionList` |
| 242 | **Replay promotion/retire would have mutated the SHARED `strategies.status`** — a replay row reaching forward state ("replay is never a promotion input"): EvaluationStep's Promoted set it 'live', the monitor's auto-retire set it 'retired' | `EffectiveStatus`: run-kind-scoped status — forward reads the column verbatim; replay DERIVES promote/retire from its own quarantined go_live_log/overfitting_status rows and never mutates the column; all four consumers (gate, monitor, allocator, turnover match) switched | `EffectiveStatus.cs`; checkpoint 4.5 |
| 243 | **The planned SimulatedClock was a liveness hazard**: a simulated-time heartbeat reads STALE to a concurrent launch, whose stale-run recovery would clear `run_in_progress` and start the sole writer against a mid-write replay (FR-34's exact double-writer) | Replay runs on the REAL clock — no replay output derives from the clock (the watermark is explicit, artifacts are dated by as_of); honest wall-clock provenance on replay run rows is a feature (recorded in D95) | `ReplayRunner` class doc |
| 244 | **`Ingest`/plants/pipeline day loop would have warned ~350×/day on plant accounts** (unknown-strategy skip) and forward read-models would have listed plants as candidate strategies | Plants skip the account-day loop silently by design (equity-only fixtures) and are excluded from the forward Strategies leaderboard; their exposure is /replay only | `DailyPipeline`; `StrategiesReadModelBuilder` |
| 245 | **A verification suite that only ever passes proves nothing — and one that hollow-greens unevaluable checks lies**: the CI-mini window cannot evaluate a 5-year survival floor | Tri-state checks (Pass/Fail/**Insufficient**): the full-scale DoD bar is AllGreen, the CI bar is NoFailures; Insufficient is visible in the report, never green | `ReplayVerification`; the report's check table |
| 246 | **The learn-period P_noise breach rate is true by construction** (the curve IS that quantile), so asserting it there would be circular | The breach-rate check reads the VALIDATE segment only (FR-42's other side) — out-of-sample honesty; Insufficient without a partition | `ReplayVerification.noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` |
| 247 | **The allocator value-add behavioral judgment below `Gate.MinTrackDays` is noise wearing a verdict** (TooEarly-capped weights are near-equal by design) | The check reads Insufficient below the gate's own minimum track; the KPI numbers are still recorded | `ReplayVerification.allocator_value_add` |

**Also in this version:** ORIENTATION gains the top-level section **"How the whole system runs, in three pictures"** (one trading evening → telling real from lucky → the sealed room that sets the pass marks, three mermaid flowcharts on the six existing house classDefs — no new classDef), placed between "Under the hood" and "The signal library" (the staging file was folded in verbatim and removed; ONE repair to the source content, adversarially caught: Picture 2's "Joins the field" node referenced `:::trade` with no in-block `classDef trade` — mermaid classDefs do not carry across fences, so the node would have silently lost the house green on GitHub; the block gains the house `trade` classDef line, byte-identical to its definition in Pictures 1 and 3); SCHEMA's two "lands with the Phase-4 build" notes flipped to LANDED (M5); the D93 regime DDL + the D94 processed_on resolution note; CONFIG_REFERENCE gains the D98 key names + the D70-backfill rows; OVERFITTING_MONITOR Appendix A's `"calibrated:phase4"` placeholders resolve to the D98 keys; RUNBOOK gains the `replay-calibrate` operator chain; REBUILD gains the historical-backfill step. *A 10-angle multi-agent review of the Phase-4 PR (each finding independently adversarially verified against the code) surfaced 15 confirmed defects; all fixed on the same branch before merge, each with a regression test. The register below records the finding AND the resolution — the provenance trace the review exists to leave.*

| 248 | **A resumed replay could REWRITE forward-priced history**: IngestStaged re-stages watermark-resolved values, and the ingestion diff compared against the latest-OVERALL version — a post-watermark correction made the replay append its stale values as a new top version with a BACKDATED observed_at, silently shadowing the correction for every later-watermark read (rules 1+3), unhealable under append-only | The no-backdate guard: a differing observation whose observed_at is older than the store's newest for that identity is a no-op (bars + corporate actions); a replay day with a null watermark now throws instead of fabricating the session fiction | `BarIngestionService.IngestEod`; `CorporateActionIngestion.AppendIfNew`; `DailyPipeline.RunDayAsync`; `FR2_BackdatedDifferingReingest_*`, `D76_BackdatedDifferingReingest_*` |
| 249 | **The same-launch watermark inversion**: an evening launch (≥22:00 UTC — the NORMAL case) that recovered missed days ingested them at NowIso, then ran today 'live' at `{asOf}T22:00:00Z` — which sorts BEFORE those instants, blinding today's run to the rows this very launch wrote; a dividend applicable only in today's (prev, asOf] window was silently never credited | CatchupRunner passes today's TRUE instant as the explicit watermark whenever the same launch recovered earlier days; a pure-live launch keeps the D92 fiction | `CatchupRunner`; `FX_CatchupObservedAt_RecoveredDaysCarryTrueObservedAt` (extended with the visibility assertion) |
| 250 | **reproduce-day double-credited weekend-effective actions**: the widened (prev, asOf] window (finding 192) dates cash events/forced trades at the ACTION's non-session date; the `AsOf >= asOf` rewind kept them, the re-run re-applied — a permanent false NFR-1 divergence | The rewind (and its guard) reaches back across the non-session gap: trades + cash events delete on `> previousSession` (consecutive sessions partition the date line, so exactly the target day's output goes) | `ScratchStore.Rewind`/`ScratchStoreGuard`; `FR25_ScratchStore_RewindDeletesGapDatedCashEvents_*` |
| 251 | **A FAILED calibration still froze its curves**: the config freeze committed BEFORE the verification.NoFailures check — the next forward run's monitor would resolve the poisoned rows, and append-only config means no removal | The freeze is gated on the verification outcome; a hard FAIL archives the report (evidence) and exits 1 with NOTHING frozen; `frozenKeys` now tells the truth in the report | `CalibrationOrchestrator`; `FX_Calibration_VerificationFailure_ArchivesReportButFreezesNothing` |

## v1.9.41 — the pre-4.11 data-quality pass: SUN excluded, BSC cleared (findings 266-267, proposal P16)

*Reviewing the live D70 historical-backfill coverage artifact (`docs/calibration/sp500/historical-coverage-2006-01-01-2026-01-01.json`) surfaced a low-coverage tail: 918 members ingested, of which **76 are zero-coverage** (bars stored only OUTSIDE the membership window — inert, since replay reads a security's bars only during its membership days) and **16 are partial-coverage** (0–90%). Two single-spell members whose gap matched symbol reuse the >2y disjoint-spell heuristic structurally cannot see were investigated read-only against the store. Verdicts: **SUN — WRONG-COMPANY** (old Sunoco the refiner, S&P 500 1996-01-02 → 2012-10-05, but the stored bars start 2012-09-20 at close ~$23 on low volume = Sunoco LP, the Energy-Transfer MLP that reused the ticker; old Sunoco traded ~$48-50 pending its ~$50 buyout, so the 11 in-window sessions are the wrong company); **BSC — GENUINE-PARTIAL, CLEARED** (the 53 in-window sessions are REAL Bear Stearns' collapse: $4.81 on 2008-03-17 → $10.94 by 2008-03-25 on 166M/136M-share days, tracking the JPMorgan buyout, last heavy bar 2008-05-30; the reused-ticker tail — ~$11 at 150-3,500 shares/day — is entirely after the 2008-06-02 removal and never read in replay, so excluding it would have wrongly dropped a real 2008-crisis member). FOXA/FOX/DOW/IR and the other partials are correct for the period they cover (post-restructure survivors, pre-event years absent = fail-closed) and are kept. **Only SUN is excluded (N=1 of 16 partial).** Chosen remediation (over a rule-3-forbidden bar delete and over a ~2 hr restore+re-backfill purge): the exclusion list + a replay deny-list — the wrong bars stay, inert, because the security leaves the replay roster.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 266 | **The ticker-reuse heuristic catches disjoint MEMBERSHIP spells but not single-spell symbol reuse**: `ReuseGapYears` returns null for a member with one spell, so a delisted name whose EODHD symbol was later reused by a DIFFERENT company (a single continuous listing) passes unflagged and ingests the wrong company's in-window bars; the in-window bar-coverage % is the signal that surfaces it but drives no exclusion (SUN: 0.7% — the reused Sunoco-LP prices attributed to old Sunoco's 2012 membership tail) | An operator exclusion list `Universe:Exclusions` with ONE list and TWO consumers: the historical backfill SKIPS the symbols on ingest (skip-and-record before any fetch → new `OperatorExclusions` report list, exactly like a ticker-reuse suspect) so a re-run reproduces the exclusion, and the replay composition DENIES them from the roster (`ExclusionScopedMembershipRead` at the replay-only membership registration — the rule-3-compliant substitute for deleting the already-ingested bars: the security leaves the roster, its bars stay inert). The two lists are locked to agreement across the Worker + Backfill appsettings. SUN listed; BSC investigated and CLEARED. The general coverage-%-threshold exclusion is deferred to **P16**. | `src/AlphaLab.Data/UniverseOptions.cs` (`Exclusions`); `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/HistoricalBackfill.cs:228-234,347-359` (heuristic), the ingest skip + `OperatorExclusion` + `ExclusionScopedMembershipRead`; `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Ops/ReplayRunner.cs` (replay registration); `tools/Backfill/Program.cs`; `Universe:Exclusions` in both appsettings + CONFIG_REFERENCE; `Finding266_OperatorExclusion_*`, `Finding266_ExclusionScopedMembershipRead_*`, `Config_UniverseExclusions_AgreeAcrossConsumers` |
| 267 | **The ops `LoggerFactory` never applied the EF `Warning` filter** (operator-hit during the §8.3 sign-off run): `Program.cs`'s standalone ops-command `LoggerFactory.Create(...)` — used by `replay-calibrate` / `reproduce-day` / `verify-wal` — carries no level filter, so `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Database.Command` logs every SQL at Information; the appsettings `"Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore":"Warning"` only reaches the resident-host `builder.Logging` path, not this factory. A full-scale replay-calibrate (~5031 sessions × ~900 names × several reads) projected **~20 GB** of per-query log noise and paid the log-formatting cost on every read (the ~2 sessions/min pace was measured with it on) | `AddFilter("Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore", LogLevel.Warning)` on the ops factory, mirroring the appsettings the resident host already honors — benefits every ops verb | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Program.cs:42` |

## v1.9.41 (cont.) — physically-impossible price bars: read-guarded and ingestion-rejected (findings 268-269, proposal P17)

*The aborted full-scale calibration's own data was audited to find why it froze nothing. Beyond the machinery bug (findings 251/255-257, its own §), the store carries **corrupt vendor PRICE bars** — a single garbage session per name, not an adjustment-path error. The exemplar is **CFC** (Countrywide, security_id 558): on **2007-11-29** both raw and adjusted close print **≈ $0.028**, wedged between $124.67 and $119.68 — a ÷4,467 dropout then ×4,290 rebound; **MEL** carries dozens of consecutive ones. Read-only measurement of the aborted run's 350 replay plants at the deepest horizon: these bars produce a **catastrophic sigma_lr tail, not a uniform shift** — **58 of 350** plants are polluted (a held bad-bar security ⇒ a ≥3× one-day equity jump), **all 20 plants with sigma_lr > 10% are polluted** (worst **360%**), lifting the polluted **mean** sigma_lr to **~124%** against a clean **~1.2%**, while the clean **median** moves only **~2×**. That tail is what drives the runaway plant equity and the affected plants' MDE floors. (Distinct from — and NOT the cause of — daily-cadence undetectability, which is the **~21.9%/yr cost drag** recorded with Change 4: daily-edge clean and polluted sigma_lr are within ~1×.) D21/D40 forbid deleting a stored bar (rule 3), so the remedy is two-sided: neutralize what is already stored at READ time (R1), reject the shape at INGESTION going forward (R2).*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 268 | **A physically-impossible single-session price bar already in the store inflates a plant's realized dispersion and explodes its equity, and can never be deleted** (rule 3 / D21/D40 append-only): the CFC $0.028 print compounds a ×4,290 one-day return into a member's equity, and a bad-bar-holding plant is exactly the polluted-tail plant (mean sigma_lr ~124%) whose MDE floor the calibration cannot trust. Nothing downstream could distinguish it from a real move | **R1 read-side fail-closed guard** (rule 10): `PopulationMarket.DailyReturn` neutralizes an impossible single-session move where the return is DERIVED — with a **one-step look-back** that resolves WHICH bar is the bad print and **spans the real move over it** rather than zeroing both sides of the V (CFC 11-28 $124.67 → 11-30 $119.68 = **−4%**, recovered not lost; the $0.028 print itself contributes no return, like a halted day). Consecutive bad bars (the MEL class) freeze, left to whole-security exclusion. Bound = the shared `Data.MaxSingleDayPriceFactor` (default 10.0 — the most extreme real single-day equity move is ~×4) | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/PopulationMarket.cs` (`DailyReturn` + `IsPlausibleMove`); `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/DataQualityGate.cs` (`DataQualityOptions.MaxSingleDayPriceFactor`); the bound threaded through `DailyPipeline` (×2) + `PlantEquityStep`; `Data:MaxSingleDayPriceFactor` in appsettings + CONFIG_REFERENCE; `PopulationMarketGuardTests` (incl. the real 2007-11-29 CFC fixture: print skipped, recovery spanned to −4%) |
| 269 | **The ingestion gate WARNED on these bars but never blocked them**: `CheckReturnOutliers`' robust-z DID fire (a 4,290× return clears `OutlierZ=8` by any margin) but at `Warn` severity — only NaN / non-positive prices were `Reject` — so a physically-impossible bad print was ingested rather than failed closed (rule 10) | **R2 ingestion Reject**: `DataQualityGate.CheckImpossibleSpikeReverts` emits `OutlierReturn` at `Reject` severity for a physically-impossible one-session **spike-AND-revert** (an interior V beyond `Data.MaxSingleDayPriceFactor`), **reusing the existing issue+severity tokens so NO schema migration is needed** (the two CHECK constraints are independent). Deliberately narrower than the robust-z Warn — it **requires the revert**, so a SUSTAINED one-way move (a real permanent event) is still warned+ingested, never dropped. On the backfill path a Reject **excludes the whole security** (D97, reported through `GateExclusions`); bar-granularity is **P17** | `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/DataQualityGate.cs` (`CheckImpossibleSpikeReverts`; `OutlierReturn` now dual-severity); `DataQualityGateTests` (spike-and-revert + CFC-shape → Reject; sustained move → Warn-not-Reject; window-edge + below-bound → no Reject) |

**Proposal P17 — bar-granularity ingestion rejection** *(filed, not built — trigger: the next full re-backfill, or the sp400 arena build, whichever comes first; until one of those the finding-269 whole-security behaviour has no effect, since R1 covers stored bars and `--reset` does not re-backfill members).* Principle: **exclude at the granularity of the DOUBT.** Identity doubt (ticker reuse, SUN/BSC — finding 266) means the whole series is suspect ⇒ whole-security exclusion is correct; PRINT doubt means one bar is suspect ⇒ the bar is the right unit. Add a **density escalation**: once a security's bad-bar count exceeds a threshold, escalate back to whole-security exclusion (MEL, carrying dozens, is the case that rule exists for). "Silent partial ingest" is a property of today's take-all-or-exclude-all implementation, not of bar-level granularity — surgical drop stays fail-closed **provided every dropped bar is reported with the same visibility a gate exclusion gets today.** DoD: dropped bars appear in the coverage artifact with the same prominence as gate exclusions; the density escalation has a stated threshold and a test; a re-run reproduces an identical dropped-bar set.

## v1.9.42 — the two-pass calibration machinery fix, B2 + B3 (findings 270-273)

*A pre-flight audit (46 agents) of the ABORTED full-scale calibration proved the machinery **fails its own verification gates and freezes nothing** — confirmed by direct query on the 396 committed replay sessions: **50/50 no-edge plants flagged suspect/retired ⇒ `joint_false_alarm = 1.00`** (bound 0.10) and **26/50 primary edge plants retired ⇒ `edge_survival = 0.48`** (floor 0.90). Root cause is a design flaw, not data: during the calibration replay the D56 curves don't exist yet, so the monitor runs on flat pre-calibration anchors + S6 escalation and RETIRES the D64 plants — which (a) truncates the S3 trajectory the curves are built from and (b) makes the KPIs read from that flat-anchor status hard-FAIL. The operator's governing principle: during calibration the monitor's verdicts are uncalibrated BY CONSTRUCTION; the category error is ACTING on them. The fix is two-pass (stop acting, keep recording), plus D63 conformance of the fallback (justified by the spec, not by whether the gates go green), plus a pre-registered per-cadence plant strength ladder. Metrics are ADDED, never narrowed. Explicitly rejected: adjusting fallback thresholds until the plants pass.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 270 | **Pass 1 — the monitor ACTS on uncalibrated verdicts during calibration**: a D64 plant retired on the flat-anchor fallback leaves the promotable set and stops emitting S3 rows, so the very curves being built collapse to survivors; the documented "raise `AutoRetireEvals` and re-run" remedy provably cannot converge (`joint_false_alarm` counts suspect-EVER, patience-invariant) | Change 1: a PLANT (`PlantCohorts.IsPlantId`) under a `run_kind='replay'` run is **never flipped to Retired** — it stays promotable and emits S3 rows for the full window. But the would-be retire is **RECORDED** (amendment A2): a distinct `go_live_log` `'WouldRevert'` row carrying the triggering signal, so finding 113's audit ("every edge-plant auto-retire logged with its trigger") and the would-be-survival KPI stay honest. The fix stops ACTING on verdicts, not recording them | `OverfittingMonitor.Evaluate` (`OverfittingMonitor.WouldRevertVerdict`); `Change1_ReplayPlant_ExemptFromRetire_*` (exempt + logged + not truncated); the live-retire control still retires |
| 271 | **The flat-anchor fallback violates D63**: flat-S3 `pct < 25 ⇒ instant Suspect` (no sustain, unlike S3Trajectory); S6 `critical_inband` = 3-consecutive-inside-band ⇒ Suspect (the exact "mid-band lifer" the §3 scope note forbids — the spec is self-contradictory); S6 `critical_neg_alpha` = 2-consecutive `t<−1` ⇒ Suspect (over-catches within-null excursions). All three flag a merely edgeless strategy, which "S3 never flags" (§3) | Change 3: flat-S3 requires **sustain** (`FlatAnchorSustainEvals=3`; a single dip is Warning); S6 inside-band **caps at Warning**, never Suspect (the §3 scope note: never catch mid-band lifers); S6 negative-alpha requires the same sustain. Grounded in the D63 rule text ALONE, never "the number now passes" (amendment C1). This also changes FORWARD pre-calibration flagging — which is the point. **Comparability caveat:** the post-change `joint_false_alarm` is NOT comparable to the 1.00 prior and is NOT independent validation (the monitor itself was adjusted) — only the curve-based metric (finding 272) is | `MonitorSignals.S3`/`.S6`; `S3_FlatAnchors_*_OnlyWhenSustained`, `S6_NegAlphaSuspectOnlyWhenSustained_InsideBandCapsAtWarning`; `AntiPredictive_LandsInTheTail_*` |
| 272 | **After Change 1 the retained metrics go vacuous, and there is no INDEPENDENT validation of the curves**: `edge_survival` reads `'retired'` status, which the exemption suppresses ⇒ trivially 1.00; and the flat-anchor `joint_false_alarm` measures the monitor's own (now-altered) flagging, not the curves | Change 2 (ADD, never replace): the retained finding-113 metric is repointed to the would-be-retire log — `would_be_edge_survival` (+ `edge_retires_logged`, verifying each `WouldRevert` carries its trigger); and TWO out-of-sample **curve-based** metrics are ADDED — `noedge_curve_breach_validate` / `curve_based_edge_survival` (per-plant SUSTAINED breach of the built `P_noise` on the held-out validate segment, the S3Trajectory logic). Each new metric gets its **OWN** `Replay.*` key (`NoEdgeCurveBreachMaxFrac` / `CurveBasedEdgeSurvivalFloor`), never reusing `JointFalseAlarmMaxFrac` / `EdgePlantSurvivalFloor5y`, so tuning one gate never moves the other. `pEdge` threaded into `ReplayVerification.Run` (the distinguishable count) | `ReplayVerification` (`WouldBeEdgeSurvival`, `SustainsAcross`, the new checks); `ReplayOptions`; `CalibrationOrchestrator`/`CalibrationReport`; `Change2_CurveBasedMetrics_ScoreSustainedValidateBreaches`, `FX_EdgeSurvivalFloor_*` |
| 273 | **`edge_plant_detected` targets the 2% DAILY plant, which cannot promote at any plausible alpha**: 0 promotions on the aborted run — daily's ~21.9%/yr random-redraw cost drag dwarfs the overlay (NOT a bad-bar artifact: daily-edge clean and polluted `sigma_lr` are within ~1×), and all edge strengths derived from one global `AlphaAnnualPct × [2,4]` so daily/monthly shared the 2% level | Change 4 (B3): edge strength is **per-cadence** (`Calibration.Plant.MonthlyEdgeLadderPct`, default geometric **2/4/8/16**; 16 = an explicit DETECTION-SANITY rung, not a plausible strategy). `PrimaryEdgeIds` is **RULE-selected** — the smallest rung clearing that cadence's pre-registered offline `cost_drag+MDE` floor (`DailyMdeFloorPct` ~37 unreachable; `MonthlyMdeFloorPct` ~15.9) ⇒ the 16% MONTHLY rung; a stated rule, not a hand-picked plant. Daily keeps its 2% plant as a SURVIVAL case (IN `FloorEdgeIds`, OUT of `PrimaryEdgeIds` — guarded by a test). The per-rung promotion IS the C-1 detection-power curve — read that, not the gate colour. **Recorded finding (three bounded parts):** (1) *a daily-cadence random redraw book carries ~21.9%/yr cost drag under the current cost model, so a plant riding that base cannot demonstrate an edge at any plausible alpha overlay*; (2) mechanism — the daily population re-draws scores every session, producing near-total reselection, and the drag follows from THAT, not from daily rebalancing as such; (3) non-transfer — finding-115 turnover matching means a real Phase-6 strategy is measured against a null matched to ITS realized turnover, so this does NOT carry over to daily-cadence strategy families | `PlantCohorts.Build`; `PlantOptions` (ladder + floors + `MdeFloorFor`); `ReplayVerification.PrimaryEdgeIds`/`.FloorEdgeIds` (public, rule-selected); `CalibrationOrchestrator.DetectionPowerCurves` (monthly); `Change4_PrimaryEdgeIds_RuleSelectsSmallestClearingRung_MonthlyNotDaily` |

**Not committed here (deliberate sequencing):** the two-pass fix is validated OFFLINE (Stage-1 gate on the aborted-run paths) then at SMOKE scale before the ~4-day run — evidence captured + a snapshot taken BEFORE any `--reset` (amendment C2, the reset deletes the sessions Stage-1 reads). The doc corpus reconciliation (MASTER §20.8/§20.9 two-pass + ladder + the daily finding, OVERFITTING_MONITOR §3 flat-anchor D63 conformance, D-numbers) is a SEPARATE commit at the end (the sanctioned CLAUDE.md C3 reconciliation pass).

## v1.9.43 — proxy-only backfill: the replay's regime warm-up + benchmark depth (finding 274)

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 274 | **The replay proxy + benchmark are short at BOTH ends**: the D70 member backfill never touched GSPC (regime proxy) or OEF (cap-weight benchmark), which sit at the Phase-1 `--years 20` window — **both start 2006-07-17/19**, so the replay window (2006-01-03…) has a ~6-month FRONT gap AND, worse, **zero** pre-2006-01-03 regime warm-up (D73 needs ≥ **956** = 200 + 3×252 sessions before the start, else `regime_labels` begin years late and the D64 regime-conditional overlays cannot condition over the early replay). Re-running the forward/historical backfill to fix this would risk the **P1 mass-eviction** — the membership reconciler is universe-blind (D70/D71), so a forward reconcile would evict the historical S&P 500 roster | A dedicated **`--proxy-only`** backfill mode (`BackfillRunner.BackfillProxiesOnlyAsync`): seed calendar + `BackfillRegimeProxyStep` (GSPC) + `BackfillCapWeightProxyStep` (OEF), and **nothing else** — no membership reconcile, no member backfill. Idempotent + append-only: it only EXTENDS GSPC/OEF backward. Default `--years 25` ⇒ `From ≈ 2001`, comfortably past the D73 floor; refuses `< 24` (fail closed). The operator runs it live (like the D70 pass); the CLI prints the post-run verification (GSPC ≥ 956 sessions before 2006-01-03, OEF a bar every replay session, `regime_labels` begin at/near 2006-01-03) to confirm before the full `--reset`. **Live run 2026-07-24:** GSPC warm-up **1,116 ≥ 956 → PASS**; OEF **5,030/5,031** (the one gap is 2014-04-22 — a genuine EODHD vendor hole, see finding 275) | `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/BackfillRunner.cs` (`BackfillProxiesOnlyAsync`); `tools/Backfill/Program.cs` (`--proxy-only` / `RunProxyOnlyAsync`); `BackfillProxiesOnlyAsync_IngestsGspcAndCapWeight_LeavesMembershipUntouched`, `_DryRun_MakesNoCallAndNoWrite` |
| 275 | **`MarkToMarket` conflated a frozen halt with a plain data gap** (surfaced verifying the proxy backfill: OEF lacks 2014-04-22, a session the name actually traded — 743 securities + GSPC have it). The mark applied **cost basis to ANY missing bar**, but D86's cost-basis mark is for a genuinely `Frozen` (unpriceable) position ONLY. For a held name with a one-day VENDOR gap the mark jumped to its (possibly ~years-old, 2006) cost basis for that single session and back — a fabricated one-day equity round-trip — and it fires for **ANY held position on ANY missing bar** (a latent GENERAL bug, not a one-hole data problem). The benchmark `buyhold:cw` holds OEF, so it hit this on 2014-04-22 (the old comment's "the dummies always hold priced names ⇒ never fires" was false). Note R1 (finding 268) does NOT fix this — it is a return-level guard on the population path, not the ledger mark, and even a return guard leaves the equity LEVEL wrong for that session | The per-position mark is a pure decision (`BasisMath.MarkOne`): priced today ⇒ raw close × shares; **`Frozen` ⇒ cost basis (D86)**; a NON-frozen missing bar ⇒ **carry forward the last known raw close** (`BarFeatureView.LastRawCloseOnOrBefore`, PIT-safe via `LastStoredDate`); cost basis is the deep fallback ONLY for a name never priced ≤ today. Fixes every future instance, so the 2014-04-22 hole needs no special-casing — the benchmark simply carries 2014-04-21's close one day | `src/AlphaLab.Core/Ledger/BasisMath.cs` (`MarkOne`); `src/AlphaLab.Data/Services/BarFeatureView.cs` (`LastRawCloseOnOrBefore`); `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Pipeline/DailyPipeline.cs` (`MarkToMarket`); `Position.Frozen` doc + D86 aligned; `MarkOne_*` (4 cases), `LastRawCloseOnOrBefore_CarriesForwardThePriorBar_NullWhenNeverPriced` |
| 276 | **The archived calibration report landed in the wrong directory** (surfaced on the Stage-2 smoke run): the D98 report — the Phase-4 **sign-off artifact**, cross-referenced by `Calibration.ReportRef` (path + sha256) — is written to a RELATIVE `Calibration:ReportDir` (default `docs/calibration`) resolved against the process cwd. .NET 10's `dotnet run --project src/AlphaLab.Worker` (the RUNBOOK's documented launch) runs with cwd = the **project dir**, so the report was archived under `src/AlphaLab.Worker/docs/calibration/…`, NOT the git-tracked repo `docs/calibration/…`. Left unfixed the full-run's sign-off evidence would land untracked | A RELATIVE `ReportDir` is now anchored to the **git repo root** (`CalibrationOrchestrator.FindRepoRoot`, walking up from the running assembly's dir for a `.git` marker — cwd-independent), so the report lands in the tracked dir regardless of how the Worker is launched; an ABSOLUTE `ReportDir` (what the tests inject) is honored **verbatim** so no test writes into the repo tree. Pure path decision, unit-tested (`ResolveReportBaseDir`) with a real-repo discovery check | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Ops/CalibrationOrchestrator.cs` (`ResolveReportBaseDir` + `FindRepoRoot`, public static); `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md` (`Calibration.ReportDir` comment); `FX_ReportPath_*` (4 cases: absolute-verbatim, relative-anchored, null-root-fallback, real-repo-resolves-to-tracked-docs) |
| 277 | **`FR25_ReproduceDay_LeavesLiveStoreUntouched` was flaky on Linux** (surfaced on the PR-event CI run for the finding-276 commit while the same-commit push-event run passed): `StoreFingerprint` mixed a **raw `FileInfo.Length`** term into a "store untouched" fingerprint. That byte-length is NOT a WAL-stable invariant — opening the store (even the after-measurement's own read connection) can trigger a passive checkpoint that flushes `-wal` pages into the main `.db`, growing the file with ZERO logical writes. The failure diff was `2\|168\|3\|6\|6\|42\|3\|3\|3\|0\|4096` vs `…\|0\|319488` — **every row count identical, only the file size differing** — so reproduce-day genuinely wrote nothing; the assertion was on a non-invariant | Drop the `FileInfo.Length` term: the ten row counts across every mutable table (runs/bars/trades/decisions/equity/control-equity/positions/snapshots/cash/regime) are the real "untouched" evidence — any actual write would add rows. Not finding 276's code (reproduce-day never touches the calibration path); a pre-existing test-quality defect, fixed to make CI deterministic before the full-run sign-off | `tests/AlphaLab.Worker.Tests/ReproduceDayTests.cs` (`StoreFingerprint`) |
| 278 | **The calibration report did not record its build configuration** — the sign-off artifact stated the window, watermark, seeds and membership vintage, but not whether the numbers came from a Debug or Release build. For a determinism-sensitive sign-off (float reordering under optimization is the classic risk) the provenance of *which build produced these numbers* belongs in the artifact, not in an operator's memory | Stamp the build config into the report header (`CalibrationOrchestrator.BuildConfiguration()` reads the SDK-generated `AssemblyConfigurationAttribute` ⇒ `Debug`/`Release`/`unknown`; rendered as `- Build configuration: **{cfg}**`). Paired with the operator's pre-run determinism check (a Release recompute of a Debug-computed replay prefix, byte-compared) so Release is only chosen after confirming Debug≡Release; the report then names the build actually used | `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Ops/CalibrationOrchestrator.cs` (`BuildConfiguration`), `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Ops/CalibrationReport.cs` (header line + `CalibrationReportInputs.BuildConfiguration`); `FX_Calibration_ReportGeneratedAndConfigRowsFrozen` asserts the line + the actual value |

## v1.9.46 — the freeze gate unblocked: `joint_false_alarm` is reported, not gating (D102, findings 279-280)

*Raised mid-flight, during the full-scale 4.11 run (session ~639/5031). Docs only — the running Worker uses its loaded assemblies, so no source edit can affect the generation in flight; the code change lands after the run (see PROGRESS "After the run completes").*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 279 | **The full-scale 4.11 run cannot freeze anything, and this was knowable before it started.** `joint_false_alarm` counts no-edge plants **EVER** Suspect (`SuspectEver`: `status IN ('suspect','retired')`) — a **MONOTONIC** statistic that can never recover once tripped. Measured read-only against the live store at session 639/5031: **50/50** no-edge plants (`plant:noedge:daily:0:0..49`), first at **2006-04-03 (~session 62)**, all 50 by 2006-11-30 ⇒ **1.00 vs `JointFalseAlarmMaxFrac` 0.10 ⇒ Fail**, locked. Finding 251 made the D98 freeze conditional on `verification.NoFailures` (a FAILED calibration must never install its curves — append-only config means a poisoned freeze can only be papered over, never removed); one Fail ⇒ `willFreeze` false ⇒ `frozenKeys` **empty**. The run will spend ~5 days, archive its report, and freeze **ZERO** config rows — the entire deliverable of checkpoint 4.11. **RECONCILIATION — this is NOT a scale surprise but a known-expected Fail never traced through to the gate.** The **Stage-2 smoke** (251/251 sessions, 2026-07-24) *already* recorded `joint_false_alarm` **100 % Fail** and classified it as expected per the **amendment-C1** comparability caveat; its **nonzero exit** was recorded and attributed to 1-year-scale `Insufficient`/non-comparable Fails. The **aborted full-scale run** before it likewise "froze nothing" at the same **1.00 vs 0.10** (alongside `edge_survival` 0.48 vs 0.90); findings 270–273 root-caused and fixed the *edge-survival* half (now 99/100) and left the `joint_false_alarm` half standing. **This is the third consecutive occurrence.** The defect is not detection — it is that **finding 251** (any Fail ⇒ freeze nothing) and **finding 271's amendment C1** (post-Change-3 `joint_false_alarm` is permanently non-comparable and expected to Fail) were never reconciled against each other; together they make the freeze **unreachable by construction**. **Caution for future readers:** `docs/calibration/sp500/2026-07-24-calibration.md` (`Replay run span: run 1..40`, `joint_false_alarm` **Pass** 0/50) is a separate **40-session** run, NOT the Stage-2 smoke — 40 sessions ends before the ~session-62 first flag, so that file must never be read as the smoke's evidence | **D102**: `joint_false_alarm` is removed from the freeze-gating set — still computed, still archived with its per-signal table, threshold unchanged at 0.10. Justified by the pre-registered amendment-C1 caveat plus the structural deadlock (flat-anchor behaviour is bootstrap-only and ends when the curves freeze, so gating curve installation on it blocks its own resolution). Finding 251's gate is retained for every check that IS independent evidence. **Implementation deferred** to after the in-flight run; because the generation is watermark-pinned and every session is already committed, the post-run resume re-runs only the verification + freeze stage — minutes, not days | Decision **D102** (MASTER §2); `src/AlphaLab.Evaluation/Calibration/ReplayVerification.cs` (`SuspectEver`, the `joint_false_alarm` check, `NoFailures`); `src/AlphaLab.Worker/Ops/CalibrationOrchestrator.cs` (`willFreeze`); test to add: a verification whose ONLY Fail is `joint_false_alarm` still freezes, and one whose curve-based check Fails still does not |
| 280 | **At flat anchors the monitor does not discriminate noise from anti-predictive — D63 conformance is incomplete.** Live at session 639, the two cohorts are flagged at *identical* rates: **50/50 no-edge** plants ever Suspect **and 50/50 anti-predictive** plants ever Suspect. One of those is the design intent (anti *should* be caught, and `anti_detection_speed` will pass on it); the other is precisely what OVERFITTING_MONITOR §3 forbids — "S3 never flags a merely edgeless strategy". Change 3 (finding 271) **helped but did not close it**: the finding-114 per-signal contribution at first-Suspect now splits **S6 43/50, S3 7/50**, so S3 is largely brought to heel (it was effectively the whole 50/50 before) and **S6 is now the dominant false alarm**. **This is not replay-specific.** Flat anchors are the pre-calibration state of EVERY arena (D100's premise), so any new arena's forward operation flags edgeless strategies at ~100 % until its first calibration freezes curves — it recurs at every arena bootstrap, not just here | **Recorded, not fixed.** No code change while the run holds the store, and the S6 sub-signal attribution needs the completed generation's `trigger_json` to be split cleanly. Filed as the open post-4.11 item: identify which S6 sub-signal still reaches Suspect on pure noise after Change 3 (`critical_neg_alpha` sustain / the inside-band Warning cap) and bring it to the D63 rule text *the way Change 3 did for S3* — grounded in the rule text alone, never in "the number now passes" (amendment C1). D102 makes this **non-blocking for the freeze**; it remains a genuine defect in forward pre-calibration behaviour and must not be closed by the D102 change | `src/AlphaLab.Evaluation/Monitor/MonitorSignals.cs` (`S6`); the per-signal contribution table in the archived report (finding 114); `docs/OVERFITTING_MONITOR_v1.9.md` §3 scope note; PROGRESS proposal register |
| 281 | **The D102 fix is necessary but NOT sufficient — `noedge_curve_breach_validate` is a second instance of the same defect, and it is the metric D102 leaves as the primary gate.** A full audit of the twelve checks feeding `NoFailures` (table below) found that `SustainsAcross` — the predicate behind BOTH curve-based metrics — returns `true` on the **first** run of `curve.SustainEvals` consecutive far-side points **anywhere** in the validate segment. That is an **"EVER" predicate**, structurally identical to `SuspectEver`: **monotone non-decreasing in validate-window length**, compared against a **fixed fraction** bound. Measured from the calendar: the Stage-2 smoke's ~5-month validate segment is **106 sessions ⇒ ~5 evaluations ⇒ 3 possible 3-consecutive start positions**; the full run's 2020-01-01…2025-12-31 validate segment is **1,508 sessions ⇒ ~71 evaluations ⇒ 69 start positions — 23×**. The smoke **already Failed** this check at **14 % (7/50)** on the *shortest possible* window, and PROGRESS recorded it as "within smoke-scale binomial noise of the 10 % bound (short ~5-month validate segment) — the metric to reconfirm at full scale", which has the **direction of the bias backwards**: a short validate window *understates* an EVER predicate. ~~Order-of-magnitude (independence assumed, so an overstatement — overlapping windows are positively correlated): 14 % over 3 positions ⇒ per-position hazard ≈ 0.049 ⇒ over 69 positions ≈ 97 % vs the 10 % bound.~~ **SUPERSEDED by direct measurement (finding 282): the 97 % figure was wrong** — it assumed serial independence, and the measured lag-1 autocorrelation of the below-anchor indicator is **ρ₁ = 0.737**, so breaches cluster and extra windows do not add proportional independent chances. Measured EVER-rate against window length on the live learn segment (K=2): **8 % @5 evals · 14 % @10 · 18 % @15 · 18 % @20 · 22 % @25 · 22 % @31** — it crosses the 10 % bound between 5 and 10 evaluations (and 14 % @10 reproduces the smoke's recorded 14 % exactly, corroborating the model). Extrapolated to the full run's ~71 validate evaluations the EVER-rate lands nearer **25–35 %**, not 97 %. The direction and the Fail are confirmed; the magnitude was overstated. **[SUPERSEDED at full scale: measured 6 % (3/50), Pass — the projection did not model the curve-estimation term; finding 289]** `curve_based_edge_survival` is the exact complement (`surviving = !SustainsAcross(...)`, floor 0.90) and is therefore **monotone non-increasing** in validate length — the smoke's 99/100 was measured on the same 3 positions. **So after D102 the run still freezes nothing**, for a different check, discovered *before* the resume rather than after it. `promotions_le_chance` / `days_to_indistinguishability` / `anti_detection_speed` share the EVER structure via `PromotedAmong`/`SuspectEver` but are **not** currently at risk (0/50 no-edge promoted at session 651; the anti bias runs toward Pass) — flagged to watch, not to change | **Requires a decision before the resume (D103, not yet taken).** The bound must be made window-invariant; three options, to be chosen on the rule text rather than on which one passes (amendment C1): (i) restate the metric as a **rate per evaluation opportunity** — the shape `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` already has, and the reason that one is sound; (ii) keep the EVER predicate but **scale the bound with the number of start positions** (`1−(1−q)^K` for a per-window hazard `q`); (iii) keep both and gate only on the rate form, demoting the EVER form to reported-only — the D102 treatment. **Not fixed here:** no code, no build, no decision taken unilaterally. Also noted: the `would_be_edge_survival_5y` comment prescribes "a floor failure recalibrates S6's patience", but RUNBOOK §8 records that loop as proven non-convergent and **gone** (finding 270) — a stale remediation pointer to correct in the same pass | Decision **D103** (pending); `ReplayVerification.SustainsAcross` (the EVER predicate), `noedge_curve_breach_validate`, `curve_based_edge_survival`, `PromotedAmong`; `Replay.NoEdgeCurveBreachMaxFrac` / `CurveBasedEdgeSurvivalFloor`; the per-member classification table below |

### Finding 280 — does the misfire survive the freeze? **PERSISTS** (answered 2026-07-25, read-only, run still in flight)

*The question asked of the code: are the S6 signals responsible for the 43/50 first-Suspect attributions evaluated ONLY inside the curves-are-null branch, or do they also run once `P_noise`/`P_edge` exist and `S3Trajectory` is active? Answered statically from the source; nothing built, tested, or written to the store.*

**Answer: PERSISTS. The S6 misfire is independent of the calibration state and does not end when the sp500 curves freeze.**

The evidence is structural, not statistical:

- The curves-exist / curves-null decision in `OverfittingMonitor.Evaluate` is an `if / else if / else` that opens at `OverfittingMonitor.cs:138` and **closes at `:170`**. It covers **S3 only** — the three arms assign `s3` and `s3Thresholds` and nothing else.
- **S6 is computed at `OverfittingMonitor.cs:174-189`, outside and after that block**, unconditionally. It is not in either arm.
- `MonitorSignals.S6` (`MonitorSignals.cs:106-108`) takes `(double rollingAlphaT, bool insideCentralBand, int priorConsecutiveInsideBand, int priorConsecutiveNegativeT)` — **no curve parameter of any kind**. The `pNoise`/`pEdge` values loaded at `OverfittingMonitor.cs:53` and passed into `Evaluate` at `:111-112` are consumed **only** at `:145-155`, the S3 trajectory arm.
- S6's only path to Suspect is `critical_neg_alpha` (`MonitorSignals.cs:110-115`): rolling 63-day alpha `t < S6NegativeAlphaT` (−1.0) sustained for `FlatAnchorSustainEvals` (3) consecutive evaluations. The inside-band arm caps at Warning by construction (`:116-123`, Change 3), so it **cannot** contribute a Suspect. Therefore all 43/50 S6 first-Suspect attributions are `critical_neg_alpha`, and that predicate reads only the strategy's own rolling t-stat and its persisted streak — **no calibrated input reaches it**.

**What the freeze does and does not change.** It does change S3 (flat anchors → trajectory) and it does change the auto-retire **patience** (`autoRetireEvals`, `OverfittingMonitor.cs:54` / `:201-202`, the calibrated `Monitor.S6.AutoRetireEvals`). Patience governs how many consecutive Suspect *aggregates* precede a Retired — it does **not** govern whether S6 reaches Suspect. So the freeze can slow the consequence and cannot reduce the misfire rate. The 43/50 is carried forward intact.

**Consequence — finding 280's trigger moves EARLIER: to before Phase 6 registers its first real strategies.** It was scheduled as a post-4.11 item on the assumption the defect was bootstrap-only and would next matter at the sp400 arena build. That assumption is now falsified, and the correct trigger follows from the fast-kill inventory rather than from arena scheduling:

- `DESIGN_IMPROVEMENTS_v1.9.md` §6 (consequence 2) names **exactly two** channels that can kill a strategy quickly: the **trade-level expectancy track** (§1.3) and **anti-predictive S3/S6 breaches**.
- §1.3 is **Phase 6 work** — `trade_evidence` has no EF mapping today, and BUILD_AND_PROMPTS §2 schedules the trade track with MeanReversion-fast in Phase 6.
- So at the moment Phase 6 first registers real strategies, the *only* fast-kill channel in existence is the S3/S6 one — and S6, its dominant arm, is at that point still flagging 43/50 strategies that have no edge at all.

Registering real strategies against a fast-kill channel that fires on pure noise is the failure this finding exists to prevent, and it arrives at Phase 6, not at the sp400 build. The sp400 bootstrap remains a *second* occasion on which it bites (flat anchors are every arena's pre-calibration state — the original row's point, and still correct); it is no longer the *first*.

**Unchanged by this answer:** the defect itself is still recorded-not-fixed, still non-blocking for the freeze per D102, and still must be closed on the D63 rule text alone rather than on whether a number passes (amendment C1). No code was changed, no remediation was chosen, and no new work item was opened.

### The freeze-gating set after D102 — per-member rationale (finding 281)

*Every check whose outcome can be `Fail`, and therefore feeds `verification.NoFailures` and the finding-251 freeze gate. Only `Fail` blocks; `Insufficient` never does. Recorded per member so the gating set has a stated rationale rather than one by accumulation.*

| Check | Fails when | Class | Rationale / risk at full scale |
|---|---|---|---|
| `promotions_le_chance` | `PromotedAmong(noEdge)` > `ceil(np+2√(np(1−p)))` = **4** | (a) evidence, EVER-structured | `PromotedAmong` is distinct-ever-promoted, so it grows with window length against a fixed bound; the bound's derivation treats per-plant false promotion as α, not `1−(1−α)^N`. **0/50 at session 651** — not at risk, but watch |
| `edge_plant_detected` | 0 edge plants promoted **and** window ≥ `MinTrackDays` | (a) evidence | **42 promoted already** (monthly@16). Window length pushes toward Pass |
| `joint_false_alarm` | — | **(c) — REMOVED by D102** | Amendment C1 declared it non-comparable and non-independent; flat-anchor regime is bootstrap-only (finding 279) |
| `anti_detection_speed` | <50 % of anti plants ever Suspect | (a) evidence | EVER-structured, but the window bias runs toward **Pass**. 50/50 caught. Safe |
| `days_to_indistinguishability` | `unpromoted×2 < noEdge.Count` | (a) evidence | Uses `PromotedAmong`; would need >25/50 no-edge promoted. Far from 0. `Insufficient` below `SeparationMinTrackDays` 252 — full run clears it |
| `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` | point breach rate > `2 × builtPNoise.FalseAlarmRate` | **(a) — the statistically sound one** | **IS in the gating set.** A RATE over validate points, not a max: **window-length invariant**, and more data tightens rather than inflates it. **Not superseded** by the sustained metric — it is the better-specified of the two. Expected to clear at full scale |
| `noedge_curve_breach_validate` | > `NoEdgeCurveBreachMaxFrac` **0.10** | **(c) — SECOND INSTANCE** | EVER predicate vs fixed fraction; 3 → 69 start positions (**23×**). Smoke already Failed at 14 % on the shortest window. Near-certain Fail. **Blocks the freeze after D102** |
| `curve_based_edge_survival` | < `CurveBasedEdgeSurvivalFloor` **0.90** | **(c) — SECOND INSTANCE (complement)** | `!SustainsAcross` ⇒ monotone non-increasing in validate length. Smoke's 99/100 measured on 3 positions |
| `would_be_edge_survival_5y` | `s5 < EdgePlantSurvivalFloor5y` | (a) evidence | Reads the would-be retire log (avoids the vacuous-exemption trap). 20y window clears 5y so it WILL evaluate. Stale comment: its prescribed S6-patience remediation was removed by finding 270 |
| `edge_retires_logged` | any would-be retire missing its triggering signal | (a) structural | Window-invariant audit. Safe |
| `allocator_value_add` | mean edge weight ≤ mean anti weight, once `T ≥ MinTrackDays` | (a) evidence | Genuine behavioural evidence; `Insufficient` below the gate minimum |
| `cohort_s3_paths_present` | no persisted replay S3 rows | (a) structural | Window-invariant. Safe |

### D103 framing — resolved: 0.10 is a RATE bound, and the implementation contradicts it (finding 281)

**Q1 — rate or lifetime probability? RATE, unambiguously, per the corpus's own named source of truth.**

Three independent lines of text converge, none of them inferred:

1. **`CONFIG_REFERENCE_v1.9.md` (the ONLY source of truth for config keys, per CLAUDE.md) line 198-200** defines `NoEdgeCurveBreachMaxFrac` as "bound on the fraction of no-edge plants that SUSTAIN-breach the built P_noise on the HELD-OUT validate segment — **the curves' own out-of-sample false-alarm rate**". The word is *rate*, and it is named as *the curve's own* rate — i.e. the same quantity the curve already carries.
2. **`CurveBuilder.cs:51`** builds P_noise as `Statistics.Percentile(xs, falseAlarmRate * 100.0)` with `anchorQuantile: falseAlarmRate`. The curve **IS** the `falseAlarmRate` quantile of the no-edge paths, so `FalseAlarmRate` is a **per-point** quantity by construction, persisted as `false_alarm_rate` in the curve JSON (`CalibratedCurves.cs:44`).
3. **`noedge_pnoise_breach_validate`** bounds a point-level rate at `2 × builtPNoise.FalseAlarmRate` — a rate compared against a rate, the same 0.10-family number, in the same report.

**Therefore the threshold is correctly specified and the IMPLEMENTATION is the defect.** `SustainsAcross` computes a *lifetime probability* ("did this plant ever sustain a breach anywhere in validate"), which is a different quantity from the declared rate and is not window-invariant. This is a **different defect from finding 279**, and the distinction matters for how each is fixed:

| | declared semantics | implemented semantics | defect is in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `joint_false_alarm` (279 / D102) | "EVER reaching Suspect … **over the replay window**" — a lifetime probability whose window is *unnamed and variable* | matches the declaration | **the specification** ⇒ removed from gating |
| `noedge_curve_breach_validate` (281 / D103) | "the curves' own out-of-sample false-alarm **rate**" | an EVER predicate over a variable window | **the implementation** ⇒ make it match the contract |

**This collapses the option set recorded in finding 281.** Option (i) — restate as a rate per evaluation opportunity — is not one candidate among three; it is **the only one that restores the documented contract**. Options (ii) and (iii) are *amendments* to a documented semantic and would require a `CONFIG_REFERENCE` change in the same PR, justified on their own terms.

**Q2 — conditional, if the decision nonetheless amends the semantics to a lifetime bound.** Then the bound **must name its window**, because an unnamed window is precisely what makes `joint_false_alarm` meaningless. The corpus-native choice is **`Gate.DetectabilityHorizonYears = 3`** (`GateOptions.cs:33`; `CONFIG_REFERENCE` line 114, D89/FR-40) — already the horizon over which the corpus asks "could this effect be detected at all?". On a 6-year validate segment that means evaluating the lifetime bound over 3-year blocks, not over the whole segment, so the statistic stops depending on how long the run happened to be.

**Q3 — the cost asymmetry, named here so that it CANNOT decide the outcome.** The candidate resolutions are not equally cheap, and the cheap ones are the ones that leave the monitor untouched:

- **Post-replay-stage changes (options i and iii)** — `SustainsAcross` and the breach criterion live in `ReplayVerification`, which runs *after* the replay and re-derives everything from the persisted S3 **percentile values** (`overfitting_checks.value`). Changing them invalidates no committed session. Cost: **one resume, minutes**, since every session is already committed and the watermark is pinned.
- **Replay-path change** — concluding instead that the **monitor's** sustained-breach rule is itself too weak (`pNoise.SustainEvals` as consumed by `OverfittingMonitor.S3Trajectory`, and `MonitorSignals.FlatAnchorSustainEvals = 3`) changes what the monitor *did* during the replay: statuses, would-be-retires and promotions all differ, so the generation is invalid. Cost: **a fresh multi-day run** (~4.5 days at the current blend).

That asymmetry is roughly **three orders of magnitude in wall-clock**, and it points entirely at the answer that requires no re-run. It is recorded for exactly that reason. Per **amendment C1**, D103 must be decided on the rule text alone — never on which option is cheap, and never on which option makes the number pass. If the evidence says the sustained-breach rule is too weak, that conclusion costs a fresh run and is still the right answer; the 3-order-of-magnitude gradient is a reason to be *more* careful about the reasoning, not a reason to prefer its conclusion.

## v1.9.46 (cont.) — the D103 evidence pass: two separate conclusions (findings 282-283)

*Offline, read-only, against the live learn segment (31 evaluations × 50 no-edge plants, uniform). The two findings below are deliberately NOT merged: they have different owners, different blast radii, and one must not be used to settle the other.*

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 282 | **The metric restatement (option i) is confirmed correct AND sufficient — owner: the verification stage.** Two call-site literals decide everything and neither is a config key: `CalibrationOrchestrator:115-116` builds both curves with `sustainEvals = 2` and `falseAlarmRate = 0.05` **hardcoded**. So (a) `curve.SustainEvals` is **2**, not the `FlatAnchorSustainEvals = 3` used by the flat anchors — the sustained-breach predicate needs only TWO consecutive points, weaker than finding 281 assumed; and (b) P_noise is the **5th** percentile, which makes the point-level bound `2 × 0.05 = ` **0.10** — *arithmetically identical* to `NoEdgeCurveBreachMaxFrac`. That is independent confirmation of the D103 Q1 resolution: 0.10 was set as a RATE, and the two 0.10s in the report are the same quantity expressed twice. **Measured per-OPPORTUNITY sustained-breach rate at the live K=2: 0.044**, versus the 0.10 bound ⇒ **PASS**, and window-invariant by construction. The EVER form on the same data is **22 % at 31 evaluations and climbing** ⇒ Fail. So restating the metric as a rate restores the documented contract *and* clears the bound, and the clearance is not a fudge: 0.044 sits comfortably inside 0.10 and does not move with window length. **Caveat:** measured in-sample on the learn segment, where the anchor is by construction the 5th percentile of these very paths; the out-of-sample validate rate against a frozen anchor must be reconfirmed when the run completes. **Also flagged:** two calibration-defining constants living as magic numbers at a call site contradicts `CONFIG_REFERENCE` being "the ONLY source of truth for config keys/defaults" — they should become keys in the same PR, with `SustainEvals` in particular named rather than buried | **D103 option (i)**, on the rule text: restate `noedge_curve_breach_validate` as a per-opportunity rate, matching the declared "the curves' own out-of-sample false-alarm rate". `curve_based_edge_survival` takes the complementary restatement. Threshold unchanged at 0.10. Post-replay-stage only — costs one resume | `CalibrationOrchestrator:115-116` (the literals); `ReplayVerification.SustainsAcross`; `Replay.NoEdgeCurveBreachMaxFrac` / `CurveBasedEdgeSurvivalFloor`; `CONFIG_REFERENCE` (new keys) |
| 283 | **The monitor's sustained-breach rule is a near-no-op filter against an autocorrelated path — owner: the monitor, and it is NOT settled by finding 282.** The sustain requirement exists to suppress noise by demanding K consecutive below-anchor evaluations, which only works if the S3 percentile path is close to serially independent. It is not: measured **lag-1 autocorrelation of the below-anchor indicator ρ₁ = 0.737**. Consequences, measured: at the live **K=2 the per-opportunity rate is 0.044 against a per-POINT rate of 0.05** — the sustain requirement removes about **12 %** of false alarms, i.e. it is very nearly doing nothing. Raising K barely helps and decays nothing like the IID law: **K=2 → 0.0440 · K=3 → 0.0372 · K=4 → 0.0307 · K=5 → 0.0259 · K=6 → 0.0215**, a mere 2× reduction across a 3× increase in required consecutive evaluations, while the IID prediction `far^K` falls from 0.0025 to 3.1e-8. The observed/IID ratios are **17.6× (K=2) · 298× (K=3) · 4,914× (K=4) · 82,963× (K=5) · 1,378,462× (K=6)**. So the rule cannot be repaired by turning the K knob — the assumption underneath it is wrong, and buying a 2× noise reduction costs a 3× detection-latency penalty on exactly the anti-predictive cases S3 exists to catch.
