This is the same root cause as finding 280 (the monitor not discriminating noise from anti-predictive at flat anchors), now with a measured mechanism | **Recorded, deliberately unresolved.** This is a monitor-design question — the candidate directions (an autocorrelation-aware anchor, a run-length distribution rather than a fixed K, or a different statistic entirely) are out of scope for D103 and must not be decided under the pressure of an in-flight run. **Explicitly: finding 282 clearing the gate does NOT close this.** A metric that reports honestly and a monitor that filters effectively are different requirements; 282 fixes the first only | `MonitorSignals.S3Trajectory` (`pNoise.SustainEvals`), `MonitorSignals.FlatAnchorSustainEvals`; `OVERFITTING_MONITOR_v1.9.md` §3; joins finding 280 in the post-4.11 monitor pass |

### Q2 resolved — no monitor verdict feeds back into a plant's trajectory, so the curves are `SustainEvals`-independent

**Structural proof.** `PlantEquityStep` computes every plant's equity as

```
equity_t = equity_{t-1} x (1 + memberReturn_t + overlay_t)
```

where `memberReturn` comes from `PopulationEngine.Step` (a random-redraw population member) and `overlay` from `PlantOverlay.OverlayReturn(Kind, AlphaAnnPct, Key, ordinal, ActiveDayFrac, MeanActiveRun, multiplier, runningMean)`. **No term reads `overfitting_status`, `go_live_log`, a promotion, an allocation, or any monitor verdict.** The class doc states the direction explicitly: "the monitor (S2/S3/S6), gate, and allocator **consume** equity curves" — one-way. `ordinal` is sessions since inception, counted from committed equity rows, so it advances regardless of verdict.

**Empirical corroboration.** All 50 no-edge plants carry **exactly 31** S3 evaluations — `path lengths across plants: [31]`, min = max. If any verdict fed back, path lengths would diverge. (This is also D100 holding at full scale: no plant truncated.)

**Therefore the cost asymmetry named in the D103 framing is smaller than assumed — measured, not argued.** Because the S3 percentile *values* are exogenous to `SustainEvals`, the built **P_noise/P_edge curves are identical under any K**, so changing K needs no curve rebuild and no fresh replay. ~~**The remaining exposure is narrower:** the persisted `overfitting_status` rows *were* computed inline at K=2 during the replay…~~ **CORRECTED — that was wrong, and the exposure is smaller still (zero).** `OverfittingMonitor` branches on `pNoise is not null && pEdge is not null`; during a calibration replay the curves do not exist (D100's premise, confirmed by the empty `Monitor.S3.*` config), so the **`else` branch ran**: flat anchors at `MonitorSignals.FlatAnchorSustainEvals = 3` against the fixed sub-25th `S3SuspectAnchor` — **`curve.SustainEvals` was never consulted by this replay at all**, nor was P_noise. The correct cost map is therefore:

| Change | Consulted by this replay? | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| the metric form (EVER ⇒ rate) | no — verification only | **zero residue**, one resume |
| `curve.SustainEvals` (2) | **no** — S3Trajectory is the curves-exist branch, i.e. FORWARD operation only | **zero residue**, one resume |
| the anchor quantile `falseAlarmRate` (0.05) | **no** — the curve is built post-replay | **zero residue**, one resume |
| `FlatAnchorSustainEvals` (3) / the flat anchors | **YES** — drove every persisted status | **fresh multi-day run** |

So the 3-orders-of-magnitude gradient named in the D103 framing was an overestimate for three of the four candidate changes and correct for only the fourth. The framing required naming it; the measurement then moved the gradient rather than the conclusion — which is the outcome the naming was for.

### Addenda to findings 282-283 (D103 evidence pass)

**282(a) — the in-sample pass is NEAR-STRUCTURAL and is therefore not evidence about out-of-sample clearance.** A K-window breaches only if **all K** of its points are below the anchor, so the breaching windows are a subset of the windows whose first point is below:

```
sustained-K rate  <=  per-point breach rate        (subset, up to edge effects)
```

and **in-sample the per-point breach rate IS the anchor quantile by construction** — P_noise is built as the `falseAlarmRate` quantile *of these very paths* (`CurveBuilder.Build(..., xs => Statistics.Percentile(xs, far*100), anchorQuantile: far)`). Measured: per-point 0.0568 against the 0.05 construction target, and every sustained rate sits under it (K=2 → 0.0440 ≤ 0.0568 ✓). So in-sample the statistic is **pinned below ~0.05 while the bound is 0.10 = 2 × 0.05** — the pass is close to guaranteed by arithmetic, not earned by the data. **The measured 0.044 therefore says nothing about whether the metric clears out-of-sample**, where the anchor is FROZEN from learn and the validate paths are fresh draws whose per-point rate is no longer pinned to 0.05. The earlier "in-sample caveat" understated this: it is not a caveat about precision, it is the observation that the in-sample number carries almost no information.

**282(b) — a consequence that D103 must confront: under option (i) at the same threshold, the check becomes REDUNDANT.** `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` already bounds the *validate* per-point rate at `2 × far = 0.10`. Combining with the subset inequality above: if that check passes, then `sustained-K rate <= per-point rate <= 0.10`, so a rate-restated `noedge_curve_breach_validate` at bound 0.10 **passes automatically**. It would contribute no independent gating information — the freeze would be gated twice on one fact. Remedies, for D103 to choose between on the rule text: (α) give the sustained form a **tighter, separately-derived bound** (the natural source is 283's quantile recalibration, which sets the target sustained rate directly); or (β) accept the redundancy and demote the sustained form to a reported diagnostic, leaving the point-level rate as the gate. Recording this now because "restate as a rate" was on track to be adopted as self-evidently correct, and it is correct about the *contract* while being incomplete about the *gate*.

**283(a) — quantile recalibration: a candidate fix distinct from the K knob, and strictly cheaper.** Instead of choosing the anchor quantile `q` to hit a target *per-point* rate and hoping the sustained-K statistic lands at `q^K` (which the ρ₁ = 0.737 measurement refutes), **choose `q` so that the sustained-K statistic itself hits its target rate under the empirical autocorrelated null**. The empirical null is exactly what P_noise is built from — the no-edge plants' own percentile paths — so the calibration is native to the existing machinery rather than a new assumption: sweep `q`, measure the realized sustained-K rate on the no-edge learn paths, and pick the `q` that delivers the target. This addresses the actual defect (the rule assumes independence) without paying the K-knob's detection-latency penalty, and it makes the bound *derived* rather than asserted. **Cost: post-replay, zero residue** — per the corrected table above, the anchor quantile is consumed only when the curve is built after the replay, so no committed session or persisted status is invalidated.

**283(b) — disambiguation the finding needs: there are TWO sustain rules, with radically different blast radii.** The ρ₁ = 0.737 measurement bears on both, but they are fixed in different places at different cost:

- **the curve-based rule** — `MonitorSignals.S3Trajectory` with `pNoise.SustainEvals = 2`, reached only on the curves-exist branch, i.e. **FORWARD operation after calibration**. Never ran in this replay. Post-replay fix, zero residue. This is what 283(a)'s quantile recalibration targets.
- **the flat-anchor rule** — `MonitorSignals.FlatAnchorSustainEvals = 3` against the fixed sub-25th anchor, which **drove every persisted status in this generation** and governs every arena's pre-calibration forward behaviour (finding 280). Changing it invalidates the generation: **fresh multi-day run**.

Finding 283 as originally written did not separate these, and the cheap half was at risk of being read as covering the expensive half. It does not.

**Magic numbers — registering the two keys is NOT sufficient (supersedes the finding-282 "also flagged" note).** Promoting `sustainEvals` and `falseAlarmRate` from `CalibrationOrchestrator:115-116` literals into `CONFIG_REFERENCE` keys leaves the load-bearing fact implicit: **`NoEdgeCurveBreachMaxFrac` (0.10) is `2 × falseAlarmRate` (0.05)** — the same "2×" that `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` applies in code as `2.0 * builtPNoise.FalseAlarmRate`. As three independent keys, a future change to the rate silently decouples the bound, and the two 0.10s in the report stop being the same quantity without anything failing. **Required in the same PR, one of:** (i) **derive** the bound from the rate so only one number is authored (`NoEdgeCurveBreachMaxFrac = PointRateMultiple × falseAlarmRate`, with the multiple itself a named key), or (ii) keep them separate but **assert the relationship in a test** that fails when they decouple, with the rationale stated at the assertion. Preference is (i): a derived value cannot drift, and the corpus's own reading of 0.10 (D103 Q1) is that it *is* the rate expressed twice.

### D103 x 283(a) coupling — D103 CANNOT close first (consequence of 282(b))

**The dependency.** 282(b) established that a rate-restated `noedge_curve_breach_validate` at the inherited 0.10 bound is logically implied by `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate`, leaving two ways out: **(α)** a tighter, separately-derived bound, or **(β)** demotion to a reported diagnostic. **(α) has exactly one principled source of that bound — 283(a)'s quantile recalibration**, which is what sets a *target sustained rate* under the empirical autocorrelated null instead of inheriting a per-point number. So the bound in (α) is not independently authorable: it is an output of 283(a). **Therefore D103 cannot close before 283(a) is decided.** This is a decision dependency, not a merge — findings 282 and 283 keep their separate owners exactly as recorded; the value of having split them is that the dependency is *visible* rather than buried inside one conflated finding.

**Decision tree.**

| 283(a) | D103 takes | Bound comes from | Recorded consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **adopted** | **(α)** | the quantile recalibration — sweep `q`, measure the realized sustained-K rate on the no-edge learn paths, target the desired rate | the sustained form stays a gate and is bound by a number derived from the null it is measured against |
| **rejected** | **(β)** | n/a — demoted to diagnostic | **an explicit gap must be recorded: nothing then gates the curve in the FORM THE MONITOR USES** |

**The (β) gap, stated in full.** The monitor consumes P_noise through `MonitorSignals.S3Trajectory` with the **sustained** rule (`pNoise.SustainEvals`). Under (β) the only surviving out-of-sample gate is `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate`, which is **point-level**. So the curve would be validated in a form the monitor never uses, and used in a form nothing validates. That is the same category error as finding 279 — gating on a quantity that is not the quantity of interest — arrived at from the opposite direction, and it must be written down as an accepted risk rather than discovered later.

**(β) is wider than the no-edge side.** Demoting the sustained form demotes its complement too: `curve_based_edge_survival` is the same `SustainsAcross` predicate on the floor-edge cohort. And **there is no point-level counterpart on the edge side** — the check inventory has `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` (point, no-edge) but nothing equivalent for P_edge. So under (β):

- **no-edge side** — gated point-level only; sustained form unvalidated (the gap above);
- **edge side** — **no curve-based out-of-sample gate at all.** The only remaining edge instrument is `would_be_edge_survival_5y`, which reads the would-be-retire log and is therefore **flat-anchor-derived** (283(b)) — a different instrument measuring a different thing, not a substitute.

Since `CalibratedKeys.PEdgeCurve("daily")` is frozen by the same run, **(β) installs P_edge with nothing validating it out-of-sample.** That is a substantive cost of (β) and a corresponding argument for taking 283(a) seriously on its merits — which is precisely the branch that the cost gradient (283(b): quantile recalibration is post-replay, zero residue) does *not* penalize. Recorded so that (β) is chosen, if it is chosen, with its price named.

### 283(a) merits procedure — PRE-REGISTRATION (D52 discipline; NOT YET EXECUTED)

**Status: no sweep has been computed under this protocol.** The selection rule below is recorded *before* the numbers exist, for the same reason the D101 ladder strengths were pre-registered: choosing the pair after seeing which one passes is the finding-279/281 defect relocated, not avoided. Nothing here may be revised once the sweep has run except by recording the revision and the reason as a finding.

**Prior exposure, declared.** One slice of this grid is already known and published in finding 283: **K = 2..6 at p = 0.05**, giving sustained rates 0.0440 / 0.0372 / 0.0307 / 0.0259 / 0.0215 and lag-1 autocorrelation ρ₁ = 0.737, all on the no-edge cohort, learn segment. The **p direction is unexamined**, and no anti-cohort detection or latency figure has been computed at any pair. The selection rule below must not be shaped around the known K-slice; it is stated in terms that were fixed before the p direction was seen.

**1. Sweep the PAIR, not a knob.** `p` (anchor quantile) × `K` (sustain length):
`p ∈ {0.005, 0.01, 0.02, 0.03, 0.05, 0.075, 0.10}` × `K ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}`. `K = 1` is included deliberately as the no-sustain baseline — it is the control that shows what the sustain requirement buys at each `p`.

**2. Null = the no-edge cohort (50 plants).** For each pair: build P_noise on the LEARN segment as the `p` quantile across seeds per evaluation index (exactly `CurveBuilder.BuildNoise`), then measure the **per-opportunity sustained-K false-alarm rate** over sliding K-windows. This is the statistic the monitor actually acts on, not its point-level shadow.

**3. Alternative = the anti-predictive cohort (50 plants).** For each pair, against the same learn-built curve: **detection rate** (fraction of anti plants with ≥1 sustained-K breach) and **latency** (median evaluations from inception to first sustained breach, among detected plants; undetected plants excluded from the median and reported separately as the undetected count).

**4. TARGET false-alarm rate — fixed here, before computing: `0.05`.** Corpus-derived, not chosen: `CONFIG_REFERENCE` calls `falseAlarmRate` "the curves' own out-of-sample false-alarm rate", and D103 Q1 established that this is the declared meaning of the number. The defect is that the declaration is currently applied to the **point** statistic while the monitor acts on the **sustained** one; the target therefore keeps the declared value and moves it onto the statistic it was always meant to describe. *(This is the one input that most needs sign-off before the sweep runs — if 0.05 is not the intended target, say so now, not after seeing the frontier.)*

**5. Selection rule — deterministic, applied in this order, no discretion at any step.**
1. **Feasible set** = pairs whose LEARN sustained-K false-alarm rate ≤ TARGET.
2. Among feasible, **maximize anti detection rate**.
3. Tie → **minimize median latency**.
4. Tie → **minimize K** (a shorter rule detects sooner and is simpler to state).
5. Tie → **maximize p** (prefer the less extreme quantile — less tail-estimation risk at M = 200).
6. **If the feasible set is EMPTY: the procedure fails, and that failure is the result.** No (p, K) achieves the target under the empirical autocorrelated null ⇒ 283(a) is rejected on its merits ⇒ D103 takes **(β)** with the gap recorded per the coupling note. The target is NOT relaxed to manufacture a feasible set; relaxing it after seeing the frontier is precisely the defect this pre-registration exists to prevent.

**6. Out-of-sample verification — the point of `--learn-through`.** With `(p*, K*)` frozen by step 5 and the curve built on LEARN at `p*`, compute the achieved sustained-K rate on the held-out **VALIDATE** segment. This is what makes the (α) bound *derived in-sample but tested out-of-sample*, and it is the answer to 282(a): the learn-segment rate is near-structural and proves nothing, the validate rate is the evidence.

**7. D103's (α) bound, derived — not separately authored.** `(α) bound = PointRateMultiple × TARGET`, with `PointRateMultiple = 2` inherited from the existing corpus relationship (`noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` bounds at `2.0 × builtPNoise.FalseAlarmRate`), now applied to the sustained statistic. Per the magic-numbers item this must be **computed from the target in code**, never authored as a third independent constant — that is what stops the two numbers silently decoupling.

**8. Pre-registered failure conditions**, each of which is a result rather than a reason to re-run:
- feasible set empty (step 5.6) ⇒ 283(a) rejected ⇒ D103 takes (β);
- validate rate > (α) bound ⇒ the calibration did not generalize; D103 cannot close on (α);
- selected pair's anti **detection rate < 0.50** ⇒ record as a finding in its own right: a curve that cannot catch anti-predictive strategies at the required false-alarm rate is a design failure, not a tuning outcome. The 0.50 bar is corpus-native — it is the same one `anti_detection_speed` already applies (`antiSuspect.Count * 2 >= ids.anti.Count`).

**9. Execution constraint.** The sweep reads only persisted S3 percentile paths (`overfitting_checks`), so it is read-only and post-replay by construction — zero residue, consistent with 283(b). It may be computed on the LEARN segment at any time; the VALIDATE half cannot run until the full-scale run passes 2019-12-31.

### 283(a) pre-registration — AMENDMENT 1: the TARGET (supersedes item 4; adopted before any sweep)

**Adopted target: at most 10 % of no-edge plants sustained-breach within any 3-year block, measured empirically on the null cohort.** This replaces the previously-registered `0.05` per-opportunity rate. Recorded as an amendment rather than an edit because the pre-registration is a dated artifact; the superseded value stays visible. Still **NOT EXECUTED** — no sweep has been computed under either target.

**Why it is strictly better than the 0.05 it replaces** (recorded so the choice is auditable, not merely asserted):

- **Measured, not converted.** It refuses the analytic `1-(1-q)^N` route, which assumes exactly the independence that ρ₁ = 0.737 falsifies (finding 283). The block fraction is measured directly on the no-edge paths, consistent with 283(a)'s "under the empirical autocorrelated null".
- **No new constant is authored.** Both inputs already exist: **10 %** is `Replay.NoEdgeCurveBreachMaxFrac`, **3 years** is `Gate.DetectabilityHorizonYears` (D89/FR-40). The magic-numbers item is satisfied structurally rather than by a naming convention.
- **It NAMES ITS WINDOW.** An unnamed, run-length-dependent window was `joint_false_alarm`'s original defect (finding 279) and `noedge_curve_breach_validate`'s inherited one (finding 281). So the **same 10 % is now correctly specified rather than merely removed from gating** — D102 deleted the broken form, this restores the number in a well-posed one. That is a materially better outcome than the threshold quietly disappearing.
- **Window-invariant by construction.** The block is fixed at 3 years whatever the run length; a 6-year validate segment is exactly two blocks.

**Consequential amendments** — the target touches more than item 5's feasibility criterion:

- **Item 2 (null statistic) is REPLACED**, not just re-thresholded: from *per-opportunity sustained-K rate* to *fraction of no-edge plants with ≥1 sustained-K breach inside a 3-year block*. Different statistic, different denominator (plant-blocks, not windows).
- **Item 7 (the (α) bound) SIMPLIFIES and the `PointRateMultiple` derivation is DROPPED.** The 2× existed to give a per-point rate out-of-sample slack; the new target is already expressed in the bound's own units, so **(α) bound = TARGET = 0.10** directly, with no third constant and no multiplication. This removes the magic-number coupling risk entirely rather than managing it.
- **Item 9 (execution) CHANGES: the LEARN half can no longer run immediately.** The block statistic needs at least one complete 3-year block ≈ 756 sessions; the run is at **675**. More importantly a calibration worth trusting wants the whole learn segment — **3,523 sessions ⇒ 4 full blocks** — so in practice the sweep now waits for the run to pass 2019-12-31, the same gate the validate half already had. (Validate = **1,508 sessions ⇒ exactly 2 blocks**, which is a clean fit and not a coincidence worth relying on.) Previously the learn half was runnable at once; it is not.
- **Items 1, 3, 5.2-5.6, 6, 8 STAND UNCHANGED** — the (p, K) grid, the anti-cohort detection/latency statistics, the tie-break chain, the empty-feasible-set ⇒ (β) clause, the out-of-sample verification, and all three failure conditions carry over verbatim.

**TWO SPECIFICATION POINTS STILL OPEN — both must be fixed before computing, because either can flip the outcome:**

1. **"within ANY 3-year block" — max-over-blocks, or pooled-across-blocks?** "Any" reads as a **max**, which is the stricter sense. But max over blocks is itself weakly increasing in block count, and learn has **4** blocks against validate's **2** — so a max-based criterion is systematically harder on learn than on validate, and could empty the feasible set, which under item 5.6 *decides* the outcome as (β). **Pooled** (total plant-block breaches ÷ total plant-blocks) is strictly window-invariant and therefore more faithful to the stated design intent, at the cost of letting one bad block average away. This residual is far milder than the original defect (4 vs 2 blocks, not 3 vs 69 windows) and biases conservative on learn — but it is not nothing, and the choice must not be made after seeing which one yields a feasible set. **Recommendation: pooled as the criterion, max reported alongside as a diagnostic.**
2. **Is the VALIDATE bound also 0.10, or 0.10 with a stated tolerance?** If the learn feasibility criterion and the (α) validate bound are the same 0.10, a pair calibrated to land just under it on learn (say 0.099) will very likely fail out-of-sample on ordinary drift. Either the selection rule should target materially below 0.10 to leave headroom, or the validate bound carries an explicit, pre-registered tolerance. Leaving them identical and discovering the problem at the resume would repeat the pattern this whole thread exists to break.

**Block boundary definition** (minor, but fix it now): calendar 3-year blocks (2006-2008, 2009-2011, …) rather than rolling 756-session windows — interpretable, aligned to the `DetectabilityHorizonYears` unit, and it makes validate exactly 2020-2022 / 2023-2025.

### 283(a) pre-registration — AMENDMENT 2, 2026-07-25 (supersedes the two open points in Amendment 1)

Still **NOT EXECUTED**. Prior text stands as written; this supersedes rather than edits it.

**2.1 — Criterion = POOLED; max = diagnostic.** The criterion is the **pooled fraction of plant-blocks carrying at least one sustained-K breach** (total breaching plant-blocks ÷ total plant-blocks). Rationale, recorded as the reason and not as a preference: **pooled estimates the quantity the target declares** — a per-plant-per-block probability — whereas **max estimates worst-case regime heterogeneity, a different quantity, and one that grows with block count**. Two estimators of two different things; the target names the first. The **max is computed and reported alongside** at every pair.

**Routing rule.** If max sits far above pooled, that is a **non-stationarity finding about the curves** and routes to the non-stationarity path. **It is never a reason to move the bound.**

> **Reference correction (verified, not assumed):** there is **no already-pre-registered non-stationarity path** in the corpus. A `grep` for `non-stationar|nonstationar|stationarity` across all docs and source returns exactly one hit — a passing remark in `POST_PHASE8_IMPROVEMENTS.md:71` about self-improving trade algorithms — which is unrelated. The corpus-native destinations that DO exist are **FR-41** (per-regime replay outcomes keyed to `regime_episodes`, D89) and **D45** (episode counter; anecdote badge below 3 episodes). FR-41 is explicitly "recorded now, **not load-bearing for this phase's DoD**" — a forward provision, not an active path. So the routing rule is adopted as stated, but its destination **must be opened as new work**, not referenced as existing. This is the same class of check that caught the non-existent "committed smoke report" in finding 279.

> **"Far above" needs a pre-registered threshold**, or the routing decision becomes post-hoc — the defect this protocol exists to prevent, reappearing in the routing rule itself. **Proposed, constant-free:** the routing fires exactly when **pooled ≤ 0.10 < max** — i.e. when the pair passes on the declared quantity while at least one block breaches the same bound. It authors no new number, reuses the bound already in play, and is decidable without judgement. Confirm or replace before computing.

**2.2 — Validate headroom is STRUCTURAL, not a tolerance.** No tolerance is authored and no multiple is reintroduced. Instead, **LEARN feasibility requires the criterion to hold in EVERY learn block** — stated up front as part of the selection rule, not reached for as a fallback. Since a per-block-all requirement is strictly stronger than a pooled-across-blocks one, **selection is strictly harder than the validate gate**, which asks only for the pooled fraction across two blocks. Headroom therefore arises from the shape of the rule instead of from an invented constant. If that empties the feasible set, **that is the result under item 5.6** — (β), with the recorded gap.

*Note how this resolves Amendment 1's open point better than either option offered there:* the max-vs-pooled asymmetry I flagged as a hazard (learn's 4 blocks being systematically harder than validate's 2) is **deliberately used as the headroom mechanism**. Learn feasibility is effectively the max criterion over 4 blocks; the validate gate is the pooled criterion over 2. Same bound, no third constant.

**POWER LIMITATION — recorded so a marginal result is read as marginal.** Validate is nominally **50 plants × 2 blocks = 100 plant-blocks**, but all 50 plants share **one market backdrop**, so the observations are far from independent and the **effective sample is plausibly near the block count itself (≈2)**. Consequently the validate check **can detect gross failure and cannot resolve marginal excess**. The report must therefore print the **two per-block validate fractions beside the pooled number**, so that a result sitting near the bound is visibly a two-observation result rather than a 100-observation one. A pooled 0.09 against a bound of 0.10 is not evidence of clearance at this power.

**2.3 — Leftover sessions are EXCLUDED from the block statistic.** Learn spans 2006-2019 = 14 years = **4 calendar blocks plus a 2018-2019 leftover**. A partial block would change the denominator, so leftover sessions take no part in the block statistic. *(Assumption made explicit, flag if wrong: the exclusion governs the block STATISTIC only — curve construction still uses the full learn segment, since P_noise is built per evaluation index across seeds and has no block structure.)*

**Calendar block boundaries — CONFIRMED:** learn **2006-08 / 2009-11 / 2012-14 / 2015-17** (leftover 2018-19 excluded); validate **2020-22 / 2023-25**. **Rolling windows are REJECTED** on the record: they overlap heavily, so they contribute dependence rather than information, and they reintroduce precisely the multiplicity problem findings 279/281/282 have been unwinding.

### PRE-REGISTERED RESPONSE — `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` failure (2026-07-25, before the number exists)

Recorded **before** the validate segment exists, for the same reason the 283(a) selection rule was: deciding what a failure *means* after seeing it is how a generalisation failure gets reclassified into a threshold problem. `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` compares validate-period no-edge S3 points against the LEARN-built P_noise, bounded at `2 × builtPNoise.FalseAlarmRate`.

**If it fails out-of-sample, the finding is stated in advance:** **P_noise built on 2006-2019 does not describe 2020-2025 — the curves do not generalise across the learn/validate boundary.** The no-edge cohort is edgeless by construction, so any excess breach rate is the curve mis-describing the null in the later period. It is a statement about the CURVES, never about the plants.

**Legitimate remedies — all concern CURVE CONSTRUCTION or RECALIBRATION CADENCE:**
- a **shorter or more recent learn window** (the 14-year learn segment may simply be too long to describe its own tail);
- **regime-conditional curves** (corpus-native: `regime_labels` / `regime_episodes` already exist, D64 plants are already regime-conditional, FR-41 already provisions per-regime replay outcomes — note D45's anecdote badge applies, since regime-conditional curves inherit a small-episode-count problem of their own);
- a **stated recalibration interval** (coherent with the 3-year block structure just adopted and with `Gate.DetectabilityHorizonYears = 3`).

**NOT among the remedies: demoting the check, re-bounding it, or widening the `2×` multiple.** This thread has already removed one check from gating (D102) and is considering restating a second (D103); a third instance would stop being a correction and start being a pattern. The check is the best-specified instrument in the set — a point-level rate, window-length invariant, comparing like against like — and that is precisely why its verdict is binding rather than negotiable.

---

**Two consequences, recorded as my additions for acceptance or rejection — not part of the specification above:**

**(a) This failure would be UPSTREAM of D103, not parallel to it.** D103(α)'s premise is that a pair calibrated on LEARN generalises to VALIDATE (pre-registration item 6). If the null distribution itself does not cross the boundary, then **no learn-calibrated bound generalises**, and the (α) derivation collapses along with the check — not because the sweep was done badly, but because its premise was false. So the order of reading matters: **`noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` should be read FIRST, before the 283(a) sweep results are interpreted.** If it fails, the sweep's validate half is uninterpretable and the remedies above take precedence over the D103 branch choice entirely.

**(b) The validate segment is not a neutral holdout.** 2020-2025 opens with the COVID break (Feb-Mar 2020), the sharpest regime discontinuity in the whole window. A generalisation failure across *this* boundary is therefore substantively plausible rather than surprising, and would be an interesting finding about curve stationarity rather than a nuisance — which is an argument for the regime-conditional remedy over the shorter-window one. It is also the same underlying phenomenon that Amendment 2.1's routing rule detects by a different instrument (max ≫ pooled across blocks), so **both should route to the same destination** — the non-stationarity work item that, per Amendment 2.1's reference correction, **does not yet exist and must be opened**.

### Consequences (a) and (b) — ACCEPTED with refinements, 2026-07-25 (supersedes the versions recorded above)

**(a) ACCEPTED as written:** a `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` failure is **UPSTREAM of D103, not parallel to it**, because D103(α)'s premise (pre-registration item 6) is that a learn-calibrated pair generalises to validate. **Reading order fixed: read the point-level check BEFORE interpreting the 283(a) sweep.** Two refinements:

- **Refinement 1 — the collapse is PARTIAL, and the learn result must NOT be discarded with the validate one.** The sweep's **LEARN half still answers 283's own question**: whether the sustain rule can be calibrated to hit its target under the empirical null. That question is internal to the learn segment and survives a generalisation failure intact. Only the **validate/generalisation half** dies. Recorded explicitly because "the premise was false" reads as "throw it all away", and one genuine result would go in the bin with the invalid one.
- **Refinement 2 — the consequence is STRONGER than "uninterpretable", and my word choice was the problem.** Every legitimate remedy (shorter/more recent learn window, regime-conditional curves, stated recalibration interval) **changes the curves the sweep was calibrated against**. So the sweep must be **RE-RUN against rebuilt curves, not interpreted and patched**. "Uninterpretable" invites salvage attempts — reading the old feasibility set "for guidance", carrying a selected pair forward "provisionally" — and none of that is valid once P_noise changes. The re-run is cheap (read-only over persisted paths), so the cost of stating this strictly is analysis time, not compute.

**(b) ACCEPTED as written**, with one correction that **strengthens** it:

- **CORRECTION — LEARN IS NOT REGIME-POOR, and my framing implied it was.** The 2006-2019 learn segment contains **2008** (GFC), **2011** (euro-debt / US downgrade) and **2015-16** (China devaluation / oil collapse). *(And although the 2018-19 leftover is excluded from the block statistic per Amendment 2.3, it still contributes to curve construction, so the Q4-2018 drawdown is in the curves too.)* So a generalisation failure would **more likely concern the SPEED and SHAPE of the 2020 dislocation than the absence of crisis data in learn**. My reading — "validate contains a regime learn never saw" — was wrong; learn saw crises, just not one that moved that fast.
- **Consequences of the correction.** It cuts against the **shorter-window remedy harder than I stated**: shortening the learn window would *strip out* crisis data the curves demonstrably benefit from, making the curves worse at exactly the tail they exist to describe. And it sharpens the preferred remedy from the generic "regime-conditional" to a **volatility-state-keyed conditional curve** — conditioning on the state that actually differs (realized-vol regime / speed of drawdown), not merely on bull/bear trend.

**Item 3 — the two detectors are COMPLEMENTARY, not redundant. This SUPERSEDES my earlier claim that they detect "the same phenomenon", which was wrong:**

| Detector | Catches | Fires when |
|---|---|---|
| `noedge_pnoise_breach_validate` | a **LEVEL SHIFT across all of validate** | both blocks bad ⇒ pooled rises above the bound ⇒ the pair is infeasible, and the Amendment 2.1 routing rule **never fires** (it requires `pooled ≤ 0.10 < max`) |
| the max-vs-pooled routing rule | **HETEROGENEITY BETWEEN blocks** | one bad block while pooled still clears |

**Either can fire without the other.** A uniform shift is invisible to the routing rule; a single-block excursion is diluted in the pooled point-level rate. This scopes the destination work item: **it must accommodate both shapes** — a uniform level shift in the null, and between-block heterogeneity — and a design that only handles one leaves the other detector pointing nowhere.

### 283(a) pre-registration — CONFIRMATIONS + milestone correction, 2026-07-25

Three items answered in discussion that had not reached the record. Recorded now, before the numbers exist, so the protocol is complete on paper rather than in memory.

**C1 — Routing threshold CONFIRMED: route iff `pooled <= 0.10 < max`.** Constant-free, reuses the bound already in play, decidable without judgement. Two notes carried with it:

- **SCOPE — it is a VALIDATE-side diagnostic only, and can never fire on learn.** Amendment 2.2 makes LEARN feasibility require the criterion to hold in *every* learn block, i.e. `max <= 0.10` on learn by construction. So for any pair that survived selection, the routing condition `0.10 < max` is unsatisfiable on the learn segment. It fires only on validate, where the gate is pooled-across-two-blocks and a single block is therefore free to exceed while pooled still clears. This is not a limitation but the intended division of labour: learn selects under the strict rule, validate is where heterogeneity can still surface.
- **PROVISIONALITY — a routed finding is recorded as PROVISIONAL, not established.** With validate's effective sample near the block count (≈2, per Amendment 2.2's power limitation), **one block over the bound is easily noise**. A routing hit is therefore a **flag for investigation**, never a conclusion about curve non-stationarity, and P18 must receive it as such. Recorded because a detector that fires rarely is exactly the kind whose single hit gets over-read.

**C2 — Leftover / curve-construction CONFIRMED as recorded.** The 2018-2019 leftover exclusion governs the **BLOCK STATISTIC only**. **Curve construction uses the full learn segment**, because P_noise is built per evaluation index across seeds and has **no block structure** — the exclusion exists solely to keep the block denominator whole. Excluding 2018-2019 from curve construction would weaken the curves for no benefit, discarding real tail data (including the Q4-2018 drawdown) to satisfy a constraint that does not apply to them.

**C3 — MILESTONE CORRECTION (supersedes the single-milestone statement in the status reporting).** "The run must pass 2019-12-31 before out-of-sample work can start" is **the FIRST milestone only**, and stating it alone understates what the protocol needs. There are **two**:

| Milestone | Unlocks | Requires |
|---|---|---|
| **M1 — run passes 2019-12-31** | LEARN complete ⇒ the 283(a) **pair-selection sweep** can run (4 learn blocks available) | ~2 days from 2009-07-13 at the current rate |
| **M2 — run FINISHES (2025-12-31)** | the **VALIDATE gate**, which needs **BOTH** calendar blocks complete — 2020-22 **and** 2023-25 | the full run |

**A run stopping mid-validate yields at most ONE usable block**, because Amendment 2.3 excludes partial blocks — halving a sample whose effective n is already about 2. So an interruption after M1 but before M2 does not "mostly" deliver the validate check; it degrades it from weak to near-useless. M2 is not a convenience, it is a precondition.

## v1.9.46 (cont.) — the in-flight run's own diagnostics: finding 284 + the 266 correction

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 284 | **93 index members carry ZERO bars inside their own membership span — finding 266's ticker-reuse defect at scale, discovered from the running calibration's own flag stream.** The replay has emitted **2,604,858 `missing_bar` warn flags across 898 sessions (~2,907/session)**, resolving to **67,788 distinct `(security, date)` gaps at a 38.4× duplication factor** (the same gap is re-flagged by every session whose lookback covers it) across **107 securities**. Of those 107, **93 have no bars at all inside their own `index_membership` span**, and the signature is unambiguous — **the stored bars always begin AFTER membership ends**: `ABI` member 1996-2008 / bars 2025-06→2025-12; `ABS` 1996-2006 / 2018-08→2019-06; `ACV` 1996-2006 / 2015-05→2025-12; `AT` 1996-2007 / 2010-07→2021-05; `BEAM` 1996-2014 / 2020-02→2025-12; `BUD` 1996-2008 / 2009-07→2025-12; `BR` 1996-2006 / none at all. That is EODHD returning the symbol's **current** holder and the backfill storing that company's history in place of the member's. Finding 266 identified this exact mechanism, acted on the single confirmed case (SUN, 0.7 % in-window coverage) and deferred the systematic threshold to **P16** — so P16 is not a refinement, it is an open defect whose measured scope is **93 securities at 0 % coverage**, a far stronger signal than the one case that was actioned. **Two secondary consequences:** the 38.4× duplication projects to **~14.6M flag rows** by completion (DB bloat + per-session write cost), and genuine new gaps are buried in 2.6M duplicates | **P16 RESCOPED to the measured 93** (from "a coverage-based exclusion threshold" in the abstract to a named, counted set). **Run-validity reasoning, recorded rather than assumed:** the wrong-company bars lie OUTSIDE the membership window, so as-of membership resolution (D70) never makes them selectable — this is **absence, not mispricing**; and plants and populations draw from the same reduced universe, so the *separation* the calibration measures is affected **symmetrically**. The in-flight run is therefore **not invalidated**. **But the universe is SURVIVOR-BIASED in a new way:** the 93 are systematically the names that exited by **acquisition or failure** (that is precisely when a ticker becomes available for reuse), so the replay's investable universe is missing its casualties. **The direction of the distortion is not obvious** — fewer failures flatters realized returns, but the missing names are also the ones whose late-life behaviour the monitor most needs to see. The consequence that matters: **the null the curves are calibrated on differs from the null forward operation will face.** Routed to **P18** alongside the learn/validate non-stationarity concern — same destination, because both are "the calibrated null does not describe the operating null" | `data_quality_flags` (`missing_bar`); `index_membership` vs `bars` coverage; `Universe:Exclusions` + `ExclusionScopedMembershipRead` (finding 266); **P16** (rescoped), **P18** (survivor-bias route) |
| — | **CORRECTION to finding 266 — the BSC clearance was incomplete; BSC belongs in the 284 bucket.** Finding 266 records "SUN listed; **BSC investigated and CLEARED**". The clearance was right about what it examined: BSC's **2008-03-17 → 2008-05-30** bars ($4.81 → $11.23) *are* genuine Bear Stearns, matching the $2-then-$10 JPMorgan deal. What it missed is on both sides of that window. **Below:** BSC's earliest stored bar is **2008-03-17**, though `index_membership` runs **1998-07-01 → 2008-06-02** — roughly a decade of membership with no data, which is why the running replay refuses BSC orders on 2008-03-18 with "no positive 21-day ADV" (correct rule-10 behaviour on absent input). **Above:** bars continue to **2011-08-25** with average daily volume of **3,548 / 5,029 / 2,691 shares** in 2009/2010/2011 against ~7.2M during the collapse — a different, essentially illiquid instrument on a reused ticker, Bear Stearns having ceased to exist in May 2008 | BSC is **reclassified into finding 284's set** (member-span coverage gap + post-exit reuse tail) rather than remaining "cleared". No exclusion-list change is proposed here: the 2008 collapse bars are genuine and in-window, so a whole-security exclusion would discard real data — this is exactly the **granularity-of-the-doubt** case **P17** exists for, and the two proposals should be resolved together | finding 266 row (amended by this correction); `securities`/`index_membership`/`bars` for `security_id=590`; **P16** + **P17** |
| 285 | **The number the gate, the allocator and the Strategies screen call "alpha" is a RAW active-return gap, not the beta-adjusted (Jensen's) alpha the spec mandates — a spec-vs-code divergence, not a judgement call.** `EvaluationStep.cs:83-84` builds `d[i] = stratReturns[i] − benchReturns[i]` and `:88` annualises it (`gap = d.Average() * TradingDaysPerYear`), persisted as `PowerReportRow.ObservedGapAnn` (`:100`). **No beta term appears anywhere on that path.** That single value then feeds all three consumers: (1) the **promotion gate** — `PromotionGate.Decide(gap, mde.MdeAnn, …)` at `EvaluationStep.cs:89`; (2) the **allocator's weights** — `AllocationStep.cs:58` `var alphaPct = (r.ObservedGapAnn ?? 0.0) * 100.0`, which becomes `AllocationRowDto.AlphaHatPct`; (3) the **alpha cell on the Strategies screen** — `StrategiesReadModelBuilder.cs:83-95` renders `ObservedGapAnn` as the `alpha` `MetricCell`. The spec is unambiguous in both places: **D26** (MASTER §2 line 153) reads *"Alpha = beta-adjusted (Jensen's) alpha + IR; **never a raw return gap**"* with the rationale *"low-beta strategies otherwise structurally rigged to lose"*, and **CLAUDE.md hard rule 6** reads *"Alpha = beta-adjusted (Jensen's) vs the cap-weight account"*. **The correct calculation already exists and is already correct:** `StrategyMetrics.JensenAlpha` (`StrategyMetrics.cs:43-54`) runs OLS of `(r_s−r_f)` on `(r_b−r_f)` with Newey–West HAC errors and returns `Beta`/`AlphaAnnualized`/`AlphaTStat`. Its only callers are `OverfittingMonitor.cs:400` (S3/S6 internals), `ReplayRegimeOutcomesWriter.cs:126` and `SeedingBacktestEngine.cs:74` — **none in `EvaluationStep` or `AllocationStep`**. So this is a wiring gap, not missing mathematics. (Second-order, recorded not actioned: all three `JensenAlpha` call sites pass `rfDaily: 0.0`, so even the beta-adjusted path is not yet on the French RF series — Phase 6, `MetricsConstants.RiskFreePlaceholderAnnual`) | **Filed, not fixed.** Four qualifications recorded with it so the scope is not overstated. **(a) SCOPE — benchmark-relative channel only.** The D36 turnover-matched random populations are drawn from the same universe and therefore carry market beta themselves, so the population-percentile channel is **beta-robust by construction**; S3's percentile ranking is unaffected. The defect hits the benchmark-relative channel — gate verdict, allocator weight, screen cell — and nothing else. **(b) WHY THE RUNNING CALIBRATION CANNOT EXPOSE IT.** Every D64 plant is an *overlay on a population member* (`PlantEquityStep`: `equity = priorEquity * (1 + memberReturn + overlay)`), so every plant is **beta-matched to its own base by construction**. The observed 0/50 no-edge promotions therefore does **NOT** validate the gate's beta handling — the test never varies beta. Only real strategies with genuinely varying beta would exercise it, and those arrive at Phase 6. ### Finding 285, cont. — why the curves survive, what does not, and the generation-2 position (2026-07-25, read-only, run in flight)

**1. The load-bearing reason the D56 curves survive finding 285 is `SafeAlpha`, NOT plant-equity verdict-independence.**

The S3 percentile that `CurveBuilder` consumes is `Statistics.PercentileRank(memberAlphas, SafeAlpha(stratReturns, benchReturns).Alpha)` (`OverfittingMonitor.cs:147` calibrated arm / `:162` flat arm), and `SafeAlpha` (`:396-408`) computes its alpha at `:400` as `StrategyMetrics.JensenAlpha(strat, bench, 0.0, DefaultLag)`. **The monitor path has been beta-adjusted all along.** Finding 285's raw-gap defect is confined to `EvaluationStep.cs:84-88` and therefore **never reached the curve inputs**.

This **supersedes** the earlier and weaker argument from `PlantEquityStep` (`equity = priorEquity * (1 + memberReturn + overlay)`, no verdict term). That argument is still true, but it establishes only that a plant's *trajectory* is independent of the verdicts passed on it — it says nothing about whether the *alpha* feeding the curve is the mandated one. `SafeAlpha` answers that directly, and is the reason to cite from here on.

**Bounded caveat, recorded so the claim is not overstated.** `SafeAlpha` carries a `catch (ArgumentException)` fallback at `:403-408` that *does* return a raw annualised mean gap (with `T = 0`). It fires only when the regression rejects its input as degenerate, so it is an exception path rather than the normal one — but "beta-adjusted all along" is true of the normal path, not of every path. Separately, the `rfDaily: 0.0` argument means this path is beta-adjusted but **not** RF-adjusted (the Phase-6 French series, already recorded on the finding-285 row).

**2. The survives / does-not-survive split, recorded explicitly.**

| Artifact | Generation 1 valid? | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **`P_noise(t)`** | **SURVIVES** | built from S3 percentile paths → `SafeAlpha` → `JensenAlpha`; beta-adjusted, untouched by 285. Plants stay promotable under D100, so the paths are full-length regardless of any monitor verdict |
| **`P_edge(t)`** | **SURVIVES** | same construction, same reasoning |
| `Calibration.DetectionPower` (the C-1 sweep) | **does NOT** | it is a promotion count, and promotions come from `PromotionGate.Decide(gap, …)` at `EvaluationStep.cs:89` — the raw-gap path |
| `promotions_le_chance` | **does NOT** | gate-derived, same path |
| `edge_plant_detected` | **does NOT** | gate-derived, same path |
| `days_to_indistinguishability` | **does NOT** | ~~read from persisted monitor status, which the finding-280 flat-anchor rule drove~~ **CORRECTED (v1.9.50 audit): reads promotions** (`PromotedAmong`, `ReplayVerification.cs:146`), not statuses — the does-not-survive conclusion stands via the raw-gap promotion channel (finding 285); the stated mechanism was wrong |
| `anti_detection_speed` | **does NOT** | same — status-derived |

Short form: **the curves survive, the verification numbers do not.**

**3. Scheduling position — generation 2 is a constraint on WHEN, not a stop.**

Generation 2 is **not a repeat of this run**. This run's deliverable is the D56 trajectory curves, and those hold. Generation 2 re-measures the *checks* — "did the machinery behave?" — with findings 280 and 285 fixed, so the verification numbers mean what they claim.

The recorded position is therefore: **freeze these curves, proceed to Phase 4.5 and Phase 5, and run the fresh generation at any convenient point before Phase 6**, bundling the 280 and 285 fixes into it. Both findings already carry a pre-Phase-6 trigger for independent reasons; generation 2 is where they land together. Nothing downstream waits on it — it is a **scheduling constraint, not a blocker**, and must not be read as one.

**4. FREEZE CAVEAT — `promotions_le_chance` and `edge_plant_detected` are computed from promotions produced by the raw-gap alpha.**

Both are in the D102 gating set and both will be read as sign-off evidence, so the caveat is recorded **before** the report is read rather than after.

*The survival argument, stated as an argument and not assumed:* every D64 plant is an overlay on a population member and is therefore **beta-matched to its own base**. The raw gap and Jensen's alpha differ by a term in `(β − 1)` against the benchmark's excess return — and because every plant shares the same base construction, **that term is common to the edge and no-edge cohorts alike**. It shifts the absolute level of both, and leaves the *separation between them* — which is exactly what these two checks measure — intact. So the checks are expected to hold their verdicts. **Generation 2 confirms it; this generation does not.**

*Why this must be written down rather than relied on silently:* without it, a check's **verdict** and the check's **meaning** have diverged with nothing in the corpus recording the divergence. That is precisely the family of problem that produced **finding 279** — a check locked at Fail against a bound nobody had reconciled with the caveat that already declared it non-evidential. The cost of recording it is one paragraph; the cost of not recording it was, last time, the entire deliverable.

**5. The recompute question stays as recorded — one line added, no position changed.**

The **fresh-run** position stands unchanged (CHANGELOG v1.9.46 cost map: a flat-anchor change "drove every persisted status ⇒ fresh multi-day run"). Adding only this: the post-hoc **recompute** alternative — recomputing statuses and promotions from persisted inputs instead of re-replaying — carries an **objective acceptance test**, so it can be scoped after the run without the decision resting on judgement: *it must reproduce this generation's existing statuses and promotions EXACTLY under the CURRENT rules before it is trusted to produce them under corrected ones.* A recompute that cannot reproduce the known answer cannot be trusted with the unknown one. Not proposed, not scoped, not decided here.

### Recorded hypothesis — per-session FLAG WRITES may drive the pace profile (NOT yet tested)

Recorded as a falsifiable prediction before the test, not after. The replay's per-session cost has an unexplained shape: it rose from ~44 s to a **185 s peak over sessions ~350-550 (2007-06 → 2008-03)** and then fell back to **~64 s**, where it has held for 200+ sessions — including straight through the Lehman peak, which is the opposite of what a volatility-driven cost would do.

**Hypothesis.** The hump is **flag-write volume**, not market conditions or portfolio occupancy. Many of the 93 have membership ending **2006-2008**; a security emits `missing_bar` flags on every session while it is a member and stops the moment it exits. That predicts a **ramp-then-fall** profile whose fall tracks the exit dates — which matches the hump resolving by 2008-05.

**Why this beats the explanation I gave earlier.** I attributed the hump to portfolio occupancy ramping and saturating. Saturation predicts a **plateau**, not a return to the earlier level; it cannot explain the fall. The flag-write hypothesis explains ramp *and* fall with one mechanism, and it is the only candidate so far that does.

**Test (read-only, no writes, when convenient — deliberately NOT run during the calibration):** correlate per-session duration (`runs.started_at`/`finished_at`) against (a) per-session `data_quality_flags` row counts and (b) the membership-exit dates of the 93. A positive correlation with (a), and a fall in both series aligned to (b), confirms. If confirmed, the 38.4× duplication stops being mere bloat and becomes a **material throughput defect** — deduplicating the flag write would then be a performance fix for every future replay, not just a tidiness one.

## v1.9.47 — AI decision transparency, specified before the implementation (D104-D105)

*Recorded 2026-07-25, during the 4.11 calibration run. Docs only: no `src/` change, no build, no tests. `src/AlphaLab.Llm` contains one `.csproj` and zero source files, which is the point — see "why now" below.*

| # | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| **D104** | **AI decision transparency: the record must be complete enough that re-execution is never required.** Four artifacts persist per AI decision — (a) the exact context pack **as stored bytes**, (b) the **raw** model output, (c) the parsed decision **and what the funnel actually did with it**, (d) model string + prompt version + sampling parameters — catching, respectively: wrong input, misparse, misapplication, and behaviour change after a model swap. Two invariants ship **with** the seat: `FX-PackNoLeak` (no pack field carries an `observed_at` later than the simulated as-of; a pack contains **only** whitelisted fields, closure-tested) and `FX-TwinDivergenceIndex` (every contestant-vs-twin divergence resolves to exactly one `ai_decisions` row). Two binding cautions: the model's stated reasoning is **more output, not a causal account** — log it, never depend on it; and the **rule-32 corollary** — these artifacts are read by humans and by nothing that judges AI output | Every other component earns reproducibility by **re-running** (§13.5, NFR-1). An LLM cannot: it will not reproduce byte-identically, and temperature pinning is not a guarantee. So the seat cannot borrow the lab's reproducibility mechanism, and the only remaining route to auditability is that **the record itself is complete**. Every clause derives from that single substitution |
| **D105** | **`reproduce-day` replays the LOGGED AI decision; it never re-calls the model.** Amends §13.5 / FR-25. For any session containing an AI decision, reproduce-day resolves it from the persisted `ai_decisions` row exactly as the funnel does (§23.3 rule 1) and makes **zero** model API calls (`FX-ReproduceDay-AiSession`). NFR-1 determinism for AI-seated strategies reads **f(inputs, watermark, seeds, stored AI outputs)** | `FX-ReproduceDay` asserts **byte-identical** output. For an AI-seated session that is satisfiable **only** by replaying the logged decision — a live model call would return something different and fail the assertion on a **correctly-committed** day, turning the lab's strongest reproducibility claim into a test that cannot pass. Settling it now costs one sentence; discovering it in Phase 5 costs a redesign of either the seat or the NFR-1 proof |

**Distinctness note (recorded so the new fixture is not later merged into the old one).** `FX-PackNoLeak` is **not** covered by the existing `FX-PackWatermark`. Byte-identity does not imply leak-freedom: a pack that deterministically includes a post-as-of fact is byte-identical on every build and still leaks. `FX-PackWatermark` asserts stability; `FX-PackNoLeak` asserts admissibility. Both are required and neither implies the other. Similarly, `FX-ReproduceDay-AiSession` extends `FX-AiDecisionIsTheRow`'s zero-API-call guarantee from the re-run path to the FR-25 reproduce-day path — the two paths are different entry points and the existing fixture does not cover the second.

**Why record this now, with no code to check it against.** A specification written *after* its implementation tends to **describe** it; one written *before* can **contradict** it, and that contradiction is the detectable signal. Finding 285 — the gate and allocator consuming a raw active-return gap as "alpha" — was findable at all only because **D26 had said "never a raw return gap" long before the code that did**, so the divergence could be found by reading two documents against each other rather than by noticing a wrong number. §23.8 places the same bet deliberately: nothing in it describes anything today, and every clause is a claim Phase 5 can be checked against.

**Scope.** Design requirements only. No implementation, no work items opened, no phase schedule changed: the seats remain Phase 5 (researcher, packs, the two tables) and Phase 6 (contestant + twin) per BUILD_AND_PROMPTS §2. Documents touched: MASTER §2 (pass table + D104/D105 rows), MASTER §13.5 (the reproduce-day bullet), MASTER §23.7-23.8 (the new section + three fixture names), TEST_PLAN §6 (the three fixtures).

## v1.9.48 — the recompute harness, adopted (D106)

*Recorded 2026-07-26, during the 4.11 calibration run. Docs only. Adopted work, not a proposal — `PROGRESS.md`'s proposal register is for "anything the docs don't cover; approved ones move to MASTER §2", and this is committed, so it lands as a decision.*

| # | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| **D106** | **Monitor-rule and metric changes are scored by recomputing from stored rows rather than re-simulating (§25).** Covers anything **downstream of the simulation** (signal thresholds, sustain counts, the alpha definition, gate rules, verification metrics); covers **nothing that changes the simulation itself** (cost model, sizing, eligibility, plant construction). Two tiers inside "covered": **direct-read** (`overfitting_checks.value` + `contribution`) and **derived-input** (member window alphas re-derived from `control_equity`); the harness classifies by which PARAMETERS a rule specification touches and refuses or escalates on any it cannot classify. Config resolves **as-of** the recomputed session, never current. Gated on `FX-RecomputeParity`: exact reproduction of generation 1's `overfitting_status`, `go_live_log` Promoted and `go_live_log` WouldRevert rows under the CURRENT rules, deterministic on re-run, still passing after a new config version is inserted | Every monitor-rule change today costs a **multi-day replay** to evaluate — which is why findings 280 and 285 are bundled into one generation-2 run rather than scored independently. The justification is the **rate, not this one saving**: findings 280–283 are a single defect class and more instances are plausible. **Not free** — new code with its own bug surface, and a buggy harness gives wrong answers fast, which is worse than slow correct ones. Rests on one condition that is part of the decision: the allocator computes weights but **moves no money**, so no verdict feeds back into a later session. If the allocator ever moves capital, the premise dies and so does the harness |

### The verification, with citations (so nobody re-derives it)

- **The S6 input is persisted.** `MonitorSignals.S6` returns `SignalOutcome("S6", rollingAlphaT, …)` (`MonitorSignals.cs:110-124`); `OverfittingMonitor.cs:193` → `AddCheck` → `Value = sig.Value` (`:300-310`) → `overfitting_checks.value REAL`. **35,689 rows already carry a non-null value in generation 1.**
- **The promotion path is recomputable on the same terms.** `PromotionGate.Decide(gap, mde.MdeAnn, d.Length, gate.MinTrackDays)` (`EvaluationStep.cs:89`); `mde` from `MdeCalculator.Compute(d, maxHorizon, gate)` (`:87`); `d` the paired difference series (`:83-84`) from `equity_curve`; `gap` the raw annualised gap (`:88`); `maxHorizon` from `strat.HoldingHorizonDays`, a **persisted column** (`LedgerEntities.cs:33`), not `config_json`.
- **`gap` is finding 285's defect**, so reproducing promotions under the CURRENT rules means reproducing the raw-gap alpha faithfully — bug included — and the corrected run substitutes Jensen's alpha at the same call site. The harness must reproduce a known defect before it is trusted to evaluate the fix for it.
- **No feedback.** The allocator's sole call site is `DailyPipeline.cs:609` with no funnel/sizing/ledger consumer; plants are retire-exempt (`OverfittingMonitor.cs:213`, D100).
- **Config as-of.** `ConfigReadService.ResolveAsOf` (`ConfigReadService.cs:32`) — `MAX(version)` among rows with `changed_on <= t`.

### Three corrections recorded rather than smoothed over

1. **The benchmark is in `equity_curve`, not `control_equity`.** `buyhold:cw` is account 401. `control_equity` supplies the population members that S3's percentile and S6's band are computed from. Both stored, so the claim holds — but a reader following the wrong citation looks in the wrong table.
2. **`control_equity` holds 650 members, not 200.** A working draft said 200; that was `count(distinct member_index)`, and `member_index` restarts per population (PK `(population_id, member_index, as_of, run_kind)`). Measured: 4 populations — daily/banded/monthly at `Populations.Size`=200 each plus the cost-free daily twin at `CostFreeSize`=50 — and the row count confirms it independently (1,272,700 / 1,958 = 650). Recorded because a document whose stated purpose is that the citations are right should carry its own citation error.
3. **Recomputing S6 is pure over `value` AND `contribution`, not `value` alone.** `threshold_json` for S6 carries only `{window_days, negative_alpha_t}`, not the band edges; `contribution` distinguishes `inband`/`elevated_inband`, so `insideCentralBand` is recoverable. A band-*definition* change additionally needs member window alphas re-derived from `control_equity`. This is why the covered side is split into two tiers.

### One known limit

**`GateOptions` is not as-of resolvable.** It is bound from appsettings at composition (`PipelineComposition.cs:56`, `SectionName = "Gate"`), so `MinTrackDays` and the MDE parameters are not versioned config rows and are not stored per run. Reproducing generation 1's promotions rests on the `Gate` appsettings block being unchanged since — an assumption the harness cannot verify from the store. Same class as finding 282's call-site literals: a calibration-relevant parameter that is not a versioned row. Recorded as a limit; making `Gate.*` as-of resolvable is **not** proposed here.

### Why the as-of requirement is structural, not defensive

The harness exists to evaluate a **new** threshold, and recording a candidate threshold **INSERTS a new config version** (rule 24 / finding 108), so by the time the harness is used current config already differs from what generation 1 ran under. A harness reading current config would fail `FX-RecomputeParity` — and under §25.3's own routing rule that failure is diagnosed as *input impurity*, sending an investigation into the store when the defect is in the reader. Same class as D104's leakage invariant: an input that must be read as-of, not as-now. The replay already resolves config this way [D96].

### Two open questions (recorded, deliberately not decided)

- **(a) Where it writes.** Recomputed rows must never overwrite generation 1's. Candidates: a distinct vintage, a distinct `run_kind`, or report-only output. Settled when built.
- **(b) Whether a recomputed number counts as sign-off evidence.** The harness would recompute the C-1 curve and the status-derived KPIs rather than re-simulate them. Whether that satisfies 4.11's DoD is a judgement to make deliberately — and it decides whether generation 2 is **avoidable or merely cheaper**.

### Ordering

**harness → generation 2 → Phase 6.** D106's trigger is **before generation 2**. Generation 2's own trigger is unchanged: **before Phase 6 registers real strategies** (findings 280 and 285). Recorded explicitly so the ordering is read rather than inferred.

### Prose convention (new in this pass)

The rule goes in the sentence and the decision number in a trailing bracket, so the text reads correctly with the bracket deleted — *"Monitor-rule and metric changes are scored by recomputing from stored rows rather than re-simulating [D106]"*, never *"D106 says that…"*. No prior `[D###]` instances existed in the corpus; this establishes it.

**Also this pass, mechanically:** `.gitattributes` (`* text=auto eol=lf`) and the one-time normalization of `MASTER_DESIGN_v1.9.md` from CRLF to LF, committed separately so the D106 diff stays pure content. Checked first, since `ci.ps1` cannot run while the calibration holds the store: all 8 tracked data files under `tests/` are already LF in HEAD, so `eol=lf` is a no-op for them and cannot break a byte-compared fixture.

## v1.9.49 — the 4.11 run completes: generation 1 recorded, the freeze blocked as predicted (findings 288-289; D103 re-scoped)

*Recorded 2026-07-31. Docs + artefact hygiene only: the report and the completed store are protected, the run is recorded, D103 is re-scoped. The gating change, the resume, the freeze, the D106 harness, generation 2, and findings 280/285 all keep their existing triggers — none is executed here.*

**The run.** `replay-calibrate` completed **5031/5031 sessions** (2006-01-03 … 2025-12-31) at 2026-07-31 08:35Z: **Release end to end** (the resume preserved `-c Release` and the full original argument set — verified against the live process's command line, not assumed), frozen watermark `2026-07-24T22:00:00Z`, one vintage, **0 plants retired across all 20 years** — D100 held at full scale, through the GFC and the COVID break, so every plant's S3 trajectory is full-length. **31,327 `WouldRevert` rows** carry the would-be verdicts (findings 113/114 stay computable). The report is archived and committed at `docs/calibration/sp500/2026-07-31-calibration.md` (build stamp **Release**, finding 278; repo-root-anchored tracked location, finding 276), and the completed store is snapshotted (`snapshots/alphalab-20260731-075529.db`, 3.88 GB, size-verified) — a 20-year generation that cannot be cheaply regenerated is now protected before anything else touches the machine.

**The freeze was blocked — Phase 4 is NOT signed off.** The report's own header states it: *"Config rows frozen this run: (none — report-only, or a verification failure blocked the freeze)"*. Two checks read Fail, `NoFailures` was false, and per finding 251's gate **zero config rows were written** — no D56 curves installed, no calibrated thresholds frozen. The first Fail is exactly what finding 279 predicted (`joint_false_alarm`; D102 is docs-adopted but deliberately not yet implemented in code). The second is `would_be_edge_survival_5y` — which **is** in the post-D102 gating set (finding 281's audit, class (a)) — so, stated plainly: **implementing D102 alone would not unblock the freeze.** That confrontation belongs to the gating audit; this pass records it and does not pre-empt it.

**The verification table, all twelve outcomes** (from the archived report):

| Check | Outcome | Detail |
|---|---|---|

(The report's inline "recalibrates S6's patience" pointer is the stale finding-270 loop already flagged in finding 281) |

Also archived at full scale: the **plant-sensitivity check** — max realistic-vs-naive divergence at t ≥ 126d is **18.0 percentile points** vs the 10-point threshold, so the naive constant-drift comparator would have mis-calibrated the monitor (D64's prohibition confirmed by measurement, not assumption); and the **C-2 sampling band** (±3.08 members at M=200).

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 288 | **The C-1 detection-power ladder is the checkpoint's PRIMARY finding (RUNBOOK §8 step 6) — the lab's detection threshold, measured for the first time.** Per-rung promotions over the full 20-year window, 50 seeds each: **daily@2%: 0/50 — exactly as pre-registered** (the D101 ladder retargeted the primary daily→monthly because ~22 %/yr cost drag puts a 2 %/yr daily overlay below the noise floor; the run **confirms the pre-registration** rather than discovering the fact). **Monthly@2%: 1/50 · @4%: 5/50 · @8%: 26/50 · @16%: 43/50** (asymptotic P(promoted) 0.02 / 0.10 / 0.52 / 0.86; median sessions to promotion 105 / 105 / 315 / 861). What it means, stated as the primary result and not as a footnote to the gate colours: **this is the smallest edge the machinery can actually see, end-to-end under costs — a 4 %/yr monthly-cadence edge is found 10 % of the time** over twenty years; 8 %/yr about half the time; 16 %/yr, 86 % of the time. The FR-40 detectability-at-admission gate's empirical floor (D89) now has its full-scale curve | Recorded as the checkpoint's headline number. **Caveat carried with the number, not behind it (finding 285(d)):** promotions are gate-derived and the gate consumed the raw-gap alpha, so the per-rung ABSOLUTE levels are generation-1 numbers contingent on that defect; the SHAPE (detection rising in plant strength) survives; generation 2 re-measures | the archived report's C-1 section + `edge_plant_detected` Detail; RUNBOOK §8 step 6; D89/FR-40; D101 (ladder + rule-selected primary); finding 285(d) |
| 289 | **The finding-281/282 full-scale projection for the EVER-form `noedge_curve_breach_validate` was wrong — projected 25-35 %, measured 6 % (3/50), Pass — and the reconciliation is the useful part.** **Window-length monotonicity is real** (an EVER predicate over 23× more start positions cannot get easier as the window grows; that half of the reasoning stands). **But curve quality dominated it:** the smoke's 14 % (7/50) was measured against P_noise built from the smoke's own ~1-year window's learn portion; the full run built P_noise from the **fourteen-year** learn segment (3,523 sessions, M=200 per evaluation index), and a far better-estimated 5th-percentile anchor collapses the per-window breach hazard by more than the 3→69 start-position inflation raises it. The finding-282 extrapolation (8 % @5 evals → 22 % @31, "≈25-35 % at ~71") was measured against partial-scale curves and carried the same omission. **Neither direction of the earlier reasoning — not the original 97 %, not the corrected 25-35 % — modelled the curve-estimation term**: how the anchor's own quality scales with learn length. Both extrapolated the window count while holding the curves fixed; the curves were the faster-moving variable | Supersession pointers added at the two places the 25-35 % figure appears (finding 281's row; the PROGRESS D103-evidence bullet). Corollary recorded, not spun: the 6 % is generation-1 evidence the two-pass premise held — full-length trajectories → a well-estimated null → an honest out-of-sample false-alarm rate — and it removes D103's live premise (re-scope below) | the archived report (`noedge_curve_breach_validate` 3/50); findings 281-282 (the superseded projection); D103 (re-scoped below) |

### D103 re-scoped — from "restate a broken metric" to "window-monotonicity is a latent hazard" (finding-289 consequence; D103 remains reserved and untaken)

D103's premise was that the EVER form would Fail at full scale and block the freeze, so the metric would need restating before the resume. **That premise is gone: measured 6 % vs the 0.10 bound.** Finding 282's option-(i) restatement is therefore **no longer fixing a failure** — executing it now would be a change justified by nothing observable. What remains true, and is the re-scoped D103:

- **The hazard is latent, not resolved.** `SustainsAcross` is still an EVER predicate, monotone non-decreasing in validate-window length against a fixed fraction bound; this run's Pass is a fact about THESE curves on THIS window, not about the metric's specification. The D103 Q1 reading stands unchanged: `CONFIG_REFERENCE` declares a *rate*, the implementation computes a *lifetime probability* — a documented contract the implementation still contradicts, currently without consequence.
- **Stated trigger — when it bites:** (a) any run whose validate window differs materially from this generation's (~71 evaluations / 2 calendar blocks) — a different `--learn-through`, a longer window, or **another arena's calibration** (the sp400 arena is now on the map — see the D87 statements in PROGRESS) — because the number then stops being comparable across runs while the bound stays fixed; or (b) **a reading at or above 8 %, or 4 or more of 50 plants breaching** — an ABSOLUTE arm, deliberately not phrased as proximity to the bound. Reason recorded with it: this run read **3/50** against a bound of **5/50**, roughly one standard deviation apart, so a trigger phrased as "within binomial noise of the bound" was arguably **already satisfied by the very reading that prompted writing it** — and a trigger that is already true is not a trigger. On either trigger D103 executes: restate to a well-posed form (finding 282's per-opportunity rate, or Amendment 1's named-window block form), chosen then, on the rule text, per amendment C1.
- **The 283(a) sweep is NOT this item's remediation.** Its pre-registration (protocol + Amendments 1-2 + confirmations) remains **intact and unspent**, owned by the finding-280 monitor work (the sustain rule's ρ₁ = 0.737 problem), which is unaffected by this re-scope. The "D103 × 283(a) coupling" note bound the OLD D103's (α) branch to the sweep's output; with D103 re-scoped to a latent hazard, nothing here waits on the sweep and the sweep waits on nothing here.

### The freeze caveat, recorded in advance of the freeze (finding 285 §4 — attached to this run's record)

`promotions_le_chance` (Pass, 0/50) and `edge_plant_detected` (Pass, 43/50) were computed from promotions produced by the **raw-gap alpha** (finding 285). The survival reasoning, written as an argument rather than assumed: every D64 plant is an overlay on a population member and is therefore **beta-matched to its own base**; the raw gap and Jensen's alpha differ by a term in (β−1) against the benchmark's excess return, and because every plant shares the same base construction **that term is common to the edge and no-edge cohorts alike** — it shifts the absolute level of both cohorts and preserves the *separation between them*, which is exactly the quantity these two checks measure. So the verdicts are expected to hold; **generation 2 confirms it — this generation does not.** **This caveat attaches to the freeze record when the freeze happens:** whichever pass eventually writes frozen config rows must carry it beside the two check verdicts it qualifies.
