## v1.9.50 — the category decision: pass-1-verdict metrics are reported-only (D107, supersedes D102)

*Recorded 2026-07-31 on `feat/freeze-gate-category` off the merged v1.9.49 main. This section is the decision (Phase B, docs); the implementation (Phase C), the freeze (Phase D) and the sign-off (Phase E) follow in the same PR as separate commits.*

**The Phase-A gate-wiring audit this rests on (read-only, 2026-07-31; established from code, not memory):**

1. **Wiring:** `NoFailures` = `Checks.All(c => c.Outcome != CheckOutcome.Fail)` (`ReplayVerification.cs:51`); `willFreeze = !reportOnly && verification.NoFailures` (`CalibrationOrchestrator.cs:157`); a Fail also exits 1 **before** any config write (`:185-190`). The freeze itself is one transaction of five append-only keys (`PNoiseCurve`, `PEdgeCurve`, `DetectionPower`, `S6AutoRetireEvals` first-freeze-only, `ReportRef`). **No per-check exemption mechanism exists anywhere in the code — `NoFailures` treats all twelve checks identically.** D102 was confirmed unimplemented: `joint_false_alarm`'s Fail flowed into the gate like any other.
2. **The full data-source map** of the twelve checks: verification-time **verdict readers** (`SuspectEver` over `overfitting_status`; `WouldRevert` over `go_live_log`); **gate-promotion readers** (`PromotedAmong` — the finding-285 raw-gap channel); **calibrated-analogue/structural readers** (S3 percentile values vs learn-built curves; existence checks); plus the allocator's **generation-time** verdict influence (`AllocationStep.cs:37-61`: the suspect-decay clamp and `EffectiveStatus` filtering consumed flat-anchor Suspects while the sessions were being written).
3. **The resume path:** committed sessions are skipped (`ReplayRunner.cs:139-162`), so unblocking costs a **re-invocation** — curve build + verification + freeze, minutes not days; the watermark pin file exists and matches the generation (`2026-07-24T22:00:00Z`).

### D107 — the membership test, stated before the membership

A check belongs to the reported-only category by **what it ASSERTS, never what data it READS** — the same discriminator that keeps `FX-PackNoLeak` distinct from `FX-PackWatermark` (two claims over one artifact: admissibility vs stability). A check asserting a property of the **pass-1 flat-anchor monitor** asserts a property of an instrument that is uncalibrated **by construction** during the very run that judges it (D100; amendment C1) — its verdict is not evidence about the curves being frozen. A check that merely reads pass-1 rows while asserting something else stays gating.

### The category — THREE members, reported-only, never freeze-gating [D107]

| Member | Asserts | This run |
|---|---|---|
| `joint_false_alarm` | a property of the pass-1 monitor (its ever-Suspect rate on no-edge plants) | Fail 50/50 |
| `would_be_edge_survival_5y` | a property of the pass-1 monitor (its would-kill rate on small real edges) | Fail 28 % |
| `anti_detection_speed` | how fast the pass-1 monitor catches anti plants, under flat anchors | **Pass** |

**Why the third member matters, recorded:** `anti_detection_speed` PASSED this run. Including it is what shows the category was drawn on principle rather than around the failures — a category containing only the two failing checks is indistinguishable from removing what failed; one that also contains a passing member cannot be read that way.

### Two verdict-touching checks STAY in the gating set, reasons recorded

- **`edge_retires_logged`** — reads pass-1 `WouldRevert` rows but asserts **LOG INTEGRITY**: every would-be retire carries its triggering signal. That property holds regardless of calibration state, so a Fail means a recording regression. The data source is incidental to the claim.
- **`allocator_value_add`** — its subject is the **ALLOCATOR**, not the monitor; verdicts were an input, not the thing asserted. **Direction caveat recorded with it:** the measured 24.17 %/yr against an MDE of 11.78 % was produced while the monitor was flagging 43/50 no-edge plants — this reading is a **LOWER BOUND under adverse inputs**, and calibrated inputs should not worsen it.

### Justification, relationship to D102, and the guard

**Justification for the category:** the pre-registered amendment-C1 caveat (pass-1 verdicts are non-comparable and not independent validation), plus the fact that each failing member has a **calibrated analogue which PASSED** — `curve_based_edge_survival` **98/100** and `noedge_curve_breach_validate` **6 %**. **This is the third check to leave the gate**, and that is stated rather than buried: **a category leaving is defensible where three individual removals would not be** — the category has a written membership test, a passing member, and a guard; three case-by-case removals would be the finding-279/281 pattern (discovering the members one failure at a time) relocated into the gating set itself.

**Relationship to D102 — SUPERSEDES, per the corpus convention** (D5/D19/D46: the superseded row is annotated in place, the new number carries the rule): D102 was adopted docs-only and never reached code; D107 subsumes its single-member removal into the category rule **before implementation**, so D102 will never be implemented as itself. D102's justification (the finding-251 ∧ amendment-C1 deadlock) carries forward intact as the category's founding case.

**The mechanism reality [from the audit, recorded in the decision]:** because no per-check exemption exists, **Phase C CREATES the mechanism** — a reported-only marking on the check record, consumed by `NoFailures` — rather than flipping a flag. Anyone expecting a one-line change is reading D102's "removed from the gating set" as if the set were data; it is code, and the set's members are decided at each check's construction site.

**The guard [D107]:** every check flagged reported-only **cites its decision number in code**, and a test pins the **exact** reported-only set — no check can leave the gating set without a recorded decision behind it, and a new member reddens the test until the addition is deliberate.

**What Phase C must add (recorded so it is not discovered later):** `CalibrationReportInputs` carries a single `BuildConfiguration` and no code-vintage field — nothing records that this generation's **sessions** came from the pre-change build while its **verification and freeze** come from the post-change build. Phase C adds the generation-provenance line (sessions committed this invocation vs pre-existing, plus the verifying build); the exact commit hashes land in the sign-off entries. The generation is legitimately assembled from two builds — the replay completed before the change, and the change is verification-stage only (zero residue, corrected 283(b) cost map) — but that provenance is **written down rather than inferred later**.

**Explicitly out of scope: S6 patience recalibration.** A replay-path change costing a fresh multi-day run, it would invalidate the very records this pass uses, and the flat-anchor path stops being used the moment these curves freeze. Finding 280 keeps its existing trigger, bundled with finding 285 into generation 2.

**Two corrections from the audit (applied in this pass):**

- **(a)** the finding-285-cont survives/does-not table said `days_to_indistinguishability` reads persisted monitor status; **the code reads promotions** (`PromotedAmong`, `ReplayVerification.cs:146`). Its does-not-survive conclusion stands via the raw-gap promotion channel (finding 285), but the stated mechanism was wrong — corrected in place, because a wrong mechanism in a table people consult is worse than a wrong conclusion they would check.
## v1.9.52 — the Phase-4.5 build opens: D108 (trend-flag inference) + finding 294 (the pool defect)

*Recorded 2026-07-31 on `feat/phase4.5-signal-library` off the merged v1.9.51 main (`9e2ab0c`). The plan-approval pass: the two questions that would otherwise have been decided **inside** checkpoint 4.5.2 are settled first, in the open. Register verified before writing: next-free finding **294**, next-free decision **D108** (D103 remains reserved-and-unassigned), next-free interface rule **UX-16** (reserved by name in v1.9.51; its rule text is still unwritten and lands at 4.5.4).*

### D108 — the trend flag's window and reference distribution

Adopted: **a uniform 5-year window for BOTH horizons, against a t reference whose df derive from the effective independent sample.** Finding 290 had left this stated-but-undecided as a §24.2 spec change; it falls due the moment 4.5.2 implements the flag, so it is taken now and carries a decision number rather than folding into the checkpoint.

**Three defects resolved together — finding 290 named only the first:**

| | 1y window, k=63 | 5y window, k=63 |
|---|---|---|
| effective independent observations | **~4** | ~20 |
| reference distribution | normal is wrong: t(df≈3)=**3.182** vs z=1.96; a nominal 5 % test has actual size ≈ **15 %** | t(df≈19)=2.093 vs z=1.96 (+7 %) |
| **the NW estimator's own reliability** (`lag/T`) | 63/252 = **0.25** — far outside where NW is reliable (standard bandwidth at T=252 is ~4–5 lags) | 63/1260 = **0.05** |

The third is the one a reference change alone cannot fix: correcting z→t would put a correct critical value on an **unreliable standard error**. Blind pinning stays legitimate throughout *because* the thresholds are significance units — a significance level can be chosen without seeing data; an IC magnitude cannot.

**The decisive reason: the trend arm fits a slope, so it needs more data than the level arm, and the window must satisfy the harder arm.** And responsiveness is not the cost it appears — the trend arm fires *within* the window, so window length does not set detection lag the way it would for a level-only test: a downward slope inside a 5-year window is detectable while the 5-year mean is still positive.

**Rejected on the record:** keeping 1y with a t reference (defect 3 survives); per-horizon windows (tailored, but the two flags become claims about different spans of history); dropping the significance claim (self-defeating — it forces an IC-magnitude cutoff, which cannot be blind-pinned, reintroducing exactly the curve-fitting the pin-before-any-grade-row rule exists to prevent).

**Two consequences that are part of the decision, not follow-on work:**

1. **The pinned constant becomes α, not a critical value.** The critical value is `t_{1−α/2, df}` computed at read time, because df depends on the effective sample. So `SignalLibrary.TrendDecayZ`/`TrendGoneZ` are renamed **`TrendDecayAlpha`/`TrendGoneAlpha`** — the `*Z` names described a normal reference that no longer exists — and they become **versioned config ROWS rather than appsettings values**, because finding 292 requires the read-model to resolve them through `ResolveAsOf` and an appsettings value is not as-of resolvable (the known `GateOptions` limit, D106).
2. **Effective n is promoted from a printed caveat to a load-bearing INPUT.** It sets df and therefore the critical value. A quantity that determines a verdict earns its own derivation fixture rather than a display test, and the derivation must be exact — the two arms differ because they fit different things: **level `df = n_eff − 1`, trend `df = n_eff − 2`**. At 5y/k=63 that is t = 2.093 and 2.101. It is still printed beside the flag (the D107 power-limitation discipline), but its status is input first.

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 294 | **§24.2 left the IC pool ambiguous by citing two paths in one sentence — and the ambiguity had a determinable answer, not a preference.** The sentence names the **Stage-1 eligible pool** (as-of membership ∩ priced-at-asOf, `Eligibility.cs:69-100`) and, in the same breath, *"the adjusted series the control populations compound on"* — whose path (`PopulationMarket.cs:31-37`) resolves membership **rawly, with no priced filter**. Read together the two read as two competing pool definitions, which is exactly how a from-scratch reading of the spec took them. Left open it would have been settled by whichever call the IC engine happened to make, changing `n` on every row and therefore every rank-IC | **Settled on correctness, not preference: the pool is the SCORABLE set.** An unpriced name yields no score and therefore cannot enter a ranking, so the priced filter is **implied by the ranking operation** rather than chosen beside it. The same rule narrows **per signal** — a scorer without sufficient history emits nothing for that name (`lowvol:L252` needs 252 sessions, `rev:L21` needs 21), the existing "absence is the honest answer" idiom — and `signal_ic.n` records the result per (signal, day, horizon), which the schema already accommodates. §24.2 is corrected to state which clause governs the **pool** and which governs the **return series**. **Burden of proof placed where it belongs:** any pool other than the scorable one now needs its own justification; the scorable one needs none | `MASTER §24.2`; `Eligibility.cs:69-100`; `PopulationMarket.cs:31-37`; `signal_ic.n`; `FX-SignalIcPit` |

---

- **(b)** `tools/resume-calibration.ps1`'s guard-2 message told the operator any rebuild "needs a `--reset` re-run" — over-broad against the corrected 283(b) cost map (verification-stage changes have **zero residue**), and it pointed at the one command that destroys 5,031 committed sessions. Fixed in the same commit: the message now distinguishes replay-path changes (fresh `--reset` generation, deliberately) from verification-stage changes (commit and resume — never `--reset`).

## v1.9.51 — the Phase-4.5 reconciliation: the prompt made buildable (findings 290-292)

*Recorded 2026-07-31 on `docs/phase4.5-reconciliation` off the merged v1.9.50 main (`e4c4ab1`). **Docs only — no `src/` change, and 4.5.1 is NOT started.** This pass makes the 4.5 prompt buildable; the build is a separate pass. Register verified before writing: main carries PR #20 and PROGRESS reads Phase 4 SIGNED OFF; next-free finding **290**, next-free decision **D108** (D103 remains reserved-and-unassigned and must not be reused), next-free interface rule **UX-16**.*

### 1. The recurrence guard applied: does any decision from D92 onward change an UNSPENT prompt?

The 4.5 prompt's newest citation is **D91**, and **D92–D107** have landed since; the last BUILD/MASTER sync was 2026-07-26, before D107 existed. Phase 4.5 is the next unspent prompt, so each decision was **read** and classified rather than assumed. Fifteen decisions exist in the range (D103 is reserved-unassigned): **four touch 4.5, eleven do not.**

| # | Subject | Touches 4.5? | Established by reading |
|---|---|---|---|
| D92 | catch-up records the true `observed_at` | **no** | inherited infrastructure: the IC engine reads bars at a watermark and benefits from honest stamps, but nothing in the 4.5 build changes |
| D93 | regime rows are run-kind-scoped | **no** | 4.5 writes no regime rows. **And the question it prompts is answered:** `signal_ic` correctly carries **no `run_kind`** — it grades *market history*, of which there is exactly one, not a run's outcomes. D93's hazard (a replay recompute overwriting a forward label) has no analogue here |
| D94 | `corporate_actions.processed_on` dropped | **no** | schema cleanup upstream of the adjusted series 4.5 consumes; no build consequence |
| D95 | the replay watermark contract, one generation per arena | **no** | **but the boundary is now stated in the prompt:** the IC backfill is *not* a replay generation — it writes no `run_kind='replay'` rows and creates no generation. Its determinism is the same *philosophy* (recompute from versioned bars at a watermark, §24.4), never the same mechanism |
| **D96** | versioned config resolves **as-of** the watermark | **YES** | the mechanism finding 292 needs: `SignalLibrary.*` reads must be as-of resolvable (`ResolveAsOf`) when serving a watermark-pinned consumer, `ResolveCurrent` for the live panel. Recorded in the prompt so the read-model is *built* with the seam rather than retrofitted |

| D99 | `422 detectability_refused` | **no** | a signal registration is **not a candidate**: it creates no `strategies` row, no `trials_registry` row, and never reaches `CandidateFactory`. §24.3's "no sweeping to maximize IC" is the boundary that keeps it so — a sweep *would* be candidate selection and would pay the trials tax |

### 2. Preconditions that have CLEARED — restated so no builder re-checks a met condition

- **4.5.3's gate — "runs only after the D70 backfill" — is SATISFIED.** The historical backfill completed 2026-07-23 and the full calibration consumed it end to end (5031 sessions). The gate's rationale (grading an sp100-only store would be redone) no longer applies.
- **The corpus ordering condition — "4.5 builds after Phase 4" — is MET** as of the PR #20 merge (`e4c4ab1`).
- **A third, not previously connected: D65's deferral has EXPIRED.** D65 sanctioned API-only (Scalar) operation *"until Phase-4 sign-off"* — a time-boxed cover that has now run out. Consequence for the panel-timing choice below: **"defer the panel" can no longer cite D65.** Deferring is still available, but it now needs its own stated justification rather than an expired one.

### 3. Findings

| # | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---------|-----------|-------|
| 290 | **The 126-day horizon is statistically incompatible with the trend flag's own window — and the same arithmetic makes the flag thin at 63.** §24.2 defines BOTH flag arms on the **1-year** window (*"decaying = the 1y trend significantly negative … gone = the 1y mean not significantly above zero"*) with **Newey-West lag = horizon**. A 1y window is ~252 trading days. At **k=126** the NW lag is **half the sample** and the window holds **~2 non-overlapping observations** — a significance test on 2 effective points is noise wearing a verdict, and no amount of backfill fixes it (the window length is fixed at 1y by definition, not by data availability). The same arithmetic, applied to the shipped horizons: **k=63 ⇒ ~4 effective observations**, **k=21 ⇒ ~12**. So the 1y flag is *already* thin at 63 — which the corpus had not stated | **P15's "horizon 126" is CLOSED — REJECTED for v1**, on the statistic rather than on cost (the ~35k extra rows are trivial; that was never the constraint). `SignalLibrary.HorizonsDays` stays `[21, 63]`. **Consequential requirement, added to checkpoint 4.5.2:** the read-model must carry the **effective independent sample** (window ÷ horizon) beside each trend flag, so a 1y/63 flag is visibly a ~4-observation claim — the same discipline as the D107 power limitation that prints per-block fractions beside a pooled number. **Left to the build, stated not decided:** whether the flag's primary window should move 1y → 5y (~20 effective obs at k=63). That is a §24.2 spec change and is not made unilaterally here | `MASTER §24.2` (the flag definition + the effective-n requirement); `CONFIG_REFERENCE` `SignalLibrary.HorizonsDays` (the "126 open" note closed); `BUILD_AND_PROMPTS` 4.5.2; PROGRESS P15 |
| 291 | **The forward sp100 → sp500 widen must land BEFORE go-live, and nothing recorded said so.** Forward IC grading begins at go-live; a universe change *after* it would put a **break in the forward IC series** — the same confound that rules out an in-place widen for the cohort curve (the D87 statements, v1.9.49). Before this pass the widen was recorded only as "a separate deliberate post-sign-off action" (RUNBOOK §8 step 9) with **no ordering relative to go-live**, so the two could have been scheduled in ignorance of each other | **The constraint is recorded in BOTH places** — the 4.5 prompt and the widen item itself — so neither can be scheduled without seeing it. **Scope, stated precisely: the BACKFILL is unaffected.** It grades historical **S&P 500** as-of membership, which D70 already satisfied; only the *forward* series is at risk. So this constrains go-live ordering, never 4.5.3 | `BUILD_AND_PROMPTS` Phase 4.5; `RUNBOOK §8` step 9 (the widen item); rule 22 / D70 / D87 |
| 293 | **P15's panel timing was still open going into 4.5.1, and its old justification had expired.** D65 sanctioned API-only (Scalar) operation *"until Phase-4 sign-off"* — that cover ran out at the v1.9.50 merge, so "defer the panel" could no longer cite it and the item could not be left to surface mid-build | **CLOSED — DEFERRED, on a justification that is not D65: the shell the panel belongs in does not exist yet.** `AlphaLab.Web` has no Strategies screen (itself deferred, due before Phase-7 exit), so shipping a *signal* panel first would mean inventing the application's shell, navigation and layout conventions inside a descriptive-only side instrument rather than in the primary screen that owns them. **Three things make the deferral honest rather than a re-run of the expired cover.** (a) **The honesty-carrying half does NOT defer:** the FR-46 read-model + `/api/v1` route ship in-phase, and per rule 18 / FR-33 every honesty guarantee is a read-model field with read-model unit tests — deferring the *rendering* defers no guarantee. (b) **The visual contract does not defer either:** `UX-16`, the `UX_DESIGN_SYSTEM_v1.9.md` component entry, and the mockup land in **4.5.4 as ONE unit**, so the panel is fully specified and reviewable before any renderer exists — a panel without its rule would be an interface with no honesty spec behind it, and that is what this ordering prevents. (c) **The deferral is bounded by an existing dated obligation** — the UI workstream, due **before Phase-7 exit**, which will build Strategies + allocation + cohort + signal panels against one set of shell conventions. **This is exactly the UX-15 / `CohortCurvePanel` precedent (D88, FR-39):** rule + design-system entry + mockup + read-model + route landed at checkpoint 3.12; the rendered panel went to the UI workstream. The instrument is not stranded meanwhile — the route serves it via Scalar, and the Phase-5 digest consumes it programmatically, not visually | `BUILD_AND_PROMPTS` 4.5.4 + FR-46; `MASTER §24.7`; PROGRESS P15 + the Phase-4.5 gate box + the GUI workstream item |
| 292 | **The Phase-5 digest requires the 4.5 read-model to be as-of resolvable, and the 4.5 spec does not say so.** §24.6 wires one digest line per signal (1y rank-IC, 5y rank-IC, trend flag) into the **context packs**. D104 — landed *after* D91 — makes every pack field subject to `FX-PackNoLeak`: **per-field**, no `observed_at` later than the simulated as-of, and **closure**, only whitelisted fields. A digest computed from a rolling window ending *now* would leak into a pack whose as-of is earlier, and a read-model built with no as-of parameter cannot serve one without a **4.5 code change at Phase 5** | **Recorded as a 4.5 build-shape requirement, not a Phase-5 wiring task:** the FR-46 read-model **accepts an as-of** (resolving both the `signal_ic` rows and the `SignalLibrary.*` z values through D96's `ResolveAsOf`), so Phase 5 wires the digest without reopening 4.5. The digest field also joins the pack whitelist when Phase 5 builds it — noted there, not built here. The live panel keeps `ResolveCurrent`: a panel answers "is this signal decaying **now**" | `BUILD_AND_PROMPTS` 4.5.4; `MASTER §24.6`; D104 (`FX-PackNoLeak`), D96 (`ResolveAsOf`) |

### 4. What does NOT carry into 4.5 — confirmed by reading, not assumed

The claim under test was that the signal library, being descriptive-only, is untouched by findings 280/285 and the D107 gating category. **All three confirmed clean**, with one live consequence found:

- **Finding 280 (the S6 flat-anchor misfire) — does not touch.** The library consumes no monitor signal, status, or verdict; its inputs are bars and as-of membership.
- **Finding 285 (the raw-gap alpha) — does not touch, WITH a consequence worth stating.** The defect lives in `EvaluationStep`'s gate/allocator/screen path. But `resmom:L252` is *"market-beta-adjusted trailing return vs the GSPC proxy"* — the library performs its **own** beta adjustment inside the scorer. That is the correct place for it, and the note the build needs is the negative one: **the resmom scorer must not be wired through the `EvaluationStep` path finding 285 indicts.** Since Phase 6's `FX-SignalParity` makes the strategy path wrap this same scorer, a beta adjustment correct here is correct in both.
- **D107 (the reported-only category) — does not touch.** 4.5 has no gating checks; nothing here feeds `NoFailures`.

### 5. The known coverage tail, reasoned for this NEW consumer (P16/P17)

The IC backfill reads the same store carrying the finding-284 coverage tail. Recorded here so the next reader does not re-derive it:

- **The 93 zero-coverage securities are INERT in a cross-sectional ranking.** They have no bars inside their own membership span, so they produce no score and enter no ranking — a Spearman rank-IC over the names that *do* have data is simply computed on a smaller cross-section that day. Absence, not contamination.
- **The ~16 partial-coverage names carry the same small wrong-price contamination already judged immaterial for the calibration** (finding 284: absence not mispricing, affecting plants and populations symmetrically). For rank-IC the exposure is milder still: a rank correlation is **ordinal**, so a wrong price moves a name's rank rather than scaling a return — and the finding-268 read-side guard already neutralises physically-impossible single-session moves at the point the score would consume them.
- **The direction of the residual, stated rather than hand-waved:** the tail is systematically composed of names that exited by acquisition or failure, so the graded cross-section is missing its casualties — the same survivor-bias shape routed to **P18** for the calibrated-null-vs-operating-null question. It is not re-opened here; it is recorded that the signal library inherits it, and that a rank-IC computed on survivors is a *slightly optimistic* instrument for the same reason the replay's null is.

### 6. The pinning discipline, moved INTO the prompt (a builder works from the prompt)

`TrendDecayZ` and `TrendGoneZ` are named `SignalLibrary.*` keys **holding `null`** in `CONFIG_REFERENCE` today, and they are pinned at **checkpoint 4.5.2 — before 4.5.3 writes a single grade row.** MASTER §24.2 stated this; the prompt did not name the keys or the reason, so it does now: **pinning them after a 20-year backfill exists would be choosing thresholds by looking at the answer** — the same defect the D101 ladder pre-registration and the 283(a) protocol exist to prevent, and the same act as the D98 freeze (written once, append-only-versioned, never re-stamped). **Guard against a plausible misreading of D106:** because a z change is later scorable by recompute over stored `signal_ic` rows, it is *cheap* — and cheapness is **not** a licence to defer the pinning. The pinning is an epistemic constraint, not a compute-cost one.

### 7. The C-1 result as interpretation context — beside the 4.5 output, never merged into it

The Phase-4 ladder measured the lab's detection threshold for the first time: **monthly 2/4/8/16 %/yr → 1/5/26/43 of 50 promoted**, i.e. **a 4 %/yr edge is found 10 % of the time** over twenty years (finding 288). A reader of a signal's rank-IC needs that number *beside* it to judge whether a live-looking rule could ever be confirmed by the arena at all.

**They are different quantities and no arithmetic converts one into the other:** rank-IC is a cross-sectional predictive correlation of a *scoring rule*, carrying no costs, no sizing, no exits and no account; C-1 is the end-to-end probability that a *whole trading operation* clears the promotion gate under costs. A signal may grade well and still describe an edge the arena cannot confirm — that gap is the honest content of putting the two side by side, and the prompt states it as a reading instruction rather than as a conversion.
